> ## The frame, in one line
>
> **Features are PLUGINS. The actor is the HUB.** Actor hub is **feature #1 of roughly a thousand**. Its job
> is to make adding feature #2 cheap — **not to pre-empt it**. A seam to an unbuilt feature is a **trigger**,
> never a gap, and never something to design.
>
> ## The scope test, which decides every judgement call
>
> **身外之物** — strip the actor naked and move them to another world; what travels? Body, pools, statuses,
> lifespan, memories travel. Money, items, rank, reputation, position stay. **If a datum would not travel, it
> does not belong in the actor.** It does **not** rank identity (circular) or record-lifecycle (a property of
> the row).
>

> ## The verifier — an INDEPENDENT agent, and it is not optional
>
> **Every slice ends with a cold-start adversarial agent that did not write the code.** Its isolation is the
> product: it cannot inherit the author's blind spot. Measured this round — **112 findings across five
> lenses, of which self-review at authorship found ZERO.**
>
> **Its mandate:** refute, not confirm · **default REFUTED when uncertain** · every finding carries
> `file:line` · re-measure every number rather than accepting it · **and a mandatory *"claims I tried to
> break and could not"* section with a minimum count, because a report that finds nothing is
> indistinguishable from a report that looked at nothing.**

> gap"* — or it will return the rest of the game as debt (`D-291`).
>
> **Re-verify with a FRESH agent.** Never re-use one that has seen the fix; it has seen the reasoning behind
> it and has lost the isolation that was the whole point. **Terminate on 2 consecutive clean rounds, never
> one.**
>
> ## The taxonomy — use it, and never say "blocked" loosely
>
> **BLOCKED** = external to this repo and unwritable here. **This round found ZERO.** Everything else is
> **WRONG** (correct it) · **UNDECIDED** (decide it) · **UNWRITTEN** (build it) · **PREMATURE** (a real
> question needing a feature that does not exist — give it a named trigger, do not answer it).

# RUN-STATE — Actor data structure (DESIGN)

> **Read this file FIRST after any compaction**, then `git log --oneline -15`, then continue.
> Never re-derive a sealed decision (§4) from memory — re-read it here.

**Started:** 2026-08-02 · **Branch:** `feat/game-logic` · **Base:** `50bff49a4` · **Size:** L
(files=10 logic=9 side_effects=2 — `workflow-gate.sh size L 10 9 2 40`)

> **⚠ SCOPE CHANGED 2026-08-02 (`D-67`): this is no longer an actor-core round.** The scope is **DEPTH** —
> manifest → kernel → up — with layers built bottom-up and **actor core LAST**. The actor design (§1-§18 of
> the dataflow spec) is complete and becomes L5. See §4l.

**Phase:** CLARIFY → DESIGN → REVIEW → **STOP at the PO checkpoint.** No code this round.
A separate BUILD goal is set only after the PO signs off on the design.

---

## 1. What this round is

Design the **actor and its data structure**. Nothing else.

The engine has ~996 lines of actual game law against ~24,500 lines of manifest plumbing. The
data structure the laws will range over does not exist, and three independent designs disagree
about what it should be. This round settles that, on paper, before any of it is built.

## 2. Standing invariants

1. **English in every persisted artifact** — docs, comments, commit messages, test names.
2. **No code this round.** The deliverable is a spec plus corrections to existing specs.
3. **Do not touch the data-ingest tier** — no glossary-service, knowledge-service, extraction, KG.
   Needing real extracted data is the signal that scope has been left; stop and say so.
4. **Never `git add -A`; never `--no-verify`.**
5. **Rot is deleted, not layered over.** An outdated statement left standing next to a corrected
   one produces two answers to one question — the exact failure this round exists to clear.
6. **Do not decide a sealed question again.** §4 is the record; §5 is what is still open.

## 3. What is IN and what is OUT

| IN | OUT |
|---|---|
| The actor's data structure: **pool · accumulated/stats · status · lifecycle** | **Combat vocabulary** — outcomes, damage chain, the syscall-vs-program question. That belongs to the combat element's own design. Current combat logic will be rewritten; it is wrong. |
| The mechanism / vocabulary line for each of the above (D-2) | The **trigger / generator mechanism** — pub-sub, register-pool, or loop (D-9). Every layer must leave a hook; none is built. |
| Per-actor-kind projection of reality-wide declarations (D-13) | The rest of the manifest — S1b enforcement arms, preset content, IMP-D5 gate |
| The rot list, with the schema cost of each removal | A float lint. It would have no subject today — the replayed path contains no float. Debt row with a trigger. |
| **A rot sweep of the existing specs** — update or delete outdated content | Any implementation |
| Applying the revised DF7-A4 (D-8) | |

## 4. Sealed decisions — PO-set 2026-08-02

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-1** | `QTY-Q11` = **yes**. Many realities, many genuinely different quantity sets, and more than one author. The declared-quantity machinery earns its cost. |
| **D-2** | **The engine closes on MECHANISM; the manifest closes on VOCABULARY.** The engine is an environment in the sense an OS or a DB is one: it fixes the operations, never the nouns. A hardcoded noun is a manifest that cannot grow. Derivation runs one way only — manifest defines the data structure, engine code derives from it. |
| **D-3** | `hp` is **one declared pool**, not a field. `Actor.hp`/`max_hp`, `VitalKind`, and `StatSlot::MaxHp` are the same rot at three levels; all three go. |
| **D-4** | An actor **may lack** a given pool. "Which pools this actor has" must be expressible. A village is an actor (`ActorKind::Locus`, applied 2026-07-30) and has no hp. |
| **D-5** | **Death is a STATUS, not `hp == 0`.** Three layers, each owning one thing: pool depletion is a fact about a number and emits an event · status is adjudicated and is what abilities hook onto · lifecycle transition is the durable consequence. Fusing them makes an ability that triggers at zero unimplementable. |
| **D-6** | `Vital` means *"which pool's exhaustion raises the mortality question"* — **not** *"which pool's zero means dead"*. The old wording put the conclusion inside the role. |
| **D-7** | **Stats are resources too.** One substrate, three kinds: **pool** (stored, spent and refilled) · **accumulated** (stored, grows via cultivation / equipment / achievement) · **derived** (not stored, recomputed from the other two). Today the accumulated kind has no home at all — `ModifierSource::Progression` names the layer and nothing produces its modifiers. |
| **D-8** | **DF7-A4 is revised** — see §7. It is not a float ban. The rule is byte-stable arithmetic with three named hazards. `world-gen` uses `f64` across 41 files and hashes its output deterministically; the old wording was contradicted by this repo's own code. |
| **D-9** | The **trigger / generator mechanism is deferred** — pub-sub, register-pool, or a loop, chosen later on performance grounds. This round's only obligation to it: every layer leaves a hook. Do not build the mechanism; do not block the path either. |
| **D-10** | **`StatSlot` opens.** Stats are vocabulary, so the closed 10-slot enum and `SLOT_COUNT` lose their present role. This was wrong at design time, not merely outgrown. |
| **D-11** | **The shipped engine is scaffolding and may be broken freely.** `ResourceDecl`, `StatSlot`, `Actor` are all in scope for redesign. Zero production realities exist, so schema movement is free today and will not be later. Design as if nothing exists, then record separately what can be reused. |
| **D-12** | **Lifecycle states are VOCABULARY; the state machine is MECHANISM.** The engine closes: holding a state, validating a transition against the declared set, the closed set of cascade policies, the append-only log, and atomicity of a transition with its cascade. The manifest declares: which states exist, which transitions are legal and what triggers them, which cascade policy each state uses, and the reason vocabulary. `HolderCascade` is the one reason the engine owns, because the cascade mechanism emits it. |
| **D-13** | *"Which pools does this actor have"* and *"which lifecycle does this actor follow"* are **one question**: how does a reality-wide declaration project onto a kind of actor? Answer it once. Answering it twice guarantees two mechanisms that drift. |
| **D-14** | **Combat vocabulary is out of scope** and belongs to the combat element's design. The current combat logic will be rewritten. Do not design around preserving it. |

### 4b. Decisions added by the census + prior-art pass — 2026-08-02, applied to the spec body

Each is landed in [`2026-08-02-actor-dataflow.md`](../specs/2026-08-02-actor-hub/analysis/2026-08-02-actor-dataflow.md) at the named
section, not merely recorded here.

| # | Decision | landed |
|---|---|---|
| **D-15** | **The 31 actor-keyed aggregates are the CORRECT decomposition and are not to be unified.** DDD practice: an *identity* may span bounded contexts, an entity *instance* may not — separate definitions per context, sharing one id, holding only the id across the boundary. `QTY-A7` is this rule stated for quantities; the corpus applied it to the whole actor before this spec existed. **The struct is renamed `Actor` → `ActorQuantities`** and is declared to be one context's view — the deterministic-law context. `D-11`'s *"`Actor` is in scope for redesign"* stands; this names what it is redesigned *into*. | §3 · §3.3 |
| **D-16** | **Three storage LAYERS — L1 simulation · L2 live · L3 durable — and the L1→L3 boundary is a LINK boundary, not a phase rule.** L1 crates do not depend on the persistence client, so a law writing durable storage is a compile error. Confirmed by a seamless-world MMO backend after ten iterations (*"simulation nodes never write to the database directly"*) and by Orleans' activation model. §4.7's phase discipline governs L1 **only**. | §4.7.6 |
| ~~**D-17**~~ | ⚠️ **RE-SCOPED by the PO 2026-08-02 — see `D-24`.** Sealed as an actor-core decision; it is not one. The prior art survives as a **hand-off** (§4.8.2), the four rulings are **withdrawn**. Re-deciding a sealed row is forbidden to the agent (§2 rule 6); the PO narrowing its own scope is not that, and the distinction is why this row is struck rather than edited. | §4.8 |
| **D-18** | **Archetype layout, and `granted` may never encode lifecycle.** A 2025 C++20 study measured the trade: sparse-set = cheap composition change, poor iteration; archetype = the inverse. We change composition once at spawn and iterate every tick ⇒ archetype. `QTY-A6`'s fixed width gains a justification independent of `size_of`. Second half: component *presence* is not mutually exclusive while a state machine's states are, so a lifecycle bit in `granted` makes *dead and alive* representable — the storage-side view of the `Suspended`-beside-`Destroyed` defect. | §3.1 · §3.2 |
| **D-19** | **Simulation LOD and fiction dilation are opposite and must never share a field.** LOD (how often/how coarsely we compute) is platform config and player-**invisible**; `TDIL_001` dilation (how much in-world time passes) is hashed rules and player-**visible** — it is the mechanic. The invisibility law is the discriminator, for the third time. | §5.8.4b |
| **D-20** | **A snapshot is a load accelerator and never a source.** The event log is the SSOT; a snapshot is a fold over a prefix of it. A snapshot that disagrees with the log is **discarded, never reconciled** — there is no merge, because the log wins by construction. It must carry the `seq` and `RulesPin` it was taken at, or it is unverifiable and will be believed anyway. Binds retention: GC must keep every ruleset a **snapshot's pin** references, not every ruleset a live binding references. | §6.1b |
| **D-21** | **"Tier" is retired as an unqualified word in this spec.** It names three unrelated ladders — **actor tier** (`Untracked..Irreversible`), **DP tier** (`T1/T2/T3` urgency), and what is now called a **storage layer**. `actor_status` is *"T2 / Reality"* while a `Stateful` actor is *"tier 2"*: two different 2s, one word. Every use in this spec is now qualified; the corpus is not, and that is `O-33`. | §3 |

### 4c. The register is adjudicated — and only FOUR rows need the PO (2026-08-02)

[§11](../specs/2026-08-02-actor-hub/analysis/2026-08-02-actor-dataflow.md) rules on all **34** open rows. Triage:

| class | meaning | count |
|---|---|---|
| **A** decidable — **decided in §11** | the document could answer it from what it already established | **22** |
| **B** needs a measurement | no argument settles it | **1** (`O-4`) |
| **C** no question left — unbuilt | design decided, work outstanding; §11.10 gives the build order | **10** |
| **D** the PO's call | value or timing, not engineering | **4 questions** |

Two merges the adjudication forced: **`O-1c`+`O-1d` → `O-1cd`** (a residency budget's only lever is
eviction, and eviction needs a durable row — so they were never independent), and **`O-1cd`+`O-19`+`O-20`
share one build**, the durable row. Listing them apart hid that.

One earlier decision is **superseded**: §5.10.2 said `ControlChanged` tells a replayer which mode a span
is in. §11.6 puts the flag on the **Decision** instead (`origin: Recomputable | Oracle`), so replay needs
no binding history at all — strictly simpler, and it makes *"an Oracle decision may never be recomputed"*
an assertion about a field rather than about a history.

**✅ ALL FOUR ANSWERED 2026-08-02.** Two were mis-framed by me (`PO-Q1` a scope error, `PO-Q3` a false
dichotomy), one was already the corpus's own decision (`PO-Q2`, `REC-12` of 2026-07-26), and one was
unrunnable (`PO-Q4`). **Nothing in this round now awaits the PO.**

| # | question | why it is the PO's |
|---|---|---|
| ~~**PO-Q1**~~ (`O-9`) | ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-02: no cross-island transfer, and the question was mis-scoped.** Remote trade, auction houses and banking are the **trade + economy** feature, per-reality — built from escrow and order books, not from the atomic two-delta primitive. Presenting the refusal as a *cost* was my error: those are a different feature, not a capability the primitive loses. A transfer is face to face; co-located actors are in one island by construction. Sealed as **`D-22`**. |
| ~~**PO-Q2**~~ (`O-1b`) | ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-02: canon is what is written to the LEDGER.** Unledgered state is fabricated, may be lost, and did not matter because nobody observed it. *Nobody asks who Farmer A was.* No game manages this; not managing it is cheaper and simpler. **The corpus had already decided it** — `AIT_001` §4.5, `REC-12`, a PO decision of 2026-07-26. My row was wrong twice (*"a timer"* → explicitly no scheduler; *"canon"* → declared NPCs are permanent in V1). Sealed as **`D-23`**. |
| ~~**PO-Q3**~~ (`O-28b`) | ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-02: neither — it leaves actor core.** Opinion, reputation, the social layer and NPC decision-making become a **separate AI + emotion feature**; folding them in makes actor core a burden. My question offered *now vs later* and both answers were wrong, because both assumed actor core owns the problem. Sealed as **`D-24`**, parked as **`P-6`**. |
| ~~**PO-Q4**~~ (`O-4`) | ✅ **WITHDRAWN 2026-08-02 — the experiment cannot run.** Its metric is validator-refusal rate; the validator is `O-13` and does not exist, so **both arms score zero and the run measures nothing**. Not a token-budget question until the ladder ships. Recommendation on the merits stands: **take three tables on judgement** — the stage split bounds the blast radius to S1 authoring, so being wrong costs re-authoring, not a schema or digest. |

### 4d. PO decisions 2026-08-02 — sealed

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-22** | **A transfer is island-local, and remote trade is a DIFFERENT FEATURE.** The atomic two-delta primitive requires both actors in one island; a cross-island attempt is refused by name. Auction houses, banking and remote markets belong to **trade + economy**, are **per-reality**, and are built from escrow and order books — mechanisms the transfer primitive was never meant to supply. Treating the refusal as a lost capability was a scope error: it is a **boundary**, not a limitation. Exactly-once handoff (Photon) stays recorded as a kernel growth path; **actor core does not need it.** |
| **D-27** | **A CONTRIBUTION IS DATA, NEVER CODE — `CPL-A17` generalised, and it is the whole decoupling design.** A feature does **not run during the tick**; it leaves **rows**, and the engine folds rows. **This collapses `D-25`'s two channels into one**: a feature owns tables, and one table shape (`ModifierRow`: actor · target ordinal · op · magnitude · layer · condition · source · expiry) is understood by the engine. **The engine never calls a feature, never enumerates features, never learns that "equipment" is a word** — it validates the row *shape* (target granted · op in the closed set · layer declared · condition names a declared threshold) and nothing else. The three alternatives all fail: a plugin registry makes the engine depend on features and makes replay load-order-dependent; **a single designated projector concentrates the coupling instead of removing it and grows without bound — §12.5's god-object collapse relocated one file over**; a runtime handshake is inverted but still order-dependent. External practice states it flatly: *systems should never have dependencies on each other*. **The test that a registry fails and this passes: a feature contributing NOTHING (opinion, titles, journeys) needs no opt-out, no null implementation, no registration — absence is free**, which is what lets the architecture survive features nobody has thought of. |
| **D-28** | **Staleness is made IMPOSSIBLE, not detectable: a modifier row is written and removed in the SAME COMMIT as the feature row that justifies it.** Equip writes `actor_equipment` **and** its modifier rows in one commit; unequip removes both in one commit. There is no sync step to forget because there is no second step — so no epoch, no invalidation window, no re-materialiser, no checker. **The corpus already validated this shape once**: `PL_007` §8.4's equipment clearing runs *"**inside** the `EF_001` HolderCascade atomic batch, **not after it**"*, for the identical reason. `D-16`'s L2 persistence service is what makes it cheap — both writes were already going to one place through one commit. Removal is **by `source`**, never by matching values, which also makes *"why is my attack 47"* answerable — the same causes-not-levels property recorded for the relational family. |
| **D-29** | **A conditional modifier's condition is a DECLARED THRESHOLD, never a predicate grammar.** *"+10 attack while below 30% hp"* tempts a predicate language over ordinals — and a grammar with nesting **is a scripting language, which is `CPL-A17` violated by the very mechanism meant to honour it**. We need none: a threshold is already the concept, already declared by the reality, already evaluated, and already sitting in `ActorQuantities.threshold_active`, so `condition: Option<ThresholdOrdinal>` costs **one bit test** and adds nothing to design, validate, sandbox or bound. An author whose condition is not expressible as a threshold must **declare the threshold** — the right pressure, because a condition worth attaching a modifier to is worth naming. **Fold order is `O-7`'s shape deliberately** — sum within layer · `DF7-A3`'s locked layer order · one clamp — because *a modifier and a delta are the same thing at different times*, and two fold rules for one concept is the defect this round keeps finding under other names. Stacking policy (full / highest-only / diminishing) is **declared vocabulary**, matching `PL_006`'s existing per-flag policy. |
| **D-30** | **The acceptance test for the architecture: adding a feature must touch ZERO files in actor core.** No engine edit, no registry entry, no struct field, no reserved ordinal, no projector edit — so `size_of::<ActorQuantities>()` never moves and `D-26`'s anti-accretion gate is never approached. **A feature that cannot be added without touching actor core is the design failing, and the feature is telling us so** — the same signal as `D-24` at feature scale and `D-25` at field scale, now at architecture scale. |
| **D-25** | **Actor core is the smallest set of fields that cannot be removed, and a feature attaches through exactly TWO channels — never by adding a field.** ⚠️ **REFINED by `D-27`: the two channels are one.** Channel A phrased as *"a feature contributes"* is ambiguous between *runs during the tick* and *leaves rows* — the first re-couples everything. **(A) PROJECT** — the feature contributes into `values[]` / `status_active` via a projection computed before phase 0 (the `DF07_001` model: **owns no aggregate**, purely derived). **(B) OWN A TABLE** keyed by `actor_id`, which actor core never reads. **There is no channel C.** A feature that cannot attach without editing `ActorQuantities` is the signal to stop — `D-24`'s test at field scale. **Where an edge lives is decided by cardinality, never by convenience: the MANY side, or a pair table when both are many.** An actor *owns* an item ⇒ the edge is on the **item** (`EF_001` `LocationKind::HeldBy` already does this); an `inventory: Vec<ItemId>` on the actor is the same error as `opinions` and `titles`, and by the third the actor is a god object. **Audit result on my own struct: `statuses: StatusSet` was `PL_006`'s whole aggregate bolted into the substrate, and — being the only field with no byte count — it made `size_of` incomputable, so the `QTY-A12` assertion could not have been written against it. Replaced by `status_active: u64`, a phase-0 projection; total stated at 216 B.** |
| **D-26** | **`size_of::<ActorQuantities>()` is the architecture's ANTI-ACCRETION GATE, not merely a `Vec` guard.** It is what makes *"a feature may not add a field"* a **build failure** rather than a review opinion — so weakening it for a good local reason removes the only mechanical defence against the god-object collapse. Corollary: **an unsized field disables it silently** (`O-36`). The three companion defences: an **ordinal belongs to a reality's declared quantity, never to a feature** (else 32 runs out and every digest moves); **only the `id` crosses a feature boundary** (`D-15`), so cross-feature effects go through channel A and never through a join; and **§4.7's laws read the quantity block only**, which is what keeps a law from coming to depend on a feature that does not exist yet — and therefore what makes `D-24`'s hand-off safe. |
| **D-24** | **The relational / emotion family is a SEPARATE FEATURE and leaves actor core.** Opinion, reputation, the social layer and NPC decision-making become an **AI + emotion engine** with its own social system and decision tree. Folding them into actor core makes actor core a burden. **The test the PO applied is now this project's scoping test: *the game is playable without this feature*.** Pools, status, lifecycle and control fail that test — remove any and there is no game. Opinion passes it — remove it and there is a game with flatter NPCs. **A thing you can remove and still ship is not substrate.** Actor core's only remaining obligation is `D-9`'s shape: *do not build it; do not block the path either* — discharged by §3.3's exclusion table, §8.1's 31-context extension point, and the fact that no law inputs a relation. Consequence: **`O-28b` leaves the deferred list entirely** (defer-gate #1, out of scope — a hand-off, not a deferral), and **`O-35`** records that `ACT_001` bundles three contexts, which is the receiving feature's first question. |
| **D-23** | **CANON IS WHAT IS WRITTEN TO THE LEDGER.** State that was never written is fabricated, may be lost, and did not matter — because nothing observed it. *Nobody asks who Farmer A was.* This is not a compromise for performance; it is what the word *canon* means here, and it happens to be cheaper and simpler. **Mechanical form (`O-1b2`):** demotion is lossless exactly when materialized state is a pure function of *(ledger entries, `RulesPin`, elapsed fiction time)* — i.e. **a tier-2 row is a fold over the log, never a source**, which is `P-F`'s snapshot rule one level down. If that does not hold, the defect is the **unlogged state**, not the demotion. `O-20`'s invertibility restriction is what makes the elapsed-time term closed-form, so it buys demotion-safety as an unnoticed side effect. Already the corpus's decision — `AIT_001` §4.5 / `REC-12`, 2026-07-26. |

### 4e. The two omissions the PO caught 2026-08-02 — measured, not asserted

The PO named two gaps this round had walked past for eight turns: **(1)** the data lifecycle from kernel to
FE, across many modules and many storage forms, plus multi-layer caching — *a game that does not define
this will certainly die*; **(2)** the ES/manifest audit — how the kernel ingests, how an actor instance is
created, its lifecycle, and what plays the role of a garbage collector, *because Rust has none and an
instance has stages, so the answer must be explicit*.

[§14](../specs/2026-08-02-actor-hub/analysis/2026-08-02-actor-dataflow.md) answers them against code. Both were real. The measured
results:

| # | Decision / finding |
|---|---|
| **D-31** | **The chain is NINE hops and at least four hold a copy of the same fact — and exactly ONE boundary has a loss contract.** `ingress` (two lanes, `Live` strictly before `Background`, admission stamps `Seq` and nothing else) → `Domain::State` in process → `IslandCheckpoint` → `event_store` (SSOT) → `load_aggregate` → `projections` → `canon_cache` → gateway → `ChannelRoom` → FE context → `localStorage`. Only `IslandCheckpoint` states what is lost and how it heals (§10.5's loss table). **`P-F`'s rule is what the whole chain needs, applied nine times rather than replaced by nine rules**: a derived copy is a load accelerator, never a source · it disagrees with the log ⇒ discarded, never reconciled · it carries the `seq` it was taken at or it cannot be checked. |
| **D-32** | **The GC answer is a GENERATIONAL INDEX, it is already built, and only its detection half exists.** `Gen(u32)` is *"bumped on lifecycle change"* and compared in `Precondition::EntityAlive { id, generation }`, so a stale reference **fails** instead of addressing a recycled slot — and `registry.rs:20` shows the team reasoning about exactly this (*"Gen(0) would RESURRECT old-epoch refs"*). **What does not exist: removal.** `actors.remove` appears **nowhere in the tree**; `actors.insert` appears only in a smoke binary. So disposal today is *nothing is ever disposed*, chosen by omission. The missing half is the **transition** — which stage removes the row, what it emits, whether an id is reusable — and `existence` (§5.8) is where that stage lives, unconnected to storage. ⚠️ **`saturating_add` on the generation counters converts a monotonic guard into a constant at `u32::MAX`**, resurrecting exactly what `Gen(0)` is guarded against, at the other end. |
| **D-33** | **There is no spawn path, and it was never the kernel's to own.** `Domain::State` is **opaque** to the kernel; entities move only through `extract`/`install` and *"it never looks inside."* The only way an actor enters an island today is `install` **on arrival**, which presupposes it existed elsewhere. This gives `O-14`'s *"`V4` spawn has no implementation at all"* its cause rather than leaving it a symptom. |
| **D-34** | **§3 decided the ELEMENT layout and never the COLLECTION.** `CombatState { actors: BTreeMap<EntityId, Actor> }` — a tree map is not an archetype store: deterministic iteration by key (valuable for replay, and likely the reason) but pointer-chasing traversal, which is the property §3.1's archetype argument exists to avoid. **A dense `Copy` element inside a tree map buys the `size_of` assertion and none of the cache locality.** Not a defect to fix today — a decision never taken, in a section that reads as though it had been. |

### 4f. SSOT + the write/read boundary — PO direction 2026-08-02

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-35** | **`services/commit-service/src/domain/` is a STUB (649 lines) and must never be cited as a decision.** It implements combat — a feature `D-14` says is **undesigned and will be rewritten** — and it **misled this specification four times**, once into writing *"this is already shipped"* about the turn economy. **The evidence rule: `crates/` (`sim-core` · `dp-kernel` · `ruleset-core` · `ruleset-loader` · `projections` · `rebuilder`) carries invariants, gates and tests and IS measurable evidence; the service-side domain sketch is not.** A stub read as truth is a rot source, and this one has a negative asset value today. |
| **D-36** | **TWO SSOTs, and only two. ① RULES** — the pinned ruleset digest plus the content manifest (`CPL-A8`). **② FACTS** — the event log. **Everything else is derived**: aggregates, snapshots, both caches, projections, `Domain::State`, room state, FE context. `Domain::State` inside a running island is **not** an SSOT — it is the fold-so-far and must be reconstructible from log + checkpoint, or the island cannot be restored. **The kernel already implements this**: `load_aggregate` reconstructs by folding events with a snapshot fast-path, and `0004_aggregate_snapshots_table.up.sql` states flatly *"snapshots are a write-path cache, **not the SSOT**"* — which is `P-F`, written by this team before I derived it from Orleans. Second time in two sections (`DR-11`). |
| **D-37** | **SINGLE WRITER per aggregate/stream, and the reason is REPLAY, not concurrency.** Two writers *"store interleaving events based on different states, and when replayed it is not possible to reconstruct the correct state."* The corpus already states the rule (`EVT-A4` producer-role binding; `PL_005` §5.2 — the **aggregate owner** emits the Derived event, never the interaction feature). **It is checked nowhere** (`O-49`), and the check is a gate over a table that already exists. |
| **D-38** | **DECLARED READERS — a sole writer is not enough, because reads form hidden contracts.** Two components reading the same rows in mutually incompatible ways, with nothing recording the dependency, is the documented anti-pattern; `PL_005`'s validator alone reads **six** other features' aggregates and none of them knows. A feature therefore **declares its read-set**, enforced at `dp::read_projection_reality`. What this buys that prose ownership never does: **the blast radius of a schema change becomes computable**, because readers are enumerable rather than greppable (`O-50`). |
| **D-39** | **ONE rule for every derived copy, applied nine times — not nine rules.** `P-F` generalised across §14.2's chain: **rebuildable from the SSOT · carries the `seq`/version it was taken at · DISCARDED on divergence, never reconciled** — the SSOT wins by construction. Today exactly one of the nine hops (`IslandCheckpoint`, §10.5) states a loss contract. |
| **D-40** | **The ownership matrix must become a MACHINE CONTRACT.** Three needs now converge on `_boundaries/01_feature_ownership_matrix.md`: `O-27`'s generated `aggregate_type` enum, `D-37`'s single-writer gate, `D-38`'s read-sets. **One generated artifact serves all three.** And the file has already been caught carrying false *"applied"* claims (`REC-97`) — the strongest available argument that a hand-maintained index cannot hold this weight. |

**⚠ Open to the PO — demolishing the upper layer.** Evidence, not a recommendation: **for** — 649 lines,
implementing an undesigned feature scheduled for rewrite, which has already supplied false evidence four
times to the person best positioned to know better; **against** — it is the only worked example of
`Domain` being implemented end-to-end (`crates/sim` has its own harness domain, so the dependency is
likely nil, unverified). **The distinction that matters is not delete-or-keep but kernel-or-feature**, and
`D-35` achieves that at zero cost either way.

### 4g. Completeness audit vs standards — the answer contradicts the question (2026-08-02)

The PO asked what else is missing, after four rounds of finding serious gaps (read/write · caching ·
lifecycle · data boundary). Searched ISO/IEC 25010's eight quality characteristics + the MMO checklist,
then measured the tree.

| # | Decision / finding |
|---|---|
| **D-41** | **Those four were never missing from the PROJECT — they were missing from the actor-core SPEC.** `crates/dp-kernel` is **15 095 lines / ~30 modules**, most headed *"Rust mirror of `contracts/<x>/`"*, and `contracts/` holds **~40 contracted domains**: `service_acl` (1 430, default-DENY) · `events` (3 281) · `capacity` (1 257) · `resilience` (1 576) · `observability` (2 315) · `tracing` (1 593) · `lifecycle` (1 197) · `entity_status` (889) · plus `cache/keys.yaml` · `retention/event_classes.yaml` · `backup/policy.yaml` · `migrations/manifest.yaml` · `rebuild/config.yaml` · `integrity/config.yaml` · `chaos` · `incidents` · `postmortems` · `slo` · `alerts` · `pii` · `supply_chain`. **This is the FOURTH consecutive round of the same error** (`DR-7` → `DR-11` → `DR-12` → this): a critical thing declared missing, and found in the tree. |
| **D-42** | **The fix is a PROCEDURE STEP, not more care — and it already existed.** CLAUDE.md's always-loaded section points at `docs/standards/README.md`, which *"catalogues every cross-cutting rule/law/invariant/machine-contract"* and has a **quick-nav by concern**. **I never opened it in nine rounds.** The order is now: **standards index → `contracts/<concern>/` → `dp-kernel/src/<concern>.rs` → then design.** Corollary: every concern this spec marks *inherited* must **name what it inherits from**, because a spec silent about a solved problem reads exactly like one with an unsolved problem. |
| ~~**D-43**~~ | ⚠️ **RETRACTED by `D-45`.** Called a danger; nothing exists to corrupt, and the defence is already built and wired. |
| **D-43-orig** (kept for the record) | **One gap the platform tier structurally cannot cover.** Practice: *events outlive the code that produced them*, and the platform has the standard answer — `event_version` + an **upcaster** path + `LAW_VERSION` classified `Mutability::Frozen`/`Strategy::Forbidden`. **But an upcaster fixes payload SHAPE, and `QTY-A14` says an ordinal is meaningless without ITS DIGEST.** Change a reality's declared quantity set and a stored `values[7] -= 30` remains shape-valid, upcasts cleanly, and now names a **different quantity**. No schema-versioning tactic can detect it, because nothing about the shape changed. The intended fix is `RulesPin` (§3) + `CPL-A8`'s manifest pin, and **neither exists in code** (`O-45`; `RealityManifest` = eight docs, zero lines). Tracked as **`O-53`**, and it is the most dangerous row in the register. |
| **D-44** | **The game tier states no quality targets.** `contracts/slo` exists at the platform tier; the game tier names no tick budget, no per-island actor ceiling, no acceptable projection lag. **`size_of::<ActorQuantities>() == 216` is the only quantified target in the entire spec** — which makes every performance argument in it (archetype layout, `Copy`, fold cost) an argument against an unstated bar (`O-54`). |

### 4h. Schema evolution — PO correction, then measurement went further (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-45** | **`O-53` is CLOSED: the ordinal↔identity defence is built, wired and guarded.** `ruleset-loader/src/epoch.rs:154` `activate_reality_epoch` → `prior_quantity_tables(reality)` → `check_never_reused(priors)` → `EpochSwitchError::OrdinalReused`, **validate-before-append** (*"a refused switch leaves the binding table untouched… an append-then-validate would have to delete a row from a table whose entire guarantee is that rows are never deleted"*), plus a redundant `NotBound` check whose stated reason is that an unbound reality yields an **empty priors vector** making every switch *"trivially permitted"* — **they named it `NV-3` and closed it.** I was about to warn about vacuity to a call site that documents its own. Also at the switch: `resolve_progression`, added after a **probe** found a reality moving to epoch 2 onto a ruleset whose ladder had been deleted, returning `Ok`. |
| **D-46** | **The MODEL the spec still owed, now written (§17.3): identity is not a per-event fact.** Two questions, not one — *what does ordinal 7 mean* is answered by the reality's **append-only ordinal registry** at **zero bytes per event**; *what rules were in force* is answered by `RulesPin` per-actor + `RulesPinChanged`. Stamping a 32-byte digest on every event pays forever for a question the registry answers once; `QTY-A14` is satisfied by the digest being **derivable from `(reality_id, epoch)`**, which is what the binding table is. `QTY-A5` supplies the registry rule — **a quantity is retired by retiring its ordinal, never by reassigning it** — the ES discipline (*you can never retire an old event version*) applied to the ordinal instead of the payload. **Remaining work is the rules half only**: `RulesPin` per-actor, `RulesPinChanged` (`O-17`). |
| **D-47** | **`O-15` gains a strictly better argument.** `prior_quantity_tables` reads the reality's ordinal history, so a collector evicting old rulesets does not merely break *replay* months later — **it breaks the next epoch switch, immediately and loudly.** A retention bug that fails at the next admission is discoverable; one that fails at a future replay is not. And the two artifacts should be separated: the **ordinal map** (small, what identity needs) versus the **full ruleset** (what behaviour replay needs) may deserve different retention answers, and nothing distinguishes them today. |
| **D-48** | **No unwired guards exist — measured, not assumed.** Swept every guard-shaped `pub fn` in `ruleset-core` · `ruleset-loader` · `sim-core` for non-test call sites: `assert_classification_is_total` 3 · `ensure_root` 2 · `check_never_reused` 1 · `assert_paths_are_total` **0**, and that zero is a **false positive** — a compile-time exhaustiveness device whose value *is* being compiled. **Make it a lint** (`O-57`): it is the shape `non-vacuity.md` does not cover, since all four of its shapes assume the check *runs*. |

### 4i. `O-34`..`O-57` adjudicated — and three clusters were one item each (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-49** | **The tick gains PHASE 0 · RESOLVE; Input becomes phase 1.** `O-37` (`control` is an unruled cache) and `O-38` (`status_active` has no computation point) are **one** item: both are *a derived field in the quantity block that must be refreshed before any law reads it*. Phase 0 folds modifier rows into `values[]`, refreshes every derived field, and evaluates modifier `expiry` (whose consequences enter this tick's wave under `O-10`'s budget — same answer as `O-12`). **No law runs; no input is admitted; nothing durable is written.** After phase 0 the quantity block is complete and self-contained, which is what makes *"a law reads the quantity block and nothing else"* a property rather than an aspiration. The cache rule it discharges: **reconstructible · never authoritative · refreshed at phase 0** — `P-F` applied inside a struct instead of inside a snapshot. |
| **D-50** | **ONE COMMIT PRIMITIVE — `commit_with_modifiers(feature_row, modifiers)`.** `O-39` (`modifier_rows` has no owner; many writers is the `D-15` smell) and `O-40` (nothing enforces `D-28`'s atomicity) both dissolve by **refusing to let a feature write the table at all**. One call, one transaction, one `seq`: unequip passes the removal **and** the modifier deletions, and there is no second step to forget because the API offers none. **Single writer** (the engine), **atomicity as a signature** rather than a rule, and shape validation at one place. It is the shape `PL_007` §8.4 found by hand — *"runs **inside** the HolderCascade atomic batch, not after it"* — made the only expressible one. |
| **D-51** | **THE SLOT TABLE — `O-43b` + `O-46b` + `O-47` are one design, and `D-23` decides it before we start.** A tier-2 row is a **fold over the ledger**, so **disposal is CACHE EVICTION, not deletion**: freeing an actor frees a *slot*, the ledger is untouched, re-materialisation is a re-fold. Container: dense `Vec<Option<ActorQuantities>>` + per-slot `gens` + a free list + an `ActorId → SlotIx` index. Iteration order = **slot order** — deterministic for replay, which is exactly what `BTreeMap` was buying and a dense array buys by construction. **`EntityId` is never reused; the SLOT is**, guarded by the `Gen` the kernel already built. **`Gen(u64)`, saturating, plus a tick invariant that no slot sits at `MAX`** — `O-47`'s real complaint is that saturation turns a monotonic guard into a constant, and widening + an invariant makes *unreachable* **checked** instead of argued, without adding a failure path to an operation whose caller could not act on one. Residue: which `existence` states permit eviction is **declared vocabulary** (`D-12`). |
| **D-52** | **One fixed-point scale: `1e-4`.** `PL_006` §8.3b mixes **‰** and **%** in one table, and `D-8` requires byte-stable arithmetic — two scales in one column means two readers compute different numbers from one row. `1e-4` represents ‰ (=10) and % (=100) exactly, gives 0.01% resolution, and `i32` spans ±214 748 whole units. **The manifest authors in human units; the loader converts once at S1→S2 and the converted value is inside the hashed bytes**, so the conversion is pinned and cannot drift. |
| **D-53** | **Every derived copy carries `(reality_id, seq)`** — 16 bytes, uniform across §14.2's nine hops. Without it *"discarded on divergence"* has nothing to compare and `P-F` degrades into a slogan. |

**Register status: 10 closed · 30 decided · 2 need a measurement (`O-4` blocked on `O-13`; `O-54`'s tick
budget and projection-lag targets need hardware) · 15 are build-only · 1 handed to `P-6` · ZERO awaiting
the PO.**

**Six merges across §11 + §18** — `O-1c`+`O-1d` · `O-19`+`O-20`+`O-1cd` · `O-37`+`O-38` · `O-39`+`O-40` ·
`O-43b`+`O-46b`+`O-47` · `O-15`+`O-53`'s retention. That rate is not luck: **a register accrues one row per
SYMPTOM, and symptoms outnumber causes.** Adjudicating in clusters is what makes 57 rows read as the ~20
decisions they actually are.

### 4j. RED TEAM — four rounds, measured (2026-08-02)

Four cold-start reviewers, disjoint lenses, all instructed to measure rather than argue and to treat
`services/commit-service/src/domain/` as a stub. Full adjudication in spec §19-§23.

| # | Decision / finding |
|---|---|
| **D-54** | **FIVE FATALs, and NOT ONE is in the actor data structure.** `ST-1` — a 90-day partition `DROP TABLE` silently truncates the fold: `load_aggregate` Path A starts at `default()` with **no continuity check**, and there is **no archive reader**, so it returns `Ok` on a state that never existed. **This attacks `D-23` at the root** — if the ledger can be truncated beneath the fold, re-materialisation returns a different actor wearing the same id. `ST-2` — `snapshot_policy.yaml` is **empty**, `snapshot_write` has **zero non-test callers**, `read_stream` has **no LIMIT** ⇒ the cold path is 100% of loads and unbounded (~90 MB to load one aggregate at 90k events). `OP-2` — replay-on-create over an **untrimmed** stream is O(N/64) sequential round trips before anyone can join. `OP-3` — the room folds the **reality** stream, not its channel; `channel_id` is on the wire and `parseEnvelope` drops it. `OP-1` — the read path has no production callers. **The design survives the review; what does not survive is everything it stands on.** |
| **D-55** | **`size_of` is unknown, and that IS the finding.** Three reviewers measured **232 · 232 · 256**; none got 216. `RulesPin` is **68 B not 40** (the overlay digest is a second 32-byte hash), and the spec's own annotations sum to 224 — **wrong on its own arithmetic before any measurement.** Real L1 footprint is **~274 B/actor**. And `grep -rn ActorQuantities crates/` returns **nothing**: §12.5's *"load-bearing compile-time assertion"* **has no subject**. **The repo's own *intent is not a mechanism* failure, committed inside the section explaining why intent is not a mechanism.** |
| **D-56** | **The anti-accretion gate redirects growth rather than removing it.** A feature that may not add a field and must affect a quantity has exactly ONE door — `modifier_rows` — and it has **no cap, no eviction order, no fold budget**, while §9.3 bounds the relational family carefully. Multi-reality makes the contributor count author-controlled and unbounded by construction. Accepted in full; the sharpest critique of my own design in the review. |
| **D-57** | **A presence claim about code must cite CODE — `D-42`'s procedure was asymmetric.** §16.4 asserted *"`CausalityWaitTimeout` and `wait_for` exist in the DP primitives, so the mechanism is there"*. **Verified: 0 hits across all source; they exist only in `docs/03_planning/`.** This is the inverse of the four false-absence claims, and `D-42` would not have caught it, because it points at `contracts/` and `dp-kernel/`. **And the SLI aimed at this failure mode is itself vacuous** — `sli_realtime_freshness` is wired through Prometheus into three burn-rate tiers and **no source file emits its metric**, so the one alarm pointed here cannot ring. |
| **D-58** | **The two highest-leverage changes, one per layer.** **Design:** `CeilingBinding::Quantity(QuantityOrdinal)` — one variant, one codec arm, one acyclicity check. The openness tax is paid **in full** while doc 35 §6.5 marks **2 of the PO's 7 systems not expressible**, and this is the seam most of them need. **Explicitly NOT raising 32 to 64**, which increases the tax and adds zero expressive power. **Runtime:** make phase 0 **one-pass and conditional** using `StatEpoch` + `StatSnapshot::is_stale`, **already shipped**, with `commit_with_modifiers` as the free bump point — measured **2 685 ns to ~122 ns** dirty, **~0** clean. §8's own census recorded that incumbent 1 800 lines before §18.1 replaced it unremarked. |
| **D-59** | **`O-56`'s clean bill was scoped too narrowly.** It swept guard-shaped `pub fn` in three crates; **constructors in a fourth were outside a scope I chose**, and two reviewers independently found `SnapshotCache::new`, `snapshot_write`, `load_aggregate` and `canon_cache::Cache` unwired. **`NV-3` inside my own verification method, one section after I named it.** `O-57`'s lint must cover constructors and must not be crate-scoped. |

**Register: 5 FATAL · ~20 MAJOR · `O-58`..`O-88` opened. None adjudicated yet — that is the next
session's work, and §23.4 already separates what the turn-based + multi-reality trade-off genuinely
excuses from what it does not.**

### 4k. THE PATCH PLAN — being first means the foundation's holes are ours to plan (PO 2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-60** | **The first game element inherits the foundation's holes as PLANNING obligations.** Being first is what surfaces them; surfacing them is what makes them ours to schedule. **The defer gate's *"out of scope — belongs to a different track"* does NOT apply to the layer this element runs on** — that is a prerequisite, not a neighbour — and CLAUDE.md's anti-laziness rule already says the rest: *missing infrastructure is not blocked, it is unbuilt work to implement*. **This corrects my own framing twice:** §23.1's *"what does not survive is everything it stands on"* read the FATALs as someone else's ledger, and §22.2 downgraded `OP-1` with *"not built ≠ broken"* — right about severity, wrong about ownership. **What it does not license:** actor core does not now own the platform. The obligation is a plan with an owner and an ordering, and for several items the honest plan is *"trails, with a named trigger"*. |
| **D-61** | **`ST-1` and `ST-2` are ONE work item, and the second is the fix for the first.** Without snapshots a partition drop is **truncation**; with snapshots plus one precondition it is **compaction**. The rule: *a partition may be dropped only if every aggregate with events in it has a snapshot at or beyond that partition's high-water version.* This also retires `ST-2`'s unbounded cold path (Path B replaces Path A) — **one mechanism, two FATALs**, and the tree already has the table, the migration and the writer signature. **And it rescues `D-23`**: *"a row is a fold over the ledger"* is only true if the ledger under it is intact, so snapshot-before-drop is what stops `D-23`/`D-51` and the whole disposal-is-eviction argument from resting on unbounded retention. |
| **D-62** | **Four Tier-0 items settle before the DESIGN is sound, and all four are ours.** **T0-1** snapshot-before-drop (`D-61`). **T0-2** measure `size_of` and put the assertion in code — three reviewers got 232/232/256, `grep ActorQuantities crates/` returns nothing, and the number was wrong on the spec's own arithmetic (207+`id`=224, stated 216) **before anyone measured, because nothing consumed it**. **T0-3** bound `modifier_rows` per actor — my own defect, and the one FATAL the actor design produced. **T0-4** generalise never-reuse over the four ordinal spaces actor core creates — **now is when it is free**, because `status_active`/`existence`/`threshold_active` persist their ordinals per actor and retrofitting after they carry state is strictly more expensive. |
| **D-63** | **Tier 1 — actor core cannot RUN without these.** **T1-1** register the actor event types: `_registry.yaml` declares 15 types across `reality`/`npc`/`world`/`canon` and **not one of the 31 actor-keyed aggregates appears** (verified). ⚠ A reviewer states the L2.I validator rejects unregistered types at write time; **I verified the registry gap and did NOT verify the rejection** — that changes the urgency, not the work. **T1-2** retention classes for actor events, plus the `NV-2` defect underneath (the canon CI gate is asserted against `retention-worker`, which never touches `events`, while `archive-worker`, which drops them, has zero class awareness). **T1-3** phase 0 one-pass + conditional. **T1-4** decide where `modifier_rows` live during a tick. **T1-5** wire one read path end-to-end — the next thing to build, not a defect. |
| **D-64** | **Tier 2 is the live path, and two items are cheap enough that not doing them is a choice.** `ChannelRoom` is the only live route from durable truth to a client and it **runs**. **T2-1 is one line** — filter `channel_id` in `parseEnvelope`; the field is already on the wire and is dropped, causing 8× read amplification, a cross-channel roster leak, and a targeting bug where the server offers a target and then rejects the action. **T2-2** return the reached cursor instead of `'$'` — the prerequisite for bounding the replay. Then T2-3 replay bound · T2-4 read `registry_version` · T2-5 bound ingress (`Bulkhead` exists and has no consumer). |
| **D-65** | **Every trailing item carries a NAMED TRIGGER, and that is the difference between a plan and an omission.** `OP-8` rebuild → the first reality large enough to exceed the 30-minute freeze ceiling. `MR-10` R×C consumer groups → the second dozen realities. `O-71` `CeilingBinding::Quantity` → **not a patch at all** but the next design round, with doc 35 §6.5's *"2 of the PO's 7 systems not expressible"* as its acceptance criterion. |
| **D-66** | **Going first worked exactly as intended.** Five FATALs; the actor data structure produced **one** (`T0-3`). The other four are foundation, found **because** actor core went first — **a game element is a load test for the platform beneath it.** The Tier-0 cost is cheaper now, before anything has persisted state, than at any later point. **And one correction stands above the rest** because it is the shape this whole session shared: `T0-2` puts a number in code that four design rounds, four red-team agents and I all reasoned from and **nobody measured.** |

### 4l. SCOPE CHANGE — depth-first, layers bottom-up (PO 2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-67** | **The scope is DEPTH: manifest → kernel → up. Layers are implemented bottom-up, each finishing to ITS OWN exit criteria — not to the extent the layer above happens to need — and actor core is LAST.** This supersedes §24's ordering, which was *actor-core-first with patches* and framed foundation work as *prerequisites of actor core*. **The difference is not cosmetic: under §24 a layer gets built to the extent something above needs it, which is exactly how a layer gets half-built and stays that way.** §24's findings all survive; §25.7 re-homes each work item into a layer. |
| **D-68** | **The measured stack, from `Cargo.toml` dependency edges — the real order, not an assumed one.** **L0 kernel primitives** `sim-core` 2 421 · `dp-kernel` 16 033 (+`breaker-core` 440) · `projections` 1 486 · `meta-rs` 4 188 — **built as code, read paths unwired** (`load_aggregate`, `SnapshotCache`, `canon_cache`, `snapshot_write` all have zero non-test callers). **L1 ruleset** `ruleset-core` 5 198 · `ruleset-loader` 5 788 — built, but **`validate.rs` is 141 lines and ONE `pub fn`**. **L2 manifest → content: 0 LOC.** **L3 game rules** `game-rules` 1 892, partly real. **L4 services** — `commit-service/domain` 649 STUB, `archive-worker` 2 826 + `retention-worker` 1 476 + `meta-worker` 8 384 built and deployed, `game-server` the only live path. **L5 observability — 9 SLI names declared, 0 emitted.** |
| **D-69** | **The three gaps the PO named, measured — and two are worse than stated.** ① **The manifest tier does not exist**: `RealityManifest` appears **once** in all code and that once is a **doc-comment in `validate.rs`**, against **154 documents**. `CPL-A8` requires the content manifest pinned into the reality binding as the ruleset is; there is nothing to pin. ② **Observability is 9 declarations and 0 instruments** — I had recorded this as one bad SLI (`O-82`); measured across `contracts/slo` it is **all nine**. Every burn-rate ladder and alert threshold sits on a series that does not exist, so `rate()` returns no data, **nothing can ever fire, and every dashboard reads green**. This is not a monitoring gap; it is monitoring that **cannot report a failure**. ③ **The collector runs**: `archive-worker` has a k8s deployment and drops partitions at 90 days. There **is** an `archive-restore` operator CLI — **but it restores into `events_restore_<YYYYMM>`, a different table `load_aggregate` does not read**. So `ST-1` is *recoverable-by-an-operator-who-knows*, not *lost* — better than recorded, still a silent-wrong-answer path. |
| **D-70** | **Six layers, each with an exit criterion, and each row is a DEPENDENCY not a preference.** **L0 kernel wiring** — every read path has ≥1 production call site + a snapshot writer with a non-empty policy; *actor core cannot read its own state without it, because `D-23` makes a row a fold and `load_aggregate` is the fold*. **L1 retention safety** — snapshot-before-drop, class-aware archive, the canon gate asserted against the component that can violate it; *without it the fold returns `Ok` on an actor that never existed*. **L2 the validator ladder** — §2.6's obligations, and `check_never_reused` generalised over every ordinal registry; *the four unguarded ordinal spaces are created BY actor core*. **L3 the manifest tier** — `RealityManifest` in code, content-addressed, pinned per `CPL-A8`; *`RulesPin` and every content reference resolve through it*. **L4 observability** — all 9 SLIs emitted, and at least one proven to fire by breaking its subject; *you cannot build L0–L3 blind*. **L5 game rules + actor core** — with `T0-2` and `T0-3` landing inside it. |
| **D-71** | **⚠ L3 is the risk, and I will not size it from here.** Everything else is wiring, a precondition, a validator body, or nine counters — all high or medium confidence. **L3 is a tier designed across 154 documents and built zero times**, and `CPL-*`/`PGN-*`/`40_progression_planner/` describe a build-time pipeline with an LLM stage, a human gate at every boundary and a provenance model. **The honest next step is a SIZING SPIKE on L3 before the layer plan is committed to** — producing an estimate here would repeat this session's characteristic error, which is exactly what I have been wrong about five times. |

### 4m. L3 SIZING SPIKE — measurement only, no code (2026-08-02)

PO: *do the spike, then keep clearing the spec — **do not implement yet**, stub code and garbage cost a
great deal to de-rot later.* This repo has the receipt: a 649-line stub misled the spec four times
(`D-35`). **No code was written.**

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-72** | **`O-89` CLOSED: L3 is FOUR things under one name, and only one is small, new and on L5's path.** **L3-α — ALREADY BUILT (~5 788 LOC):** `ruleset-loader` already ingests authored TOML through a provider layer stack (`layer.rs`/`patch*.rs`, `resolve()`, `load_reality()`), content-addresses it (`store.rs`, digest-verified on read), pins it (`binding.rs`) and switches epochs with never-reuse (`epoch.rs`) — and `artifacts/engine_default.toml` is a real authored artifact test-asserted to resolve to the same digest as the code. **My §25 measurement counted the string `RealityManifest`, not the capability**; the capability is largely there under another name. **L3-β — SMALL, high confidence:** three edits to shipped, tested code — a generic (or second typed) `put`/`get` on a store whose addressing and verification do not change · **one field** on `RealityBinding` + a migration · admit both digests atomically in `activate_reality_epoch`, which already validates-before-appending. **L3-γ — not a layer** (see `D-74`). **L3-δ — a separate TRACK, not a dependency.** |
| **D-73** | **Generation is not on the critical path: L5 needs *a* manifest, not a *generated* one.** The CPL/PGN corpus is ~6 279 lines across 16 documents and 0 LOC. Generation is how you get a manifest **cheaply, at scale, from a book**; hand-authoring is how you get one **at all**, and `engine_default.toml` proves that path works — layered, digest-verified, test-asserted. This is not an argument against `CPL-A10`'s generator-as-spine design; it is an argument about **order**. **Consequence: the risk in the layer plan MOVES** — L3 is no longer it. **L2 is now the largest genuinely-new build** (§2.6's obligations against a `validate.rs` that is 141 lines and one `pub fn`), and L0 is second (wiring a read side that has never had a production caller). |
| **D-74** | **WE decide the manifest's STRUCTURE; the author decides VOCABULARY — and measured, 29 accreted fields are FOUR declaration kinds.** *(PO correction, mid-spike.)* Saying the fields *"arrive with their features"* hands **schema authority** to features — `O-68`'s defect one level up, and how a manifest becomes a god class by the slow road without anyone deciding it should. The corpus already carries the axiom (**`EPL-A2`**: *slot shapes registered in code, members authored per reality — `QTY-A6` generalised*); nobody applied it to `RealityManifest`, which is exactly how 30 feature documents produced 29 fields. **The four kinds:** **ROSTER** id-keyed, 14 · **RELATION** pair-keyed, 4 — **`D-17`/`P-C` one level up** · **TUNING** classification-governed, 8 — `ruleset-core/classification.rs` already ships `Floor` × `Mutability` × `Strategy` · **GEOMETRY** 2 — `world-gen` already owns it · plus one seam case, `progression_kinds`, a roster that **resolves into the `Ruleset`**, which is the S1→S2 boundary and the reason quantities are ordinals while content is ids. **Three of the four kinds are shapes this project has already decided elsewhere.** |
| **D-75** | **THREE LEVELS, and conflating any two is this session's characteristic failure: the KIND is ours (engine, closed) · the RECORD is the feature's · the MEMBER is the author's.** Deciding the four kinds is L3-β's design work and it **collapses L2's validator estimate from 29 bespoke surfaces to four generic ones**, stops the structure accreting (a new feature declares a *member*, never adds a *field* — `D-25`'s two-channel rule at the manifest tier, and there is no third channel here either), and answers the addressing question **once per kind** instead of 29 times. ⚠ **Honest limit:** the classification is derived from field names plus the few reject rules referencing them — **a proposal over 29 data points, not a verified taxonomy.** Confirming each against its claiming feature doc is cheap and **not yet done**. |
| **D-76** | **`O-92` — the addressing seam between the two pinned artifacts, which nothing states today: QUANTITIES are addressed by ORDINAL (dense, inside the hashed bytes, never reused); CONTENT is addressed by OPAQUE ID (sparse, referential, validated by existence).** Measured from the corpus's own reject rules — `CanonicalActorDecl.spawn_cell` and `.glossary_entity_id` reference content **by id**, not by index. ⇒ `T0-4`'s never-reuse generalisation **stops at the ruleset boundary and does not follow content into the manifest**, which is what makes L3-β plumbing rather than new invariant design. |

### 4n. THE LINE, DRAWN COMPLETELY — what actor core has vs what the author defines (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-77** | **The answer takes THREE columns, not two — `D-75` restated as the reference.** **KIND** (engine, closed) fixes the operations, the arithmetic and the storage shape · **RECORD** (the feature) fixes what one declaration of that kind contains · **MEMBER** (the author) fixes which ones exist and their values. **Actor core HAS: the numbers and the operations over them** — `id` (permanent, never reused) · `RulesPin` · `values[32]` ordinal-indexed · `granted` (absence is structural, not a zero) · `threshold_active` (edge state — a crossing is `prev != now`) · `status_active` (a projection; a law asks *is it stunned*, never *why*) · `control` (a cache) · tier × existence × residency; and the fold · the delta pipeline · threshold detection · propose→adjudicate→apply · the bounded wave · phases 0-6 · the slot table · `commit_with_modifiers` · ordinal never-reuse · the lifecycle machine. **The AUTHOR defines:** which quantities exist and their kind · floor/base/ceiling-binding/regen-shape/at-floor per quantity · which thresholds with what hysteresis · which statuses with what stack policy · which lifecycle states, legal transitions, triggers, cascade policy and reason vocabulary · **which actor kinds exist and what each projects** (`D-13`) · absorption chains per damage kind · delta source→class assignment · the `ControlAuthority` vocabulary · budget values · **which quantity is `Vital`**. **The test for a new concern:** if the author would choose *from a set the engine defines*, it is vocabulary; if they would define *the set*, stop. |
| **D-78** | **⚠ `O-96` — my two specs state the modifier-layer boundary OPPOSITE ways, and the decision spec was wrong.** `DF07_001:170` locks the order (`DF7-A3`) and the dataflow spec cites *"`DF7-A3`'s locked order"* three times, while the decision spec §2 listed *"the layer names and their order"* as **author vocabulary**. **Resolved: engine-locked for V1, and the reason is arithmetic rather than convenience** — per-mille factors sum **within** a layer and do not chain **across** one, so an author inserting a layer changes arithmetic they cannot see; and it would open a **fifth** ordinal space for `O-65` to guard. **Openness is preserved**: an author who wants *"sect blessing"* declares a **modifier SOURCE inside an existing layer**, the same shape as a feature contributing to an ordinal the reality declared without owning one. **Corrected in the decision spec.** |
| **D-79** | **Two more contested rows resolved.** **`wave_budget.on_exceeded` is MECHANISM, engine-fixed `Refuse`** — `max_depth` stays vocabulary; §2.6.9's schema still offers `Truncate`, which `O-10` rejected outright and two reviewers found independently. The precedent is in the same document: §2.6.11 reclassified `residency_budget` **out** of the manifest by exactly this test. **`TierCapacityCaps` is not a conflict with `P-E`** — *how much of an actor we materialise* is invisible config, but *how many actors in this world have state* is player-visible (120 stateful NPCs and 1 000 are different worlds), so **the cap's existence is engine and its value is authored**. Both readings are right; the wording invites the confusion and the spec must say so. |
| **D-80** | **🔴 `O-97` — a FOURTH category the vocabulary/mechanism model has no slot for: ENGINE CAPABILITY WIDTH.** `MAX_DECLARED_QUANTITIES = 32` is **not vocabulary** (an author cannot move it) and **not properly mechanism** either — raising it is *"a code change that moves no existing digest"*, so it silently changes what an artifact means to a binary of a different width. **A property of the BINARY that an artifact must be checked against** has no home in `D-2`'s two-way split, and that is precisely why `O-64`'s failure surfaces as *"stored ruleset is not decodable"* — corruption — when the truth is *this binary is too narrow*. Naming the category is what makes `EngineTooNarrow` obviously required rather than a nicety. |
| **D-81** | **The one-sentence form, for the next reader: actor core has the NUMBERS and the OPERATIONS over them; the author declares WHICH numbers exist, what they are called, what shape they change by, and what it means when they cross a line. The engine never learns the word *qi*. The author never writes a loop.** |

### 4o. AUTHOR AGENTS — 4 genres, 166 blind wishes, measured (2026-08-02)

Four agents role-played excited authors of different genres. **Each wrote its wish list BEFORE reading a
single file** — reading the surface first would make them fit their wishes to what exists, a
self-confirming measurement. None was pointed at doc 35 §6.5. Full detail in spec §28.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-82** | **The measured expressiveness is ~13%, and the HOME GENRE scores LAST.** 修真 **11%** (4/38) · 武俠 **12%** (5/42) · political chronicle **12%** (5/43) · modern occult **16%** (7/43). Total **21 ✅ / 45 ⚠ / 100 ❌ of 166**. **This kills the "cultivation engine in disguise" reading**: the author with no qi, no realms and no soul expressed the *most*, and catalogued twelve cultivation artifacts that *"blocked not one wish"*. What the numbers actually say: this is a **declaration engine with no consequence layer**, and cultivation is the most **consequence-dense** genre — so the genre it was built for is the one the missing half hurts most. |
| **D-83** | **🔑 THE BUILD ORDER IS FIXED BY MY OWN SPEC AND I NEVER NOTICED.** §2.6.4's `TransitionDecl.trigger` is `OnStatus(StatusOrdinal) \| OnAdmin \| OnCascade` (verified, spec line 282). **A lifecycle transition triggers on a STATUS, so LIFECYCLE QUEUES BEHIND STATUSES** — razed, abandoned, refounded, annexed all wait on `threshold_sets` → `statuses`. That converts *"which first"* from a preference into a **dependency**, and it makes the threshold→status table not one improvement among several but **the keystone**. Found by the political-chronicle author; sitting unnoticed in my own §2.6.4 for twenty-eight sections. |
| **D-84** | **Two changes, each with FOUR independent arrivals, and their difference decides the sequence.** **① `threshold_sets` → `statuses`** — asked as #1 by 修真 and political, named as the largest ❌ block by the other two; **designed in §2.6.2/§2.6.3, zero code** ⇒ it is *work*. **② A SIGNED arrow from a declared quantity into a ceiling/slot** — wuxia #1, 修真 #2, occult #1 (as signedness), **and the multi-reality red team independently from code alone** (`CeilingBinding::Quantity`, `O-71`); `CeilingBinding` is two variants and `rate_factor_milli` is `u32` ⇒ it is a **reversal**. **A reversal is cheap now and expensive once content exists; work costs the same whenever it happens.** So take ②'s decision now even if its code lands later, and build ① first because the largest surface — and lifecycle — is behind it. |
| **D-85** | **🔴 `granted` has ZERO occurrences in `crates/`, so `D-4` is a drawing.** §3 puts `granted: u32` at the centre of the actor and §3.3 rests *"a village has no hp stays representable"* entirely on it — *"absence is STRUCTURAL, not a zero"*. It does not exist. And `resolve_block` loops `for slot in StatSlot::ALL` **unconditionally** (`resolve.rs:73`), so anything reaching stat resolution carries all ten combat slots — a famine with `MaxHp 100` and `CritMult 1500`. **The claim I asserted most confidently about the actor is the one with no field behind it**, and the agent sent specifically to test it is the one who measured that. |
| **D-86** | **What survived four hostile authors, recorded because it is evidence and not reassurance.** **`D-5`** — all four read `ZeroBehaviour`'s missing `Defeat`, all four found the refusal message explaining it, **none called it a gap**; the political author called it *"a genuinely good decision"*. **§5.8's axis split** — verified from outside as the right model for *razed → refounded* (tier-2 `Stateful` keeps an edge out of a terminal state, `ActorId` never reused). **The refusal discipline** — praised independently by all four: *"I have used engines that would have accepted all three and let me discover the truth in playtesting six weeks later."* **And two real manifests, ADMITTED with digests** (15/32 and 11/32 ordinals, zero findings) — the first end-to-end evidence this session that the authoring path works at all. |
| **D-87** | **Keep ~13% as the standing measure.** It is the number to hold against every future expressiveness claim, and the cheapest available test of whether the verb layer actually moved: **re-run the same four blind wish lists after threshold→status ships.** Also keep both admitted manifests as fixtures — with `PGN-A8`'s warning attached, that a fixture already containing the answers makes every downstream stage vacuous. |

### 4p. RE-SCORED BY OWNER — the denominator was wrong (PO 2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-88** | **`D-82`'s ~13% was measured against the WRONG DENOMINATOR, and it is a category error I had been correcting everywhere else.** §26.5 established that features do not own manifest structure; `D-24` moved the relational family out entirely; `D-75` split kind/record/member. **Then I scored 166 wishes spanning the whole game against ONE feature's declaration surface and reported it as a measure of that feature.** A natal treasure is an **item** wish; master-disciple qi transfer is a **relational** wish that `D-24` handed off by the PO's own test. **Corrected: within actor core's own scope, ≈116 wishes, 21 ✅ = 18% clean, 66 ✅+⚠ = 57% partial.** Roughly half the 100 ❌ were never actor core's to answer. |
| **D-89** | **The correction moves the denominator and leaves the DIAGNOSIS intact — which is why it is a correction and not a defence.** Actor core's own ~50 ❌ are still dominated by the same two things all four authors named: **statuses/thresholds** and **the signed arrow**. `D-83`'s keystone (lifecycle queues behind statuses, because `TransitionDecl.trigger` is `OnStatus`) and `D-84`'s sequencing both stand unchanged. |
| **D-90** | **The 166 wishes are re-filed as a REQUIREMENTS CORPUS for nine features (spec §29.4).** `PL_007` items ~18 · `P-6` relational ~22 · `ABL_001` abilities ~10 · `FAC_001` factions ~8 · `COMB_001` combat ~6 · `PF_001` place/overlays ~7 · `FF_001` family ~5 · `CFT` crafting ~4 · world scope ~4 · cross-feature ~12. **Each of those features would otherwise start from a blank page**; this is genre-diverse input written by authors who did not know what was easy, which a design round cannot generate for itself. ⚠ **`PGN-A8` applies:** they are **expectations, not specifications** — *"an author once asked for it"* is not a reason to build, and a corpus that already contains the answers makes every downstream design vacuous. |
| **D-91** | **Two expectations have NO owning feature at all, and nothing currently decides whether they are buildable.** A **world-scoped quantity** (天道 pressure rising as cultivators ascend, making tribulations harsher for everyone) and a **group-held quantity** (per-sect contribution points — the political author hit the same wall from the other side). Both need a **quantity holder that is not an actor**. §4.5.4's *"the environment is a quantity holder too"* names the idea and nothing stands behind it. Either such a holder exists, or both are permanently inexpressible (`O-118`). |
| **D-92** | **The classification in §29.2 is a JUDGEMENT CALL on about a dozen borderline rows and is stated so it can be disputed.** A breakthrough gated on *"a pill AND a tribulation"* is one wish touching three features; those went to **cross-feature** rather than being silently assigned. Anyone re-running this should re-classify rather than inherit my split. |

### 4q. CLOSING SWEEP — three rows close on evidence in hand (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-93** | **`O-118` closes: the PO assigned the vocabulary, and the mechanism resolves without new machinery.** **[CORRECTED by `D-171`: measured from DESIGN DOCS, not code - `ActorKind` has ZERO hits in `crates/`.]** Measured `ActorKind = Pc \| Npc \| Synthetic \| Locus` — no `Group`. **天道 world pressure: a World IS a locus** (`SPG-A1` — a locus is an entity; doc 36's tree runs `Universe · World · Region`), so a world-scoped quantity is an ordinary actor quantity on the World locus-actor, and the **cultivation system** names it. **Per-sect contribution points are NOT group-held at all** — each member has their own score *with* that sect, so it is per-`(actor, faction)`: a **pair-keyed relation**, `D-17`'s shape and §26.9's kind ②, owned by the **social system**. **Residue, narrowed not dismissed (`O-118b`):** a sect **treasury** *is* held by the group as such, and a faction is neither a place nor a person — one question for the social system, with a stated shape to answer against. |
| **D-94** | **`O-60` closes — the columnar-vs-`size_of` tension DISSOLVES at the real scale.** AoS was measured at **11× penalty at 65 536 residents**; the hard stateful population cap is **120 per reality** (`TierCapacityCaps`). At 120 the columnar benefit is unmeasurable and the `size_of` gate is `D-26`'s only mechanical defence against field accretion. **Keep AoS, keep the gate. Revisit trigger: a stateful cap above ~10 000 per island**, which is where the reviewer's own table first shows a ratio worth paying for. |
| **D-95** | **`O-59` closes — modifier rows are L1-RESIDENT**, projected alongside the quantity block and bounded by `T0-3`'s per-actor cardinality cap. An L2 fetch would be one cross-layer read per actor per tick — forbidden by §4.7.6 and priced at ~65 ms/tick at 65 k. **Consequence: `T0-3` is load-bearing for the MEMORY MODEL, not only for accretion** — `O-54`'s *"216 B × residents"* becomes *"(quantity block + capped modifier rows) × residents"*, and the cap is what makes that a number instead of an unknown. |
| **D-96** | **`O-62` closes — a TURN is an INPUT admitted into a tick; it is not a tick.** The phase runner's tick is wall-clock; fiction time advances in declared increments, exactly as `PL_001`'s fiction clock and fast-forward chain already work, and `ingress.rs` already stamps `Seq` at admission with validation deferred to step time. ⇒ **tick rate does not scale with player count**, which was the failure the compute reviewer named, and `D-19`'s two clocks stay separate: simulation LOD is *how often we compute* (config, invisible), fiction dilation is *how much in-world time passes* (hashed rules, visible). **Neither is the player's turn.** |
| **D-97** | **The spec is CLOSEABLE, and the remaining list is short.** Decided and written: 96 decisions, the engine/author line drawn completely (§27), four manifest declaration kinds (§26.9), the layer plan with exit criteria (§25). Measured rather than asserted: `size_of` three ways (none matching the spec), the nine-hop chain, 9 SLIs with 0 instruments, `granted` = 0 occurrences, 166 blind author wishes, two **admitted** manifests with digests. **Still open: 4 rows needing a decision or a measurement, ~15 build items, 1 handed to `P-6`, 1 to the social system. AWAITING THE PO: exactly one — `O-107`, the signed arrow.** |

**Drift `DR-15`:** §5.8.2 said demotion *"destroys canon"* while `D-23` says a tier-2 row is a fold over the
ledger. **I recorded the clash as `ST-6` and then left the paragraph standing for twenty sections.** Fixed
in place. The lesson is not *record contradictions* — I did — it is that **a recorded contradiction that is
not applied is indistinguishable from an unnoticed one**, and the register made me feel finished.

### 4r. FEATURE-COUPLING REVIEW — clearing the path before feature #2 (PO 2026-08-02)

PO: *a ruleset that is not per-feature was wrong from the beginning. Actor is the first feature and then
there are N more — clear the path NOW, so implementing one feature later does not mean editing the whole
system.* Full sweep in spec §31.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-98** | **THE DISCRIMINATOR, and without it `D-2` is unfalsifiable.** *"The engine closes on mechanism"* can be claimed after the fact for any closure. The test: **a closed set is MECHANISM if the engine's arithmetic DIFFERS PER MEMBER; it is A FEATURE'S VOCABULARY IN COSTUME if the engine treats members UNIFORMLY and only one feature knows their names.** Applied, it **clears** `ModifierOp` · `ZeroBehaviour` · `RegenType` · `CapRule` · `CurveKind` · `ProgressionType` (arithmetic genuinely differs per member) and **convicts** `StatSlot` and `CombatRules`-as-a-`Ruleset`-field. Belongs in the standards index. |
| **D-99** | **🔴 `Ruleset` is a god struct with one field per feature — and the fix is already in the same file, applied once.** `progression: Option<ProgressionDigest>` is content-addressed and optional; `combat`, `stats`, `quantities`, `resources` are **inlined**. `PGN-R1` found the pattern under pressure (*"inlining is structurally impossible, not merely large"*) and nobody generalised it. **`size_of::<Ruleset>() <= 2344` with 2312 measured = 32 bytes of headroom** — `D-26` calls this shape a *gate*, and here it is a **countdown**. Live consequences: **combat is not declinable** (`O-114`), and **one `law_version` for all laws means a combat balance patch moves EVERY reality's digest, including realities with no combat.** ⇒ **`C-1`: `Ruleset` becomes a SET of per-feature parts, each content-addressed, `law_version` per part.** The mechanism ships — `store.rs` already does exactly this for progression, digest-verified on read. |
| **D-100** | **🔴 `StatSlot` is COMBAT'S vocabulary living in the engine core — measured, not argued.** `resolve_block` iterates `StatSlot::ALL` **uniformly**; the enum is used **by name in exactly 12 sites, every one of them combat** (`combat/stats.rs:49-55`, `resolve.rs:131`); `SLOT_COUNT` has a **25-site** blast radius. `D-10` opened `StatSlot` because *"stats are vocabulary"* — this establishes **whose**. It is the wall all four authors hit (a granary's capacity must be called `max_stamina`), and moving it into combat's part is what gives `O-107`'s signed arrow somewhere useful to point. ⇒ **`C-2`.** |
