| **D-184** `[E]` | **How the frame re-reads the session's findings.** `O-121` *actor core is doing engine core's job* -> **restated correctly: it is doing PLUGINS' jobs**; the split is **hub vs plugin**, and *engine core* is the hub's substrate. `C-1` `Ruleset` -> per-feature parts -> **a part IS a plugin's ruleset**; `C-1` was rediscovering the plugin boundary from the data side. `C-2` -> **shrinks** (`D-182`). `C-3` -> **this IS the split, and now it has a NAME and a TEST** instead of a boundary drawn by feel. `D-160`'s leak -> **obviously combat's**; without the frame the leak was inevitable. `C-9` no ownership feature -> **a missing PLUGIN, which the frame PREDICTS**: money is external, so it must have a plugin, and there is none. |
| **D-185** `[E]` | **Take the FRAME, keep OUR EXECUTION - stated so this is not a reversal of 36-43's measurements.** **TAKE:** hub + plugin · `contribute()` · intrinsic-only actor · the attachment list · *"combat-core is a hub, not an implementation"*. **LEAVE:** chained multiplication (`value *= c.value`, order-dependent, patched with a deterministic sort) · `f64` **where a digest exists** · `dimensions.yaml` as a flat god-list with **zero readers** · **`CapMode` declared twice** with different variant sets · 34 of 40 configs with no consumer. **Neither project won. The PO's frame plus our arithmetic beats either alone**, and saying so is more useful than defending forty sections. |
| **D-186** `[E]` | **THE SCOPE, WRITTEN DOWN - what was missing on day one.** **`ACTOR CORE = THE HUB`:** identity (`EntityId`) · **INTRINSIC quantities only** · lifecycle / existence / residency · **the attached plugin list** · **THE FOLD**, which aggregates contributions and **knows nothing about what they mean**. **`PLUGINS`:** **combat** reads the actor and derives its own stats · **cultivation** contributes progression, tiers, breakthrough · **ownership** holds money and items, **never in the actor** (`C-9` - no folder exists) · **social** holds relations, rank, reputation, **never in the actor** · **ability** holds techniques and their costs. **AND THE RULE THAT WOULD HAVE PREVENTED EVERY `D-174` LEAK: if a datum would not travel with a being stripped naked into another world, it does not belong in the actor - however convenient it is to put it there.** |

Under the plugin frame that stops being an aspiration and becomes CHECKABLE, because a plugin that cannot add a field has nothing to touch.** |

### 5l. REFOCUS - the item round is a PLUGIN, reconciled later; the hub becomes buildable (PO 2026-08-02)

PO: *ownership was just created by another agent - I asked it to reference the actor data structure. Let us
focus on OUR work: this session is still building the FIRST feature; the other features are plugins and get
reconciled later.*

|---|---|
| **D-198** `[META]` | **The item round is DOWNSTREAM and reconciliation is DEFERRED BY THE PO.** `D-195`'s factual correction stands - `C-9` was wrong, the home exists, and the error was mine (asserting a directory's contents without listing it). **But the alarm was disproportionate:** a parallel agent was asked to reference the actor data structure, which is the intended flow, and **a plugin building on a snapshot of the hub is normal, not a crisis**. The handoff section is compressed to a note naming the gap for whoever reconciles. **This session's subject is the FIRST feature.** |
| **D-199** `[E]` | **🔴 The hub spec was a BOUNDARY STATEMENT, not a buildable spec - now fixed.** It said *"the dense ordinal block"* and stopped. Added: the concrete shape, the attachment set's form, and the contribution seam. **`ActorHub { id: EntityId (8 B) · quantities: [i32; MAX] (128 B) · existence (~3 B) · attached: PluginSet (4 B) }`.** |
| **D-200** `[E]` | **The stored value stays `i32` even though quantities now carry a scale - because `Scaled` is a COMPUTATION type, not a STORAGE type.** A quantity's **domain is declared once in the ruleset and does not vary per actor**, so the actor stores one `i32` per ordinal and the declaration says how to read it: **Linear** -> the value · **Log** -> the milli-log magnitude · **a pool's `current`** -> a permille of its capacity, where the capacity is **a separate quantity**. ⇒ **storage stays 128 B and `D-26`'s anti-accretion gate survives.** A per-actor `[Scaled; 32]` would have been **256 B to carry a number identical for every actor holding that quantity - a per-entity copy of a per-ruleset fact**, which is exactly the second-SSOT shape `D-188` removed. |
| **D-201** `[E]` | **`PluginSet` - a bitmask over PLUGIN ORDINALS, assigned once and never reused**, under the same law the quantity ordinals live by, for the same reason: a renumbering silently redefines every stored value. **And `granted` becomes DERIVABLE rather than stored: `granted = OR over attached plugins of their declared-quantity mask`.** Quantity `q` is present exactly when the plugin declaring `q` is attached - a village has no hp because combat is not attached; a stone has no qi because cultivation is not. **Nothing must be kept in step, because there is only one fact.** **Width stated rather than assumed:** a `u32` holds 32 plugins - **a ceiling to CHOOSE, not a truth**; the ordinal law makes raising it later an engine release that moves no digest, while lowering it is impossible. **Deliberately not decided here.** |
| **D-202** `[E]` | **THE CONTRIBUTION SEAM - the whole plugin contract, and it is two row types.** `ModifierRow { target: QuantityOrdinal, op, value, source, layer }` · `DerivationRow { target, source: QuantityOrdinal, op, factor_milli, floor, ceil }`. **Rules:** a contribution is **DATA, never executable logic** (a plugin cannot run inside the fold, and the fold cannot run inside a plugin) · a row addresses an **ordinal, never a name** · a conditional row's condition is a **declared threshold**, never a predicate grammar (*a grammar is code wearing a data costume*) · **the hub never inspects `source` or `layer` semantically** - it orders by them and nothing else, and **if the hub ever needs to know what a source MEANS, a plugin's vocabulary has leaked into it.** ⇒ **`D-30`'s acceptance test made concrete: a new plugin ships ROWS and a DECLARATION. If it needs anything else from the hub, the boundary is wrong.** |

### 5m. `Existence` and LAYER ORDER - the last two gaps, and both SHRINK the hub (PO 2026-08-02)

PO: *ok, do it.* Spec: hub 3.3 + 3.8.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-203** `[E]` | **`Existence` IS ALREADY SHIPPED and it is called `GoneState` - measured before designing anything.** `crates/dp-kernel/src/entity_status.rs:28`, **51 code sites**, mirrored in `contracts/entity_status/gone_state.go`, with a precedence lattice and `is_live()`/`is_terminal()`: **`Active \| Severed \| Archived \| Dropped \| UserErased`**. **And the important part is what it is NOT: it answers *"is this row reachable?"* - moved between realities, reality archived, hard delete, GDPR erasure. It is PLATFORM existence.** **Fiction existence - alive or dead - is NOT here and must not come here: death is a STATUS, owned by the status plugin. Two beings can both be `GoneState::Active` while one is a corpse.** ⇒ **nothing to design**; confusing the two would have put a fiction concept into a field that a GDPR erasure writes. |
| **D-204** `[E]` | **And the materialisation tier and residency are NOT the hub's - they are the substrate's.** Measured at **zero code occurrences** (`Untracked`/`Tracked`/`Minor` have no hits), and by the strip-naked boundary they belong to the layer that already owns the storage tiers. ***Where a being's state lives* is not *what a being is*.** **The hub shrinks again**: the existence axis is one shipped enum, and two of the three axes earlier drafts assigned to it leave. |
| **D-205** `[E]` | **🔴 THE LAST `StatSlot`-SHAPED THING, AND IT IS IN THE FOLD: `ModifierSource = Base \| Archetype \| Progression \| Equipment \| Status \| Lex` names THREE PLUGINS inside the engine's closed enum.** Applied the discriminator against `resolve.rs:82-88`: the loop runs **identical arithmetic for every member** and only the **ORDER** differs; percent is summed with **no source filter at all**. **`Lex` is genuine mechanism** - it has its own code path (a separate `lex_clamps` parameter and `intersect_clamps`, applied last and inescapable). **`Base` and `Archetype` are the hub's own.** **`Progression`, `Equipment`, `Status` are plugin names in the engine.** ⇒ **`ModifierRow.layer: u8` - a LAYER ORDINAL, ordering only, declared, never reused.** The hub guarantees exactly two things: **a stable total order by layer ordinal**, and **one distinguished final layer that clamps inescapably**. Which layers exist and what they are called is **declared**. |
| **D-206** `[E]` | **The defect this must not reintroduce - and why the OPEN form is SAFER here, which refines `D-109`.** `XST-D7` records a real one: `resolve_block` iterated an inline `[Progression, Equipment, Status]` literal, so `Base`, `Archetype` and `Lex` **flat** modifiers were constructible, accepted **and silently discarded**, while percent was not source-filtered at all - **a world rule worked or did nothing depending on which operator the author picked.** Its lesson: *"the enum must not be able to express something the resolver ignores."* **Declared layers make that structurally impossible, because the resolver iterates THE SAME LIST that declaration writes - there is no second hand-maintained list to drift from.** **And this REFINES `D-109`:** the prior project's flat global stat list was *worse* open than closed - **but only because NOTHING READ IT**; the compiler lost its ability to notice and nothing replaced it. ⇒ **The rule is not *open versus closed*. It is whether the READER and the WRITER share one list.** A declared layer list has exactly one reader, the fold, and that is what makes opening it safe. |

### 5n. SEAL + THE VERIFICATION PROTOCOL - an independent agent per layer (PO 2026-08-02)

PO: *seal it, then update the run state. An important note this time: there will be a QUALITY AND COVERAGE
verification slice at EVERY LAYER of the game system. Deploy an INDEPENDENT AGENT to verify and report back
to the main session to fix bugs. `/goal` cannot act as a verifier, so we FORCE the process - the session
verifies itself by deploying an independent, adversarial agent.*

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-207** `[META]` | **The PO's diagnosis is exactly right, and CLAUDE.md already states the hole: the `/goal` evaluator READS THE TRANSCRIPT ONLY - it cannot run a command or read a file, so it is satisfied by the agent CLAIMING a check passed.** *"`/goal` enforces persistence, not honesty."* The existing layers are `/goal` (talkable-past), the RUN-STATE file (survives compaction, not talkable-past but not a verifier either), and the rule-based scripts (mechanical but narrow - and `D-154` measured `design-lint` unable to even scan `docs/specs/`). ⇒ **A FOURTH LAYER: an INDEPENDENT COLD-START AGENT per build layer.** Its isolation IS the product - it cannot see the reasoning that produced the artifact, so it cannot inherit the reasoning's blind spot. |
| **D-208** `[META]` | **🔴 THE EMPIRICAL CASE IS THIS SESSION'S OWN DRIFT LOG, and the number is the argument.** Six recorded drifts: `DR-17` (a `StatSlot` measurement classified by FILENAME rather than by what the call did) · `DR-18` (`ModifierRow` cited as the template with **zero** code occurrences) · `DR-19` (a WITHIN-realm golden vector used to settle a CROSS-realm question) · `DR-20` (a design-doc reading presented as *"measured"*) · `DR-21` (the scope discipline written in `D-156` and broken by `D-160`, two sections later) · `DR-22` (asserting a directory's contents without listing it - `docs/specs/`, the directory being written into all session). **Attribution: THREE caught by the PO, TWO caught by me on a later pass, ONE caught by accident. ZERO caught at the moment of writing.** **Self-review at the moment of authorship has a measured hit rate of zero in this round**, which is why the mechanism must be an agent that did not write the thing. |
| **D-209** `[META]` | **`VS-n` - A VERIFICATION SLICE IS ADDED TO EVERY LAYER `L0`..`L5`, and a layer is NOT done until its `VS` returns clean.** The slice is not a review; it is an adversarial refutation pass with a **structured return contract**, so findings are actionable rather than narrative. **A layer whose `VS` was skipped is UNBUILT for planning purposes**, exactly as `D-30`'s acceptance test treats a plugin that touches actor core. |
| **D-210** `[META]` | **THE AGENT CONTRACT - six rules, each derived from a measured failure rather than from policy.** ① **Cold start**: the agent reads the ARTIFACT and the CODE. **The derivation record and the RUN-STATE are the author's own reasoning and are NOT evidence** - if a contract claims something about code, it is verified in code. ② **Refute mandate, with `REFUTED` as the default under uncertainty** - an unverifiable claim is a finding. ③ **`file:line` required** - a finding without a citation is not a finding. ④ **Re-measure every numeric claim** rather than accepting it. ⑤ **Grep every code identifier the artifact names** and report any with zero non-docs hits. ⑥ **The report must include a `CLAIMS I TRIED TO BREAK AND COULD NOT` section with a minimum count** - *because a report that finds nothing is indistinguishable from a report that looked at nothing*, which is the non-vacuity standard applied to the verifier itself. |
| **D-211** `[META]` | **THE CHECKLIST IS THIS SESSION'S FAILURE MODES, NOT A GENERIC RUBRIC.** Five hunts, each with a real occurrence behind it: **① phantom citation** - a named type/field/constant with zero code occurrences (`granted`, `ModifierRow`, `CausalityWaitTimeout`) · **② scope-of-evidence gap** - a measurement that does not cover the claim it supports (`DR-19`, `DR-22`) · **③ vacuous gate** - a check cited as evidence whose scope excludes the thing it supposedly verified (`D-154`) · **④ second SSOT** - one datum with two owners (`D-188` found three already inside the struct) · **⑤ boundary leak** - a decision stated inside another feature's territory (`D-174` found four). **A generic *look for bugs* rubric would have caught none of these**, which is the point. |
| **D-212** `[META]` | **THE LOOP, and the one rule that keeps it honest: RE-VERIFY WITH A NEW AGENT.** report -> main session fixes -> **a FRESH agent re-verifies**. **Never the same agent**, because an agent that has seen the fix has seen the reasoning behind it and has lost the isolation that was the entire product. **Terminate on `K` consecutive clean rounds, not on one** - a single clean round is as likely to mean the agent tired as that the artifact is sound (`K = 2` unless the layer is load-bearing). **And the loop is bounded**: findings whose fix is out of the layer's scope are PARKED with a named trigger rather than fixed, so the loop cannot be held open by work that belongs elsewhere. |
| **D-213** `[META]` | **COVERAGE is the second axis, and it is measured the way this repo measures deferrals: MECHANISED versus PROSE-ONLY.** For each claim in a layer's spec the verifier reports whether it is **enforced by something that can fail** (a test, an assertion, a gate, a validator) or **stated only in prose**. **A prose-only claim is not a defect - it is an untracked one**, and the repo's own history says untracked prose is what rots: nine of nineteen deferrals in one measured day were prose-only, and the same defect shipped twenty-seven times with **not one caught by the test suite**. **The verifier's coverage table is what makes prose-only claims visible at the moment they are written.** |
| **D-214** `[META]` | **FIRST INSTANCE RUN AT SEAL TIME, not deferred to `L0`.** The three contracts were handed to a cold-start adversarial verifier **before** this seal, on the reasoning that a protocol described but not exercised is `D-177`'s *intent is not a mechanism* one more time. **The seal is therefore conditional on that report** - findings are folded in, and anything that survives is recorded rather than quietly dropped. |

### 5p. `V-SEAL` ROUND 2 - the design, and its stated limit

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-222** `[META]` | **Round 2 uses TWO LENSES, not a repeat - because a repeat measures agent variance, not artifact quality.** Round 1 hunted **citation and measurement** defects and found 22. Re-running the same mandate would mostly re-find the same class. ⇒ **`V-A` IMPLEMENTABILITY** - a senior Rust engineer assigned to BUILD the contracts, whose finding is *"here is where I cannot proceed without guessing"*, forced to name the exact missing decision (a width, a bound, a tie-break, an error case, an ordering) · **`V-B` LOGICAL VALIDITY** - an auditor who does not care whether the design is good, only whether each conclusion follows from its premise, and who classifies **every** *must/never/always/is refused* as **MECHANISED or PROSE-ONLY**, which is `D-213`'s coverage axis actually executed. **Both also carry a light phantom sweep**, because that class recurred six times. |
| **D-223** `[META]` | **Both are told the corrections are marked in place, and NEITHER is told what round 1 found.** The `V-SEAL/F-n` tags are discoverable **from the artifact**, exactly as a reader would find them - so fix-verification is possible **without anchoring**. Being handed round 1's conclusions would have destroyed the isolation that is the entire product (`D-212`), and a fix written under pressure is precisely where over-correction and new unsupported assertions collect, so **the corrections are the part to audit hardest**, not the part to take on trust. |
| **D-224** `[META]` | **🔴 THE MECHANISM'S STATED LIMIT: two agents drawn from the SAME MODEL have CORRELATED BLIND SPOTS.** Independence here is **isolation from MY reasoning**, which is real and is what caught all 22 - it is **not** statistical independence. **A defect that this model class does not perceive will survive any number of rounds**, and adding agents raises confidence **sub-linearly** rather than converging. ⇒ **`K` consecutive clean rounds is a STOPPING RULE, not a proof**, and the honest ceiling on what `V-SEAL` can certify is *"nothing this model, cold, could find in these documents"* - **not** *"nothing is wrong."* **Recorded so the next reader does not mistake a clean round for correctness**, which is the same error `D-154` made about a green gate. |

| **D-226** `[META]` | **🔴 AND I CITED LINE NUMBERS THAT DO NOT EXIST.** `F-20` pointed at `resource/table.rs:229-233` and `:242-247`; **the file is 238 lines.** The checks are real and the quoted message is verbatim - they are at **`:89-108`**. `F-17` likewise cites `progression/mod.rs:194` for `tier_max`, which is at **`:193`** (`:194` is `within_tier_curve`), and misses a fifth `u64` at `:197`. **The phantom class has now appeared as a phantom TYPE, a phantom MEASUREMENT, and a phantom LINE NUMBER** - and the third is the one hardest to catch by reading, because a wrong line number in a correct claim looks exactly like a right one. |
| **D-227** `[META]` | **🔴 `Scaled` IS NOT IMPLEMENTABLE AS SPECIFIED - five of its six operations are underspecified or arithmetically broken.** **`compare`** ranks canonical values backwards (`I-1`). **`add` same-scale** has no canonical result when the `i64` sum exceeds `i32` and is not divisible by 10 (`I-2`: `{2_000_000_001,0} + {147_483_647,0}` ends in 8). **`add` different-scale** requires dividing by `10^(Δ/1000)`, which for `Δ = 1` is **irrational** - milli-log scale is not quantised to decades and no second table is specified (`I-3`). **`multiply`** overflows identically and the exactness ledger says only `add` is inexact (`I-5`). **`band_delta`'s *"small correction from the values"* is a LOGARITHM** with no table, no rounding mode and no bound - and it is the single primitive four laws key on (`I-6`). **The canonical form's *"no leading-zero scale"* rule also points the wrong direction** - the rewrite drives `scale` UP, so its stopping condition is a ceiling, not the stated floor, and no scale bound exists anywhere (`I-4`). |
| **D-228** `[META]` | **🔴 MY FIX FOR A NON-VACUITY FINDING IS ITSELF VACUOUS.** `F-21` correctly diagnosed that a declared layer list leaves `closed-set-gate.py`'s coverage (verified: 407 files scanned, gate bites on `--self-test`, its regex requires `const NAME: [Enum; _]`). **The prerequisite I then specified - *"every declared layer is reachable by the resolver"* - CANNOT FAIL once the resolver iterates the declared list**, which is my own argument two paragraphs earlier. **A check with no failing input, written as the remedy for a coverage gap, in a repo whose LOCKED standard is that a check which cannot fail is worse than none.** The agent supplied the non-vacuous form: *every `ModifierRow.layer` present in submitted rows names a DECLARED layer* - which **can** fail, when a plugin ships a row for a layer it forgot to declare. |
| **D-229** `[E + F:18_combat]` | **🔴 A LIVE SHIPPED DEFECT NEITHER I NOR ROUND 1 FOUND: `derive_move_range` SETS, and silently discards every modifier on `MoveRange`.** `resolve.rs:73-117` resolves `MoveRange` like every other slot - archetype, six ordered flat layers, summed percent, both clamp passes - and writes it at `:116`. Then `:130` calls `derive_move_range`, whose last statement is `block.rs:89` **`self.set(StatSlot::MoveRange, …)`: an unconditional overwrite.** ⇒ **An Equipment `Flat(+2)` on `MoveRange` is constructible, accepted, and silently dropped TODAY.** That is `XST-D7`'s exact signature, surviving in **the one slot `C-6` nominates as its first `DerivationRow`** - and the contract never says whether a `DerivationRow` **SETS or CONTRIBUTES**, so it cannot even express which behaviour it means (`I-17`). |

A later layer wins."*** That is a **ruleset-composition** layer with an ascending fold order; hub 3.8 introduces a **modifier-ordering** layer with an ascending fold order, unqualified. **And this document opens by explaining that it renamed itself `engine-substrate` because *"an unscoped title would have been one name for two concepts."*** Same defect, same round, two hours apart. |

The same 56 against shipped code with pinned realities would be a data migration plus every author's manifest.** **The verification found the bugs in the layer nothing has reached yet - which is the only place finding them is cheap.** |

### 5t. WHAT "BLOCKED" MEANS - the PO's challenge, and my word was WRONG

> **PO:** *let me make this precise. What counts as BLOCKED? Something that cannot be realised because the
> technology does not exist? Because the knowledge does not exist - an algorithm nobody has written? A
> theorem that needs proving?*

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-247** `[META]` | **🔴 MY WORD WAS WRONG, AND THE PROOF IS THE PO'S VERY NEXT QUESTION.** CLAUDE.md's LOCKED anti-laziness rule says *"missing infrastructure is NOT blocked - it is unbuilt work to implement"*, and that **only a dependency EXTERNAL to this repo that you cannot write** clears gate #4. I used *"blocker"* to mean **blocks the seal** - a **process** state - while it reads as **cannot be done**. **And that misreading is exactly what happened: the PO's next message asked whether the design was unbuildable.** A word that produces the wrong question in one turn is the wrong word. |
| **D-248** `[META]` | **🔴 CHECKED ALL 14: ZERO ARE BLOCKED IN THE PO'S SENSE. Not one needs a nonexistent algorithm, an unproven theorem, or unavailable technology.** The tempting one is the opposite of blocked: **`Scaled`'s comparison and addition are SOLVED PROBLEMS from the 1960s** - every arbitrary-precision decimal library normalises to a canonical form (`1 <= |value| < 10^k`, sign, then exponent, then mantissa) and quantises the exponent to **decades**, not to milli-units. **Mine broke because I invented a representation instead of using the known one**, which is the sharpest possible form of *not blocked, just wrong*. Likewise `band_delta`'s *"logarithm"*: integer `ilog10` is in Rust's std, and a small mantissa table gives milli-log deterministically. And `exp10_table` is generated offline in exact rational arithmetic, rounded half-even, checked into source with a regeneration test - the same shape as any trig table ever shipped. |
| **D-249** `[META]` | **THE TAXONOMY I SHOULD HAVE USED, and the classification of all 56.** **BLOCKED** - external to this repo and unwritable here (an unproven theorem, a nonexistent algorithm, unavailable technology, another team's unreleased work): **0 of 56.** **WRONG** - I asserted something false; the fix is to correct it: **~20** (`F-10`'s canonical form, `F-17`'s `i64`-cannot-hold-`u64`, citations past end-of-file, the 8/14 contradiction, the `Layer` collision, the self-sealing sentence, the scope test's stated domain, four miscounts). **UNDECIDED** - a choice nobody has taken: **~10** (`op`'s variant set, SET-versus-CONTRIBUTE for a derivation, `PluginSet`'s width, whether a third domain member exists, the canonical filler for a valueless ceiling, free-versus-absorbed, the verb for *cost exceeds current*). **UNWRITTEN** - work that must be done and CAN be done here: **~26** (the clamp row, the Log fold, `exp10_table`, `base(q)`/`floor(q)`'s source, `band_delta`'s integer algorithm, the migration, and the ~23 prose rules that need gates). **Every one of the last three classes is work. None of it is a wall.** |
| **D-250** `[META]` | **The rule I will apply from here, so the word cannot drift again.** ***BLOCKED* is reserved for a dependency this repo cannot write.** Everything else gets its true name: **WRONG** (correct it), **UNDECIDED** (take the decision), **UNWRITTEN** (build it). **And a finding that stops a SEAL is not a blocker - it is a `SEAL-STOP`**, which is a statement about a document's readiness and says nothing about whether the thing can be built. **The two were fused in one word, and the fusion cost a round of alarm.** |

### 5u. DEEP DIVE + DECISIONS - the UNDECIDED class, cleared

> **PO:** *so there are no blocks here at all. Wrong -> fix it. Undecided -> deep dive, analyse, decide.
> Unwritten -> put it in the run state and write a spec for it.*

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-251** `[E]` | **🔴 THE DEEP DIVE DISSOLVES SEVEN FINDINGS AT ONCE: `Scaled` CONFLATED A REPRESENTATION QUESTION WITH A DOMAIN QUESTION, AND LEAVES THE ENGINE SUBSTRATE ENTIRELY.** `D-169` collapsed pool, money and cultivation magnitude into one type on the grounds that all three are *(bounded value, log scale)*. **Re-examined against what each actually needs:** a **pool's `current`** is a **permille `i32`**, Linear, and never spans decades · a pool's **capacity** is **a separate quantity ordinal**, itself Linear or Log `i32` · **cultivation magnitude** is a **milli-log `i32`** in the Log domain, where the only operation is addition-of-logs (= multiplication of magnitudes) and **exact decade-crossing addition never arises** · **only MONEY needs both exactness and unbounded range in one value** - and **money is the ownership/item round's, not the substrate's** (`D-181`: 身外之物). ⇒ **`Scaled` is deleted from the engine substrate.** `I-1`, `I-2`, `I-3`, `I-5`, `L-6`, `L-7` and `I-20` all fall with it, because **the type they attack has no remaining user here.** |
| **D-252** `[E]` | **And when the item round DOES need it, the answer is a solved 1960s problem, written down here so it is not re-invented a second time.** **Decade-quantised exponent with a normalised mantissa** - the shape every arbitrary-precision decimal library uses: `{ mantissa: i32, exp10: i16 }` meaning `mantissa x 10^exp10`, canonical when `mantissa == 0 => exp10 == 0`, else **exactly nine digits**, `100_000_000 <= |mantissa| <= 999_999_999`. **`compare` becomes exact and total** - sign, then `exp10`, then mantissa - because equal exponents mean the same decade, which is the invariant my milli-log version never had. **Both counterexamples resolve:** `999_999_999` is `{999999999, 0}` and `10` is `{100000000, -7}`, and `0 > -7` gives the right answer; `{1, scale 500}` is **not representable at all**, which is the point - **a milli-log exponent was the defect, not a feature.** `add` aligns to the larger exponent and the loss is bounded and explicit; `multiply` adds exponents; `band_delta` is `(exp_a - exp_b) x 1000` plus a bounded mantissa correction from a small table. |
| **D-253** `[E]` | **`op` - DECIDED: `ModifierOp { Flat, Percent }`, the shipped enum** (`game-rules/src/stats/modifier.rs:99-106`). It is also **the one closed set the discriminator genuinely clears** (`resolve.rs:84` versus `:95`), so it is the last thing to invent a replacement for. `DerivationRow` uses the same op set; a derivation that needs a divisor gets one **as a field**, not as an op - see `D-256`. |
| **D-254** `[E + F:18_combat]` | **SET-versus-CONTRIBUTE - DECIDED: a `DerivationRow` CONTRIBUTES.** SET is the shipped defect: `block.rs:89` `self.set(StatSlot::MoveRange, …)` unconditionally overwrites a value that `resolve.rs:73-117` has just resolved through six modifier layers and two clamp passes - **so an Equipment `Flat(+2)` on `MoveRange` is accepted and silently discarded today** (`I-17`, `D-229`). **Choosing SET would be adopting `XST-D7`'s signature as a design.** ⇒ **CONTRIBUTE**, and the change in every existing reality's resolved `MoveRange` is a **migration handed to combat with its cause named**, not a silent behaviour change. |
| **D-255** `[E]` | **`PluginSet` width - DECIDED: `u32`, 32 plugins, with the reason stated rather than assumed.** It matches `MAX_DECLARED_QUANTITIES = 32`, so the two ceilings move together instead of drifting apart; and the ordinal law means **raising it later is an engine release that moves no existing digest** (only `0..n` is encoded), while lowering is impossible - so `u32` is the reversible direction. **`L-10` is answered honestly rather than by fiat: the 33rd plugin DOES touch actor core, and that is a VERSION BUMP, not a silent failure** - the acceptance test holds **up to the declared widths**, which is what it should have said. |
| **D-256** `[E]` | **A third `QuantityDomain` member - DECIDED: NO.** The domains stay `{ Linear, Log }`. **A permille is a UNIT of a Linear quantity, not a domain** - it is declared by the pool row that owns it (a `ResourceDecl` already names its quantity ordinal), so the fold needs no third arithmetic and `I-9`'s *"three read modes, two members"* resolves to **two modes plus a unit**. **And `I-10`'s real finding stands: the Log fold is UNWRITTEN** - `+10 %` on a milli-log must **add milli-log**, not scale it - so `QuantityDomain` earns its closure only when that branch exists. **Until then it is `UNWRITTEN`, and the discriminator's verdict on it is deferred rather than claimed.** |
| **D-257** `[E]` | **The canonical filler for a valueless ceiling - DECIDED: `source` is OMITTED from the canonical encoding when `rule` is `TierBased` or `Unbounded`.** `I-13` is right that eight encodable states for four meanings would give one set of rules two digests; the repo has ruled on this exact class twice (the empty-progression pin, and encoding the ceiling **discriminant** so `Slot(0)` and `Fixed(0)` cannot collide). **Omission, not a zero filler.** |
| **D-258** `[E]` | **Free-versus-absorbed - DECIDED: they are the SAME EVENT, and *"free"* was the wrong word.** A cost below the resolution floor is **ABSORBED with a zero charge**, and it emits, exactly as 11 requires. `L-14`/`I-30` found the contradiction; the resolution is not to add an exception but to **delete the second vocabulary**. |
| **D-259** `[E]` | **The verb for *cost exceeds current but not capacity* - DECIDED: it was never missing. `ZeroBehaviour` ships it** - `Clamp` (*"clamp at the floor and carry on"*) and `BlockCosts` (*"refuse any action whose cost this pool cannot pay"*), `resource/mod.rs:107-114`. **`L-15`/`I-21` are correct that the CONTRACT omitted it**; the fix is a citation, not a design. **And this is the fifth time this round that a *missing* thing turned out to be shipped** - `granted`'s replacement, `check_never_reused`, the causality wait, the ownership home, and now this. |
| **D-260** `[META]` | **UNDECIDED class: CLEARED. Nine questions, nine decisions, and SIX of them shrank the design rather than growing it** - `Scaled` deleted, no third domain member, no new op set, no new verb for cost-versus-current, omission instead of a filler, one vocabulary instead of two. **The remaining two are the ones that add: `CONTRIBUTE` semantics and the `u32` ceiling.** ⇒ **Next: the WRONG class (mechanical), then the UNWRITTEN class into the slice board with a spec each.** |

Nine items, each buildable here, each with a named done-when.** `U-1` **the Log fold branch** — *"+10 %"* on a milli-log must ADD milli-log; **until it exists `QuantityDomain` has not earned 14's verdict** (done when a Log quantity folds and a bite-test shows the Linear branch giving the wrong answer for it). `U-2` **the clamp channel** — the seam has two row types and the shipped clamp is a **two-pass ordered** parameter pair with intersect semantics and a floor-wins contradiction rule; a plugin cannot submit one today (done when `EC-1` ordering and `XST-D8` intersection are both expressible). `U-3` **`exp10_table`** — generated offline in exact rational arithmetic, round-half-even, checked in, **with a regeneration test**, because a startup `powf` is the 1-ULP bug in the fold. `U-4` **a `Floor` mirror of `Ceiling`** — `base(q)` has four competing shipped sources and `floor(q)` has one, pool-only. `U-5` **`band_delta`'s integer algorithm** — `ilog10` + a bounded mantissa table; it is the primitive four laws key on. `U-6` **`DerivationRow` needs a divisor field** — `factor_milli` alone cannot express the shipped move-range derivation (measured: `10_000 / 3 = 3_333` versus `x333/1000 = 3_330`). `U-7` **the `fold_layer` reachability check in its NON-VACUOUS form** — *every `fold_layer` present in submitted rows names a declared layer*, which can fail when a plugin ships a row for a layer it forgot to declare. `U-8` **the migration from the shipped `commit-service` `Actor`** — it holds `hp`, `max_hp`, `stats`, `snapshot` across a `Domain::extract`/`install` boundary with a `size_of` budget. `U-9` **~23 prose rules -> a gate or a relabel**, because in this repo a rule with no mechanism is worse than an absent one. |

| **D-264** `[META]` | **Where this leaves the round: 0 blocked · WRONG cleared · UNDECIDED decided · UNWRITTEN tracked with done-whens.** **The three contracts are 1 018 lines**, links resolve, no Vietnamese, and **every claim the two lenses refuted is either corrected, retracted, or restated with its real bound.** **What has NOT happened: a third verification round.** `D-212`'s stopping rule is `K` consecutive clean rounds and **round 3 has not run** — so this is a *fixed* state, not a *verified* one, and the difference is exactly the one `D-154` got wrong about a green gate. |

### 5w. `V-SEAL` ROUND 3 - the design: REGRESSION and ABSENCE

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-265** `[META]` | **Round 3's first lens takes THE EDITS as its subject, not the design - because the edits are now the riskiest artifact in the room.** Round 1's fix pass measured **27% defective**: corrections that were themselves wrong, citations past end-of-file, and a remedy that could not fail. **Round 2's pass was LARGER** - roughly 25 edits in one sitting, including **deleting a type that all three documents referenced**. **Same pressure, more surface.** ⇒ **`V-C` REGRESSION**, told to assume the same failure rate until it proves otherwise, and to hunt **dangling references after the deletion · orphaned arguments whose premise was retracted · corrections that are themselves wrong · fixes that removed something TRUE · new phantom citations introduced BY the fixes · a rename that did not propagate**. |
| **D-266** `[META]` | **The second lens hunts ABSENCE, and it is structurally different from all three that came before.** `V-SEAL` rounds 1, 2A and 2B all **REACTED to text on the page** - a citation to check, a claim to refute, an operation to implement. **None of them could see what was never written**, because nobody can argue with a sentence that does not exist. ⇒ **`V-D` COMPLETENESS**, hunting: **the lifecycle nobody wrote** (spawn, attach, initialise, detach, tear-down) · **the failure modes nobody named** (a malformed row, a row for an unattached plugin, a duplicate, a panic mid-fold) · **concurrency and ordering** · **versioning and migration** · **persistence** (what actually enters the ledger for a hub operation) · **observability** (*"why is this number 47"*) · **what the SHIPPED code demonstrably needs and the contracts never mention** · **anything real that falls in none of hub / substrate / plugin.** |
| **D-267** `[META]` | **One constraint keeps the absence lens honest: a missing thing is a finding ONLY if the agent can name WHAT BREAKS WITHOUT IT.** An absence hunt with no such bar returns an infinite list, because everything not written is technically missing. **Requiring a concrete broken scenario, a question nobody can answer, or a code path with no spec** is what makes the output a work list rather than an anxiety list - and it is the same discipline `D-210` imposed on the refutation lenses by demanding a *"tried to break and could not"* section. |

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-268** `[META]` | **🔴 THE ABSENCE LENS FOUND WHAT THREE ROUNDS OF REFUTATION STRUCTURALLY COULD NOT - and the methodological lesson is general: a verification programme made only of REFUTATION lenses has a blind spot EXACTLY THE SIZE OF WHAT WAS NEVER WRITTEN.** Rounds 1, 2A and 2B each reacted to text on the page - a citation to check, a claim to refute, an operation to implement. **Nobody can argue with a sentence that does not exist**, so 78 prior findings contained none of these 15. |
| **D-269** `[E]` | **🔴 THE WORST FINDING OF THE PROGRAMME, and I verified both halves by hand: `M-1` + `M-12` together mean THE CONTRACTS SPECIFY A SYSTEM THAT SPAWNS ACTORS WITH ZERO HP AND NO WAY TO GIVE THEM ANY.** **`M-12`:** the archetype is a **separate `&StatBlock` parameter** seeding `flat` at `resolve.rs:75` - **not a modifier** - and 3.8 correctly said so **while converting the six-member source enum to `fold_layer` ordinals and never saying what becomes of the parameter.** It is **the only source of a non-zero starting value in the shipped system**; `StatBlock::zeroed`'s own doc reads ***"NOT a playable actor - a zeroed block has 0 max HP"***, and `StatBlock::from_defaults` exists because ***"'Playable with zero declaration' is a HARD REQUIREMENT"*** - **a requirement neither new document restates.** **`M-1`:** there is no spawn contract at all; an actor is born today by two independent hand-written statements (`isle.spawn_entity(id)` and `state.actors.insert(id, Actor::new(&ruleset, 100))`) that nothing forces to agree, and `Actor::new` gets its numbers from `CombatStats::archetype_melee` - **the thing the new contract deletes.** ⇒ **This is not UNWRITTEN. It is WRONG: the surgery removed something that ships, works, and is load-bearing.** |
| **D-270** `[E]` | **🔴 `M-4` CRITICAL, and it lands on the exact section I closed by declaring it already solved: TWO IDENTITY SPACES, ZERO CONVERSION SITES.** The hub's item 1 is `EntityId(pub u64)` (`sim-core/src/types.rs:17`). The hub's item 3 is `GoneState`, whose resolver is keyed by **`EntityRef { entity_id: String (UUID), aggregate_type: Pc\|Npc\|Region\|WorldKv\|Session, reality_id: String }`** (`dp-kernel/src/entity_status.rs:126-133`). **Measured by me: ZERO conversion sites between them anywhere in `crates/` or `services/`.** ⇒ Given `EntityId(1)` inside an island, **nobody can say which row a GDPR erasure or a reality archive writes.** `D-203` concluded *"there is nothing to design"* - **true of the enum, false of the keying**, and the difference is the whole of item 3. |
| **D-271** `[E]` | **🔴 `M-3` CRITICAL: by the substrate's OWN rule, an actor's initial state is FABRICATED.** 2 states *"canon is what is written to the ledger; state that was never written is fabricated and may be recomputed differently tomorrow."* **No document names a single event a hub operation emits** - not creation, not attachment, not a quantity change. `CombatEvent` has 8 variants (`Struck · Missed · Defended · Moved · Fled · Downed · StatusExpired · EncounterEnded`) and **none creates an entity**; the platform registry's 15 event types **none creates an entity**. 11's seven rows are all about a value being **bounded** - **not one is about a value being SET.** |
| **D-272** `[E]` | **🔴 `M-7`: `StatSnapshot` / `StatEpoch` SHIP, and the hub neither inherits, replaces, nor retires them.** `Actor.snapshot: StatSnapshot` (`actor.rs:31`), resolved once at encounter start, with `StatEpoch` a **5-tuple** `{manifest_version, progression_turn, equipment_version, status_version, archetype_version}` whose ***"job is to make staleness detectable"*** via `is_stale(&current)`. **Three of those five members are exactly the plugins 3.8 names.** ⇒ **Nothing in the contracts says WHEN the fold runs or whether a resolved value is snapshotted** - so a progression tick mid-encounter retroactively changes how earlier rounds should have resolved, and the shipped comment says that is ***"the normal case, not an exotic one."*** **A plugin author cannot know whether their row takes effect this round or next.** |
| **D-273** `[E]` | **Eleven more, each with a named breakage.** `M-2` **no detach contract** - detach a plugin and its `quantities[q]` is unaddressable while still occupying a hashed, checkpointed slot; re-attaching resurrects a value from an arbitrary past. `M-5` **no malformed-row semantics** - five reachable cases, each a live choice between *refuse the row / refuse the fold / drop silently*, and the shipped `intersect_clamps` **already deferred this in writing** to *"wherever those clamps get declared, which does not exist yet"* - **3.7 IS that place.** `M-6` **no purity/failure contract for the fold**, while the neighbouring seam states one explicitly (`Domain::apply` *"MUST be deterministic and total"*) and backs it with catch-unwind, poisoning and quarantine. `M-8` **`PluginSet` ordinals have no declaration home and no never-reuse check**, so *"never reused"* is prose where the quantity side earns it from a hashed table plus a cross-epoch checker. `M-9` **no migration for an entity created under an older epoch** - `swap_ruleset` touches `D::State` **not at all**. `M-10` **no explain path** - `resolve_block` returns a bare `StatBlock` and discards `source`; *"why is this number 47"* has no answer. `M-11` **the plugin -> quantity -> domain -> layer registry is owned by NONE of the three layers** - `QuantityTable` stores names only. `M-13` intra-layer order. `M-14` **no `size_of` assertion for the hub**, in a repo where four such assertions ship and one carries a four-entry repin log. `M-15` the `Gen` threading is named as open work and **is on no clearing list**. |
| **D-274** `[META]` | **THE FIVE HUB ITEMS, ADJUDICATED BY THE AGENT: items 2, 3 and 4 are NOT DONE-ABLE; items 1 and 5 are PARTLY.** **1 Identity** - adopting `EntityId` is a no-op, but the UUID bridge (`M-4`) and the `Gen` threading (`M-15`) are open. **2 Intrinsic quantities** - the read rule and the width are decided; **initial value, detach, persistence, migration and the size assertion are not.** **3 Existence** - *"nothing to design"* is the claim to challenge: the enum is free, **the keying does not exist.** **4 Attachment** - one sentence and a width the doc declines to choose. **5 The fold** - **the arithmetic is exact and matches shipped code**; the mechanism (when it runs, determinism, malformed rows, intra-layer order, provenance, the Log branch, and 3.8's own unassigned prerequisite) is not. **And the report is non-vacuous: 9 entries under *things I expected to be missing and found*.** |

Third occurrence of that exact shape.** **`R-14`**: `layer.rs:41` is `impl Layer {`; the enum is `:22` and the quoted doc line is `:44`. **`R-15`**: `island/mod.rs:92-100` is a **doc comment**, not the discard - which is at `:301` - and CLAUDE.md already records that *"a doc comment is prose that happens to live in a source file"*. |

### 5z. `M-1`/`M-12` REJECTED - the PO caught a FOURTH encroachment, and I had amplified it

> **PO:** *that is not a gap - you are encroaching on a feature again. Spawn WHAT, when the map system is
> not built and the archetype feature does not exist?*

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-283** `[META]` | **🔴 `D-269` CALLED `M-1`/`M-12` *"the worst finding of the programme"*. IT IS NOT A HUB FINDING AT ALL, and I amplified it instead of applying my own scope test - the FOURTH encroachment this round, and the second I accepted from an agent rather than authored.** **Spawn is not a hub operation.** It needs **a place to spawn into · a template of what to spawn · a reason to spawn** - a map/place feature, an archetype feature, and an encounter or world-gen feature. **Measured: no `SpawnPoint` exists anywhere in `crates/` or `services/`; `struct Place`/`struct Tile` live only in `tilemap-service`; the only `Archetype` type is in `world-gen`.** ⇒ **there is nothing to spawn INTO and no template to spawn FROM.** |
| **D-284** `[E + F:18_combat]` | **And the archetype is COMBAT'S, by measurement and by a decision taken on day one.** `StatRules::melee_archetype` and `slot_defaults` are `[i32; SLOT_COUNT]` over **ten COMBAT slots**; `melee_archetype`'s own module doc records its provenance as *"`CombatStats::archetype_melee`'s 12 / 2 / 450 / 100"*. **`D-14`, the fourteenth decision of the entire round, already ruled: *"Combat vocabulary is out of scope and belongs to the combat element's design."*** ⇒ **absorbing the archetype into the hub would be exactly the encroachment the whole `C-3` split exists to prevent** - and I proposed it under the banner of fixing a gap. |
| **D-285** `[E]` | **What the hub ACTUALLY owes is one sentence, now written as 3.4b: *every quantity of every attached plugin begins at the initial value that plugin's own declaration supplies; a quantity of an unattached plugin is ABSENT, not zero.*** That is already the ruleset's shape - **`ResourceDecl.base` is documented as *"The value an actor starts at"*** and `ProgressionKindDecl.initial_value` is its progression counterpart. **The hub READS them; it does not author them, and it has no opinion about what a melee fighter starts with.** ⇒ **`M-1` and `M-12` collapse from *"the worst finding of the programme"* to a one-paragraph statement of an obligation that was always small.** |
| **D-286** `[F:18_combat]` | **The real question survives, and it is COMBAT'S: *"playable with zero declaration"* is a SHIPPED REQUIREMENT with a SHIPPED IMPLEMENTATION, and nothing in the new frame restates it.** `StatBlock::zeroed` - *"NOT a playable actor - a zeroed block has 0 max HP"*; `StatBlock::from_defaults` - *"'Playable with zero declaration' is a hard requirement, and this is where that requirement is now met from."* **Handed to combat with the measurement attached** (handoffs 1.5), and **whether it stays combat's or moves to an archetype feature that does not exist yet is a decision this round deliberately does not take.** |
| **D-287** `[META]` | **The pattern, now at four, and its tell is stable: AN AGENT MEASURES CORRECTLY AND SCOPES WRONG, AND I AMPLIFY THE SCOPE INSTEAD OF CHECKING IT.** The completeness agent's measurement was exact - the archetype IS the only non-zero source today - and its conclusion (*the hub must replace it*) assumed the hub owns whatever the hub's removal exposed. **A finding's EVIDENCE and a finding's OWNER are separate claims, and only the first arrives verified.** ⇒ **Mechanical form: before acting on any agent finding, run the strip-naked test and the feature-index check on the FINDING ITSELF** - not on the artifact it points at. |

### 6a. A FOURTH CATEGORY - PREMATURE - because actor hub is FEATURE #1 and nothing is built

> **PO:** *nothing is built. I already said it: actor hub is the FIRST feature.*

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-288** `[META]` | **🔴 THE TAXONOMY WAS MISSING A CATEGORY, and its absence is why 15 absence-findings all read as debt.** `D-249` gave four buckets - **BLOCKED** (external, unwritable: **0**) · **WRONG** (correct it) · **UNDECIDED** (decide it) · **UNWRITTEN** (build it). **A fifth is needed: PREMATURE - a REAL question whose answer requires a feature that does not exist yet.** It is not blocked (nothing external), not wrong (nobody asserted anything false), not undecided (there is no basis to decide on), and **not unwritten** - **writing it now would be inventing the second feature's requirements from the first feature's chair**, which is the encroachment `D-283` just caught. |
| **D-289** `[META]` | **Re-triaged against *"actor hub is feature #1, nothing is built"*: FIVE of the fifteen absence-findings are PREMATURE, not debt.** **`M-1` spawn** and **`M-12` archetype** - rejected outright (`D-283`/`D-284`): not the hub's, and **there is nothing to spawn into and no template to spawn from**. **`M-2` detach semantics** - detach *which plugin*? **Plugin #2 does not exist**; the question is answerable the moment it does, and not before. **`M-9` epoch migration** - **no reality has ever been pinned**, so there is no entity created under an older ruleset to migrate. **`M-8` plugin-ordinal registry and never-reuse** - the mechanism is right and the shape is known (`never_reuse.rs` is the template), **but an ordinal space with one member cannot be renumbered**, so the check has no failing input until feature #2 arrives. ⇒ **Each gets a NAMED TRIGGER rather than a slice: *"when plugin #2 is declared"* / *"when the first reality is pinned"*.** |
| **D-290** `[E]` | **And the other TEN stay, because they are the hub's own and answerable now.** `M-5` malformed-row semantics - **the seam is ours and the shipped `intersect_clamps` explicitly deferred this to *"wherever those clamps get declared"*, which is 3.7**. `M-6` the fold's purity and failure contract - the neighbouring `Domain::apply` states one and backs it with catch-unwind and quarantine. `M-7` **when the fold runs and whether it snapshots** - `StatSnapshot`/`StatEpoch` ship and the hub neither inherits nor retires them; **this is the largest genuine one**. `M-10` no explain path. `M-11` the declaration registry, owned by none of the three layers. `M-13` intra-layer order. `M-14` a `size_of` assertion, cheap and mechanical. `M-15` `Gen` threading (substrate's). Plus `M-3`'s **hub half** - *what does the fold's own operation write* - **though its spawn/attach half is premature with `M-1`**. And `M-4`'s bridge is **half premature**: the platform keyspace matters when the hub meets the platform, which is not feature #1. |
| **D-291** `[META]` | **What this changes about the PROGRAMME, and it is the useful part: a verification agent cannot see BUILD ORDER.** Every lens was handed three documents and a repo, and **not one was told this is feature #1 of N**. So each measured the contracts against **a finished system**, and reported every seam to an unbuilt neighbour as a hole. **That is a correct measurement and a wrong frame**, and it is the same shape as `D-287` one level up: **the agents' EVIDENCE is reliable and their SCOPE is not**, because scope is exactly the thing the prompt did not give them. ⇒ **Future `V-SEAL` prompts must state the build position** - *"this is feature 1 of N; a seam to an unbuilt feature is a TRIGGER, not a gap"* - or the absence lens will keep returning the rest of the game as debt. |

| **D-293** `[META]` | **SEALED, by cutting rather than by analysing further.** **`handoffs-to-features.md` (276 lines) DELETED**, replaced by **`2026-08-02-seams-and-triggers.md` (50)** - a register whose rule is *a MEASURED FACT about shipped code, the SEAM it touches, and the TRIGGER that makes it someone else's problem; no mechanics, no proposals, no numbers we chose.* **`engine-substrate.md` 428 -> 142**: money representation, power-scale domain, permille pools, unified ceiling, band delta and per-feature ruleset structure **all removed - every one serves a feature that does not exist.** **`actor-hub.md` 403 -> 172**: the `WARNING CORRECTED` block per verification finding moved to the derivation record, **because a contract states what is decided, not how it got there.** **1 107 -> 364 lines.** |

### 6d. BUILD — the decisions the implementation forced (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-298** `[E]` | **The hub is a CRATE, `crates/actor-hub`, and it does NOT depend on `game-rules`.** The crate-versus-module argument is `IMP-Q2`'s verbatim — *"leaning crate, because the gate is the point"* — and the fold is a function of `(actor, declarations, rows)` with one correct answer, so the same purity boundary applies. **The refused dependency is the load-bearing half:** the hub sits *beneath* the features and combat is a feature, so a hub → `game-rules` edge becomes a **cycle** the day combat is rewritten as a plugin. `actor-hub` is registered in `crate-purity-gate.py`'s `PURE_CRATES` **without** `game-rules` in its workspace set, and R1 is deny-by-default — so the absence *is* the refusal, not a comment about one. |
| **D-299** `[E]` | **`ModifierOp` moved DOWN to `ruleset-core`; `game_rules::stats::ModifierOp` re-exports it.** `D-253` decided the hub's `op` is *"the shipped enum"*, and `D-298` forbids the dependency that would have reached it. The three options were: depend on `game-rules` (rejected — the cycle), define a second `Flat \| Percent` (rejected — one concept with two names, the exact rot this project deletes rather than layers over), or **move the definition beneath both consumers**. No law changed; `cargo test -p ruleset-core -p game-rules` is unchanged at **103 passed, 0 failed**. |
| **D-300** `[E]` | **`GoneState` moved DOWN to a new pure leaf crate `entity-existence`; `dp_kernel::entity_status::GoneState` re-exports it, and the public path is unchanged.** Hub §3.3 names *this* enum, and `dp-kernel` carries **tokio, sqlx and a `SystemTime`** for the resolver that lives beside it — so a pure hub could not reach it, and a copy would have been a **second SSOT for a precedence lattice that is also mirrored in Go**. **The repo already had this exact shape:** `breaker-core` was extracted out of `dp-kernel::resilience` for the same reason (loom could not build through tokio) and `dp_kernel::resilience::CircuitBreaker` is likewise unchanged. **What did NOT move:** the 4-layer resolver, the cascade, `EntityRef`, the envelope, the cache — and the parity/precedence tests stayed with them, so they now exercise the type *through* the re-export. |
| **D-301** `[E]` | **`U-9` is discharged with a mechanism, and its scope is a DIRECTORY.** `scripts/hashed-substrate-float-gate.py` scans `crates/ruleset-core/src` and `crates/actor-hub/src` **recursively**, so a file created tomorrow is covered on its first line — an enumerated file list is *default-uncovered* (`NV-3`), and a new `f64` field is exactly the thing that arrives in a new file. **It is not a float ban**: `DF7-A4` was revised this round precisely because *"float cannot reproduce across targets"* is false for basic IEEE 754 operations, and `world-gen` disproves it across 41 files while hashing deterministically. Wired pre-commit; CI discovers it automatically via `gate-wiring-gate --run-all`. |
| **D-302** `[E]` | **And the gate's own self-test caught a hole in the gate — which is the entire argument for writing one.** `\bf(?:32\|64)\b` requires a word boundary before `f`, and the suffixed literal **`2f64` has a digit there** — so a gate written to refuse `f64` passed `let x = 2f64;`. The left edge is now `(?<![A-Za-z_])`, which still refuses `my_f32_helper` and the hex literal `0xf32`. **The self-test feeds every rule input that VIOLATES it and input that must not trip it**; a self-test made only of clean input proves nothing. |

### 6e. THE B1 VERIFIER — 9 findings, and the pattern is the SAME ONE (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-303** `[META]` | **🔴 THE VERIFIER FOUND ZERO VACUITY IN ELEVEN TESTS AND FOUR GATE RULES — AND THREE CITATION DEFECTS. That ratio is the round's whole lesson repeating itself.** It mutated `attach`, `detach`, `contains`, `count_ones`, `from_bits`, `EMPTY`, both `new` bounds and `bit()`, and **every mutation went red**; it re-derived the `const` assertion at 16 and at 64; it injected `std::fs`, `ruleset-loader`, `game-rules` and `serde_json` into the crate and got a finding for each. **The mechanisms held. The PROSE did not.** Same shape as `D-287`: what fails is never the thing that was measured, it is the sentence written around it. |
| **D-304** `[E]` | **`F1` MAJOR — I cited `QTY-A14` for a failure the newtype does not touch, and the paraphrase was exact enough to be convincing.** `ordinal.rs` said a newtype makes *"the exact silent failure `QTY-A14` names"* a type error. **Two different failures were being treated as one:** **SPACE** confusion (a quantity ordinal passed where a plugin ordinal was expected) — which the newtype *does* fix — and **TABLE** confusion (reality A's ordinal 3 is `qi`, reality B's is `mana`) — which it does **not**, because `QTY-A14`'s actual axiom is *"any datum that leaves the island carrying an ordinal MUST carry the digest that gives it meaning"* and **nothing in the crate carries a digest**. The derived `PartialEq`/`Ord`/`Hash` compare the two equal. ⇒ the module doc now separates them, states the carrier as **UNWRITTEN**, and the seam is registered as `S-11`. **The damage of the original wording is precise: a future reader greps `QTY-A14`, finds it "addressed in shipped code", and never builds the carrier.** |
| **D-305** `[E]` | **`F3` MAJOR — the never-reuse law was stated for BOTH ordinal spaces and mechanised for one, with the missing check unnamed.** That is `NV-6`'s last row verbatim: *"an axiom that constrains code — name the check that would go red. If none exists, that missing check is itself the finding."* And the tell is that **the same file's sibling did it right**: `plugin_set.rs` names `M-2`/`D-289` and its trigger on `detach` in the same slice. ⇒ `ordinal.rs` now has a section saying plainly that the plugin space has **no** never-reuse mechanism, why building one today would be `NV-1` vacuity (an ordinal space with one member cannot be renumbered), and its trigger — **when plugin #2 is declared**. Registered as `S-12`. |
| **D-306** `[E]` | **`F2` MAJOR at the time it was written, and MEASURED RESOLVED rather than argued away.** The purity-gate entry pre-authorised `entity-existence` and `serde` for a `GoneState` justification that did not yet exist in the crate — spending deny-by-default's diff-visibility in advance. **B2 and B6 then made both real.** Re-measured **by deletion, one entry at a time, against the live tree: removing `entity-existence`, `serde`, `blake3` or `sim-core` each produces a finding.** Every entry is now load-bearing. **The verifier's method — delete it and see whether the gate notices — is the only way to tell an allowlist entry from a wish, and it is now how this repo checks one.** |
| **D-307** `[E]` | **Four MINOR findings, all fixed, and one of them was a bite-test I had narrated wrongly.** `F4`/`F5`/`F6` dead rustdoc links (a public doc pointing at a private item, and two unresolved intra-crate links) — `cargo doc -p actor-hub --no-deps` is now **clean**. `F7` `QuantityOrdinal::get()` had **zero coverage**: replacing its body with `0` reddened nothing, and `get` is the ENCODING path, so a wrong ordinal there is the never-reuse hazard arriving silently — two round-trip tests added. `F9`: my doc claimed raising `MAX_DECLARED_QUANTITIES` to 64 reds the width assertion **first**, and it does not — `ruleset.rs`'s own `size_of::<Ruleset>()` budget fires before it. The assertion is real; the narration was off by one step, and it now says so. |
| **D-308** `[E]` | **THE BUILD FOUND A DEFECT WITH ITS OWN TEST, WHICH IS THE ARGUMENT FOR WRITING THE TEST FIRST.** `HubRegistry` initially stored *which* plugin declared each fold layer. `build_is_order_independent` FAILED: a fold layer is a shared ordering position that two plugins legitimately declare, so *"first declarer"* is a fact about **the order the declarations arrived in** — two identical realities built from the same set produced two different registries. **And nothing read the field.** A stored value with no reader that also breaks an invariant is a defect twice over; it is now a `bool`. |
| **D-309** `[E]` | **`bound` on `DerivationRow` is kept, and the contradiction `R-19` raised is GONE rather than tolerated.** `D-279` flagged the row's inline bound as *"a THIRD ceiling model, in the round whose §8 is titled 'Ceilings — one model'"*. **The scope seal deleted §8**, so there is no longer a "one model" claim for it to contradict. What remains is stated exactly: the bound limits **the amount this row contributes**, not the quantity — which the shipped move-range derivation demonstrably needs (`clamp(base + speed / per_tile, 1, max)`) — and the quantity **ceiling model** stays `U-4`, another feature's. |
| **D-310** `[E]` | **The fold's honest limit, written into the code rather than discovered later: a derivation reads a MODIFIER-ONLY value.** Three passes — check every row, resolve from modifier rows, turn derivations into contributions against those values, re-resolve and emit. **A derivation of a derivation is therefore inexpressible**, and that is the shipped shape (`resolve_block` runs its modifier loop and *then* derives `MoveRange` from the finalised `Speed`), chosen because the alternative is a dependency solver the hub has no basis to design. Registered as `S-13`. |

### 6aa. ROUND 15 — the twin defect, made mechanical (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-443** `[META]` | **🔴 A verifier measured SIX findings in one commit that are all the same object**, in the commit whose stated subject is that object: `baseline_is_green` cased and its Rust twin not · the negative sentinel guarded at its PRODUCER and not its CONSUMER · the environment asserted for the MUTATION child and not the BASELINE child · the gate half's drift and timeout counters cased and the Rust half's not. **Noticing this by reading is what has failed fifteen rounds.** ⇒ a **PARITY table**: each row names a property both halves must have and the substring that witnesses it, plus an assertion that every such guard has a mutation row. It found two gaps on its first run. |
| **D-444** `[E]` | **🔴 A self-mutating file reds on ANCHOR DRIFT, not on the rule — and red for the wrong reason reads exactly like red for the right one.** Mutating this harness removes its own anchor, so the copy's drift check fires and the parent records RED while the rule was never exercised. ⇒ the child is told **which row is under test** and skips that anchor. |
| **D-445** `[E]` | **🔴 And the survivor that took longest was a DUPLICATED RULE.** `run_rust` carried **two** `if baseline is not None:` blocks — the funnel added this round, and the injection block it was meant to replace — so the second re-evaluated and masked every mutation of the first. The same class as `D-413`'s duplicated fence logic, two rounds on. ⇒ one block. Then the remaining survivor was the same shape one level deeper: `baseline` injected the VERDICT, so the line reading the probe's return code was reachable only by seeding a red crate suite. ⇒ `baseline` injects the PROBE; one extraction, one path. |
| **D-446** `[META]` | **A witness table is DATA wherever it is defined.** `_outside_tables` walked `tree.body` only, so the parity table — a local — counted as code and every anchor it names counted twice. ⇒ `ast.walk`, and `parity` joins the `*MUTATIONS` names. |

### 6ah. THE GATES WERE SLOW FOR REASONS THAT WERE NOT CHECKING (2026-08-04)

PO, on seeing the verification cost: *a gate is necessary against rot, but an
inefficient gate is stagnation.* Profiled rather than guessed, and every second
removed here was **the same answer computed again** — no rule weakened, no case
dropped, and the self-test's output is byte-identical.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-506** `[E]` | **`cargo test` ran FOUR times with identical arguments inside one process.** The repository cannot change mid-process, so runs two through four were the first answer, bought again. ⇒ memoised on the argument list. **The bypass is the load-bearing half:** `--self-test` drives these measurements with INJECTED runners precisely to assert *which arguments each one asks for* on a machine with no toolchain, and a shared cache would hand one case another case's answer. An injected `run` or `which` therefore never reads and never writes the cache — bitten by removing the condition and watching the cases collide. |
| **D-507** `[E]` | **The pure text functions were re-deriving the same four documents 91 times** — 3 616 calls to `_claimable` and **2.68 million** to `_scan_line` beneath it. ⇒ memoised **on their arguments**, whose first element IS the document text. That is the whole safety argument: **an edited document is a different key, so there is no invalidation to forget and no identity to go stale** — a path-keyed cache would have had both problems. `_marker_hits` returns a mutable list, so the cached form returns a tuple and the wrapper copies; handing every caller the same list is a defect no timing would ever reveal. |
| **D-508** `[E]` | **The numbers, measured the same way on both sides — and the first set was WRONG.** The profiled run said 33.0s, which is cProfile's overhead, and `read` showed 10.1s of `tottime` against 0.709s of `cumtime` — an impossible pair that should have been read as *the profiler is lying about this row* rather than as a finding. Re-measured by running the **committed** gate and the new one from the same directory, back to back: with cargo present **26.8s → 13.1s**; with cargo absent — the environment the mutation harness's only automatic runner has — **8.5s → 3.3s**. So the Python checking saves 5.2s and the toolchain saves the rest. The full 126-mutation sweep now runs in **10.0 minutes**, all 126 red. **A profiler is a hypothesis generator, not a measurement**, which is `D-503`'s lesson wearing different clothes: only the before/after under identical conditions settled it. |

| **D-509** `[E]` | **🔴 A third cache SURVIVED its own bite test, and was removed rather than cased.** The file-read cache, keyed on `(path, mtime, size)`, went GREEN when its key was mutated to drop the mtime — and correctly so: **the documents cannot change while the process runs**, so nothing inside a self-test can tell a fresh read from a cached one. Measured best-of-three with cargo absent, it was worth **2.94s against 2.98s** — inside the noise, because the profiler row that motivated it reported `tottime` 10.1s and `cumtime` 0.709s for the same function, which is impossible. **Bought nothing, guardable by nothing, so it is gone** — the identical call made on the two-phase span half (`D-505`) six hours earlier, for the identical reason. The two caches that *earn* their place each have a mutation row now: `the cargo memo forgets that an injected runner is not the real one`, and `the blanking cache drops an argument from its key`. |
| **D-510** `[E]` | **The first bite of these caches was RED FOR THE WRONG REASON, and only a control showed it.** Running the mutated copy out of a scratch directory made two of three go red with `FileNotFoundError` from `measure()` — an artifact of *where the file sat*, not a disagreement, and it would have certified both caches on evidence about pathnames. ⇒ the probe now writes beside the original **and runs an UNMUTATED copy from the same place first**: if the location were doing the work, the control would red too. A bite test needs a control for exactly the reason a rule needs a canary. |

**Drift.** `DRB-17` — splitting `_marker_hits` into a cached tuple form and a
copying wrapper moved a line that a mutation row anchors on, and the harness's
own anchor-drift detector caught it on the next sweep, refusing to report
anything until the row was retargeted. The optimisation was three lines and it
still broke a witness; **the drift check is what made that a two-minute fix
instead of a silently weakened row.**

### 6ag. ROUND 19 ADDENDUM — the fix for an enumeration was exponential, and every check still said GREEN (2026-08-04)

Not a verifier finding. Found by the **wall clock**: the post-fix sweep of round
19 was still on its first table after 53 minutes, which was the only symptom the
apparatus ever produced.

`D-496` removed the `{0,3}` word bound from the bolded-figure shape because the
bound was an enumeration. That was the right call and the replacement was
catastrophic: three star-quantified space runs inside one repeated group, so a
run of words can be split many ways and **every split is re-explored when the
closing `**` never arrives**.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-502** `[E]` | **🔴 The bolded-figure shape was EXPONENTIAL, and it read GREEN on every arm this apparatus owns.** Measured on an unpaired `**`: n=10 **0.001s**, n=20 **0.89s**. On the four real governed blocks: **0.00s → 6.64s**, taking the gate's whole `--self-test` from seconds to **189.5s**. rc stayed 0, all sixteen shape cases still passed, `--check` still agreed with the artifacts. **Only the wall clock moved.** ⇒ the repair is ONE token: the separator between two words is **mandatory**, because an optional one lets `ab` be re-parsed as `a`+`b` and that ambiguity is the whole defect. Measured after: **0.9ms** on a 5 000-word probe, the worst governed block **7.272s → 0.00003s**, the finding set **identical on 4/4 blocks** and the same **94** figures across the whole run-state document. |
| **D-503** `[E]` | **THREE repairs that LOOK unambiguous were written, believed, and measured wrong.** An alternation separator — still exponential (n=20, **0.89s**, no better than the defect), because a greedy `[ \t]+` still gives its characters back. One disjoint character class per separator — still exponential (n=24, **4.72s**), the empty-separator re-parse. A probe left running to the far end of the curve makes the scale plain: at n=32 those two forms cost **2 144s** and **1 948s** — half an hour, for one bolded phrase of thirty-two words, in a check that reports itself GREEN. A two-phase span/body split — linear, but its span half was **redundant**, which is `D-505`. **A regex being readable is not a proof that it is linear**, and reading it four times produced four wrong answers. Only the growth curve ever settled it. |
| **D-504** `[E]` | **The class: this apparatus can see a HANG and cannot see a SLOWDOWN.** Its one timing alarm is the harness's `SLOW` marker, which fires when a child exceeds the 300s child bound. **189.5s is not 300s**, so a 60× regression sat below every alarm for a full round while reporting full coverage. ⇒ a **budget** is what separates slow from hung, and the gate now carries two probes with one. The 24 is not a round number: it is read off the MEASURED growth of the mutation the case exists to catch — ~4× per two words, so 24 costs the mutant **~4s** against a 0.5s budget, while 28 costs it a minute and 40 **hangs** the child. A probe that is too strong is as useless here as one too weak, because a hang is not a failing case. |
| **D-505** `[E]` | **🔴 The bite test refused to certify the fix's own mechanism, and it was right.** Two of the five bolded-figure rows survived. *The span half:* the mutation restoring the old regex made the child exceed 300s, so the verdict was **SLOW, not FAIL** — `D-478` declining to accept a hang as a bite, applied to my own new work an hour after I wrote the row. Measuring instead of arguing showed the half bought **nothing**: with a mandatory separator one regex is already linear (**0.9ms** at 5 000 words; the identical 94 figures), so the piece is REMOVED rather than cased — a component no case can honestly guard should not exist. *The bound:* `{0,3}` on the new shape permits **four** words, and the existing four-word cases matched it, so the anti-enumeration row went GREEN. ⇒ a **twelve-word** case: a case has to outrun the numbers a mutation would plausibly pick, not just the last one that was tried. |

**Drift.** `DRB-15` — the exponential form was shipped in round 19 and passed
every gate, every case and the pre-commit hook; what caught it was noticing that
a background job was slow. Had the sweep been left to run unattended it would
have finished, GREEN, in some number of hours, and the regression would have
been recorded as a successful verification round. `DRB-16` — the repair for
`D-502` shipped with a comment asserting *"linearity needs BOTH halves, and
neither half is sufficient alone."* That sentence was written in the same hour as
the finding it belongs to, was never measured, and was **false**; the bite test
found it within the hour. Four consecutive readings of a regex produced four
wrong answers about its complexity, and every correct answer in this section
came from a growth curve.

**And the rule caught its own author, in the sentence describing it.** Writing
the handoff narrative for this finding, I put `**189.5s**` inside the governed
handoff block — a bolded figure no rule reads, in a block that claims every
figure in it is checked. `O-R15-4`'s detector reported it on the next run. The
remedy is the one its comment already prescribes: unbold it. A measurement
quoted in prose is not a governed figure, and the difference is exactly the
bold.

### 6af. ROUND 19 — two decisions in one commit, the second feeding the first what it rejects (2026-08-04)

Round 18 wrote `D-478` (*a bite is a FAILING CASE, because a mutant that cannot
execute proves nothing*) and `D-489` (*a self-test that dies prints a `FAIL`
line*) **in the same commit**. The second hands the first exactly the input it
was written to reject. **NV shape #3 — an adjacent decision defeats it** — which
the standard names as the hardest, and both halves were mine, one screen apart.

|---|---|
| **D-492** `[E]` | **🔴 `B1`. `120/120` was 118 verdicts and two artifacts, by `D-470`'s own arithmetic, in the commit that fixed `D-470`.** The crash wrapper printed the word `FAIL`, which is precisely the token the harness counts as a case disagreeing — so a child that DIED read as a rule that BIT again. ⇒ **`CRASH` is not `FAIL`**: the gate still exits non-zero, so a human and the driver both see red, and only the harness's *"did a case disagree"* question gets the honest answer. Cased with a child that reaches cases, passes them, and is then caught crashing. |
| **D-493** `[E]` | **...and the two rules that had been standing on that artifact DID have cases — the cases just crashed.** `guard`, `check_block`, `check_doc` and the missing-end-marker case all called into the rule and let the exception escape, so for eighteen rounds a traceback stood in for a disagreement. ⇒ **an exception inside a case is a failure OF THAT CASE**: attributed, named, and the run continues — the same argument the harness already makes about a timeout being a finding about one mutation rather than the end of the sweep. Both rules now red by disagreement. |
| **D-494** `[E]` | **🔴 `B2`. The label rule examines 11 of 140 rows, and the defect it was built for is not one of them** — because round 18's remedy was to RENAME the label, which deleted the dot the rule keys on and moved BOTH rows out of scope. **The fix removed its own subject.** Widening the token shape was measured and rejected: keying on *declared identifiers* cry-wolfs on `guard`, `scan`, `first`, `line`; keying on capitals reaches five rows and still reports `TOTAL`. ⇒ a **derived sibling rule** instead: if two rows in one file have anchors differing by exactly one CAPITALISED token, each label must name its own. That is `D-476`'s exact shape. Measured before wiring: 2 sibling pairs shipped, 0 findings, and the verifier's swap-the-anchors probe reports **both** halves. Lower-case differences are excluded because requiring them produced three findings on three correct rows. |
| **D-495** `[E]` | **`B2`'s sub-defect: the label rule used a raw `text.find` in the file that mutates itself** — landing on the TABLE ROW naming a rule instead of the rule, so the "enclosing definition" spanned the whole table and the one file the rule cannot police was the one it lives in. `_find_one` exists for exactly this. |
| **D-496** `[E]` | **`M3`. The word bound was itself an enumeration, and it lasted ONE round.** A planted `**999 Rust integration tests pass**` — four words — sat in the governed block with the gate silent and rc 0: `D-479`'s shape one word further along. ⇒ unbounded, measured to add **zero** findings on all four shipped blocks. A bolded SENTENCE is still excluded by SHAPE rather than by counting — any punctuation after the digits ends the run, cased both ways. |
| **D-497** `[E]` | **`m4`. A byte-identical row shipped TWICE**, so `120` counted 120 rules where there were 119, and `--only` matched both — against a governed block that says *"one mutation per PRODUCTION RULE"*. ⇒ deduped, and the property is checked, with a planted duplicate proving the check can see one. |
| **D-498** `[E]` | **`m5`. The FLOOR-WINS degradation is documented at length and enforced by that paragraph alone.** Ceiling-wins survived all 301 tests. `ContributionBound`'s fields are `pub` and `amount_reported` is `pub`, so the path is reachable from the surface this crate's own SDK argument calls the plugin API. |
| **D-499** `[E]` | **Two canary mechanisms, one bug, twice — so the mechanism is now shared rather than re-derived.** `canary_verdict` gained an `expect` count and the sibling rule uses it; re-deriving it had produced the identical *unreachable second clause* defect in two consecutive commits. Six inputs drive it, including the pair form, so `expect` has a subject at something other than its default. |

| **D-500** `[E]` | **🔴 The CRASH/FAIL split turned FIVE MORE rules into honest survivors, all the same shape: a case that calls its own subject and lets the exception escape.** For nineteen rounds the escaping exception WAS the evidence, because a non-zero exit read as a bite; the moment that stopped, every such case became a survivor at once. `measure()` — whose entire design is that an unobtainable figure is an `Unmeasurable`, never a crash — was the stand-in for the cargo-absent guard; the harness's own timeout case let `TimeoutExpired` escape the call it was written to prove catches it. ⇒ **a case owns the exception its subject raises**, named and attributed, and the run continues. |
| **D-501** `[E]` | **And the last two were the canary lesson, on the third and fourth rule beside it.** The duplicate-row check could be deleted because the table is clean, and the label rule's `_find_one` could revert to a raw `find` because no case had an anchor placed where the two differ. ⇒ a canary in the duplicate set; and a probe whose anchor lives ONLY in a table and whose label names a token that lives ONLY in the table region — the single placement where table-aware and raw searching give opposite answers. **Its first version was itself a second occurrence of its own anchor**, so it passed for the wrong reason; the literal is split now. |

**Drift.** `DRB-13` — repairing the bolded-figure comment, I spliced on an index
