### 6ae. ROUND 18 — the class fix, after four rounds of writing better rows (2026-08-04)

Four rounds running, the blocking finding was **a row asserting coverage of
something adjacent to what it actually touches**: `D-458` (a row claiming the
index half of a key was cased), `D-459` (a row claiming a case that was on the
sibling function), `D-470` (a row that mangled a tuple), and now a row labelled
`Accumulator.wanted` whose anchor is the **Emit** push. Each round's remedy was
to write a better row, and **writing is the thing that keeps failing.** Round 18
stops writing rows and starts reading them.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-476** `[E]` | **🔴 `B1`. A row labelled `Accumulator.wanted` mutates the EMIT record, and the field it names survived all 300 tests.** The test it points at reads `c.site == CapSite::Emit`; the accumulator's `wanted` is asserted **only by sign** anywhere in the suite. Replacing it with the emitted value understates by ~4.3 × 10⁹ and makes the two sites indistinguishable — which `report.rs` says is *"exactly why the site is recorded rather than the number alone"*. **Round 17 rewrote this very line** (`D-473` moved the two pushes) and did not ask what reads its fields. ⇒ a case pinning the saturated total, and the row renamed to the record it touches. |
| **D-477** `[E]` | **🔴 THE CLASS FIX: a row's LABEL must name what its ANCHOR touches, checked mechanically.** A code-shaped token in a label — one carrying `_`, an interior capital, or a `.`/`::` — must be reachable from the anchor. A **dotted** token names a member, so both halves must meet on ONE line of the anchor; a **plain** token names the subject, so it may appear in the anchor or in the enclosing definition. **Measured over all 132 shipped rows before it was wired: one finding, and it was `D-476`. Zero false positives.** An English word carries none of those shapes, so the rule cannot fire on prose; a FILENAME is excluded, because `_index.md` looks dotted and is not a member — and reporting it would have been the cry-wolf direction on a correct row. |
| **D-478** `[E]` | **🔴 `B2`. `D-470` fixed one row and left its sibling in the same table — and the sibling is the counter-example to its own guard.** *"The `_index.md` scope row deleted"* still mangles a two-line tuple: the child reaches **62** cases, raises `TypeError`, prints **ZERO** `FAIL` lines, and is reported RED. `D-470`'s guard asks *did the child reach A case*; reaching is not answering. ⇒ **a bite is a FAILING CASE**, and the Rust twin asks whether the **NAMED TEST** failed rather than whether the process exited non-zero. Both directions cased, including the exact shape that defeated the first version: a child that reaches cases, passes them all, then raises. |
| **D-479** `[E]` | **🔴 `B3`. A stale, live, derivable figure inside a governed block — the class the whole gate exists for.** *"(172 lines) and … (142)"* were true at `d3bb441da` and are **207**/**157** at HEAD; they sat **34 lines below** a copy of the same two numbers that IS governed and IS correct, and survived two commits and two review rounds. A third shape — unbolded, parenthesised — that neither `CLAIMS` nor the bolded-figure detector reached. ⇒ rewritten into the shape the rule already reads, so the copy is **governed** rather than merely corrected. |
| **D-480** `[E]` | **`M4`. The bolded-figure detector admitted ONE word.** `**315 passed**` matched; `**302 Rust tests**` did not — a governed figure sitting in a governed block, invisible on **both** arms, because a key that is never matched is neither compared nor reported as surplus. ⇒ `{0,3}` words, and the header block's rust-test phrasing gets a `CLAIMS` row and joins its `must_claim` set. `D-460`'s lesson — *"a checker's scope is a claim, and so is the SHAPE it recognises inside that scope"* — one word further along. |
| **D-481** `[E]` | **`M1`. `D-474`'s three rejected shapes rejected one of them for the wrong reason.** *"An `:end` with no block name"* was written `<!-- actor-hub-figures:end -->`, which fails on the missing SPACE, not the missing name — so the `+` quantifier the row is about stayed unpinned, and so did the `$` anchor. Both were survivors. ⇒ an EMPTY name (`:end  -->`) and a trailing tail. **A rule tested only through data it happens to have is a rule with half a test** — `D-474`'s own sentence, applied to `D-474`. |
| **D-482** `[E]` | **`M2`. The compile-failure guard has two literals and only one had a subject.** Dropping `"could not compile"` was green; dropping `"error[E"` reds. And the uncased half is the only one that catches a mutant that fails to **PARSE** — which is `D-470`'s entire subject, on the Rust twin. Third occurrence of *"two literal forms and only one cased"*. |
| **D-483** `[E]` | **`M3`, disclosed rather than papered over.** `D-471` carried BOTH halves of the prefix state into the tail. The comment half **cannot vary on any conforming document**: `_unsentinelled` requires every scope to end on a line ending ` -->`, and `_scan_line` closes an open comment on any line containing `-->`. Measured: 0 of 11 prefix shapes. **Two individually-correct decisions, the first defeating the second.** ⇒ the code stays (it is correct, and free); its **mutation row and case are RETIRED**, because a row certifying coverage that does not exist is the defect this whole round is about. NV-1 disclosure written beside it. |
| **D-484** `[E]` | **`M5`. `D-462` deleted the word-spelled seam count at the location the finding pointed at and left the copy in the sibling document**, where it contradicts `S-1`..`S-18` on the next line. `rows.rs`'s own module doc: *"A correction sweeps a CONCEPT, not the location the finding pointed at."* ⇒ the count is gone; the governed range carries the fact. |
| **D-485** `[E]` | **`m1`/`m2`. The tail rule's blockquote tolerance and its REACH were both uncased.** `[ \t>]*` had no subject — every probe wrote its table and heading at column 0, while the slice-board block is a blockquote from end to end, so the form its own tail would need was the untested one (third occurrence). And the scan could be restricted to the first block, or to one of thirteen claim shapes, and stay green. ⇒ a two-block probe on a non-first shape, and blockquoted terminators in both forms. |
| **D-486** `[E]` | **`m3`. `DR-4`..`DR-11` each named TWO unrelated entries in one document.** `DR-11` was *"§9.2's three storage layers written from Photon and Orleans"* and *"the `D-459` fix written from the verifier's phrasing"*. **An id that resolves to two things is not an id.** ⇒ the younger BUILD series moves to `DRB-n`; the design prose keeps the older claim on the prefix. |
| **D-487** `[E]` | **`m4`. An acceptance test asserted a decoupling claim that is false as written.** *"None of those words appear anywhere in `crates/actor-hub/src/`"* — measured: `qi` five times, `cultivation` twice, one of them **live code** (`QuantityTable::assign(&["hp", "qi", "speed"])`). The decoupling the test proves is real; the sentence asserting it was not. ⇒ narrowed to what is true and measurable: the hub names none of them as a **concept**. |

**What round 18 did NOT do.** `m5`'s arithmetic — `D-469` says *"all three pairs
of `check_modifier`"* where three conditions make **two** adjacent pairs, and
*"all five swaps"* counts across two functions. The verifier measured that the
two pairs with no row **also red**, so there is no behavioural hole; the row's
arithmetic is corrected in place and nothing else changed.

| **D-488** `[E]` | **`file-ceiling-gate` fired a second time, at 433 lines, and the split repeated the registry split's exact defect in reverse.** The five tests about what a `Capped` record SAYS moved to `fold_survivors/capping.rs` — and the first attempt shipped a `use super::{fixture, p, q}` missing `deriv`, caught by the compiler, plus an unused `OpKind` and **one mutation row whose test name was not requalified**, which the Rust sweep reported GREEN immediately. Every one caught by RUNNING it: the compiler, `cargo build --tests`, and the sweep. |

| **D-489** `[E]` | **🔴 The FAILING-CASE rule immediately found four rules that had read RED for eighteen rounds on the strength of a TRACEBACK.** Deleting the cargo-absent guard, the empty-id guard, the missing-end-marker guard or the driver's timeout handler makes the child **raise** rather than fail a case: the harness saw a non-zero exit and called it a bite, while not one case had disagreed. `run_rust` was given a guard for exactly this on the Rust side that morning; the Python side had nothing. ⇒ **a self-test that dies has failed, and says so in its own vocabulary** — one wrapper per gate, turning an escaping exception into a `FAIL` line. All four now red for the right reason. |
| **D-490** `[E]` | **`D-467`'s compile-failure guard was measured UNREACHABLE the same day it was written, and removed rather than kept.** `named_failed` — *did the NAMED TEST fail* — is strictly stronger: a mutant that does not build cannot print `test <name> ... FAILED`, so every input the `BROKE` guard would catch is caught one line above it. Its two rows and two cases went with it, replaced by three shapes the stronger question separates: a mutant that does not build, one that breaks a DIFFERENT test, and a `--exact` name that matches nothing. **A check that cannot fail is worse than no check, and that is true of a check written this morning.** |

The verdict is now a function driven on four injected inputs — the same injectability `run_rust` needed two rules earlier, for the same reason. Six of six label-rule mutations RED. |

**Drift.** `DRB-12` — the label rule was designed, measured against the shipped

### 6ad. ROUND 17 — a row claiming coverage, one row after the row about rows claiming coverage (2026-08-04)

Round 16 recorded `D-458`: *"the row claiming the coverage was itself the miss."*
`D-459` was written in the same commit, by the same pass, and did it again — it
asserted that `check_derivation`'s source-before-target precedence already had a
case. That case is on `check_modifier`, which writes the same two lines again.
**Three of `check_derivation`'s five adjacent precedence pairs were survivors.**

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-469** `[E]` | **🔴 `B1`. `D-459` claimed coverage that does not exist, and three of five precedence pairs were uncased.** Measured: swapping source/target in `check_derivation` is GREEN while the identical swap in `check_modifier` four lines up is RED — the case is on the twin, and the row read the twin as if it were the subject. Also GREEN: source-quantity before layer, and divisor before bound. **`RowRefusal` is what substrate §7 calls the whole product of a refusal**, and `D-459`'s own text says reporting the second of two is a wrong answer, not a cosmetic one. ⇒ the fix is not another pair-case, which is how this got here: **a row violating EVERY condition, then each repaired in turn**, each step naming the reason that must surface. One test pins all five adjacent pairs of `check_derivation` and a sibling pins both of `check_modifier` (three conditions, two pairs — this row first said three, and a round-18 verifier measured the arithmetic), so the two functions are no longer distinguishable only by which one a reader happens to open. All seven swaps now RED across the two functions, none by compile error — including the two the mutation table has no row for, so the chain tests pin the TOTAL order rather than only the rowed pairs. |
| **D-470** `[E]` | **`M1`. `D-467` asked *"did this mutant red for the RIGHT reason"* of the RUST half and not of its twin — and one shipped row was already answering it wrongly.** *"The 6-BUILD scope row deleted"* removed the first line of a two-line tuple and left the continuation dangling, so the child died in **python's parser** and not one case ran: counted RED, so **`106/106` was 105 verdicts and one artifact.** ⇒ two fixes, because there are two defects: the row deletes both lines, and `_mutate_and_run` now requires the child to have **reached a case at all** — a self-test prints one `ok`/`FAIL` line per case, so the test is "did it get that far", not a list of exception names, which would be the enumeration this file keeps being caught by. Parity gained the property. |
| **D-471** `[E]` | **`M2`. The tail scan carried the fence half of the prefix state and dropped the comment half — two lines below the call that passes BOTH for the block.** Measured on one document shape: a fence opening in the block and closing in the tail gives 0 findings; the same shape with a comment gives **1** — a figure inside `<!-- … -->` refusing the commit, from the hook that fires repo-wide. **And the half that WAS carried had no case**: replacing the whole expression with `None` survived, while being load-bearing (dropping it turns a correct document into a false positive). One half carried, its twin dropped, and neither cased — in the rule round 16 added to close a hole. |
| **D-472** `[E]` | **`m1`. One of three rounding modes was pinned.** The negative direction separates truncation from flooring and the ceiling direction is caught, but **half-rounding is neither**, and both data points of the divisor case — `10 000 × 1/3` and `10 000 × 333/1000` — are invariant under it: one rounds down either way, the other is exact. So `(n + d/2)/d` survived all 296 tests, and the test asserting the divisor's whole reason for existing was the one that could not see it. ⇒ `10 000 × 2/3 = 6 666.67`, which half-rounding moves. |
| **D-473** `[E]` | **`m2`. `FoldReport.capped` is public output and `emit` writes its two records deliberately; the sequence was asserted by nothing.** Which record a reader meets first is the difference between *"the accumulation overflowed and was then clamped"* and *"the value was clamped, and separately the accumulator says it overflowed."* |
| **D-474** `[E]` | **`m3`. The sentinel assertion could be weakened to accept any HTML comment, because its only case reverted the DATA to a heading — and a heading fails either form.** *"NAMED"* is the whole point of the rule. ⇒ the predicate is extracted so a case can drive it with scopes that violate it, and three shapes are asserted rejected: a bare comment, an `:end` with no block name, and a heading. **A rule tested only through data it happens to have is a rule with half a test.** |
| **D-475** `[E]` | **`m4`, corrected rather than left standing — and the correction was itself wrong.** The previous commit's message said **41**; this row said **43**; a round-18 measurement on a clean tree, three consecutive runs, says **44**. **Three statements of one figure, three different numbers, in the row whose subject is that the figure is ungoverned.** ⇒ it is not stated anywhere from here on, which is the only stable answer for a count that changes on every commit that adds a case. Nothing in the tree governs that number — no document states it — which is exactly why it went stale: it is a figure outside every checker's scope, which is the sentence this round's own slice board is built around. It is not added to a governed block, because a count of self-test cases changes on every commit that adds one; it is recorded here as wrong and not repeated. |

**Drift.** `DRB-11` — the fix for `D-459` was written from the verifier's phrasing
(*"the sibling source-before-target pair has a case"*) without opening
`check_derivation` to confirm which function the case was on. The claim was true
of the twin. That is the same reading-instead-of-measuring the whole round is
about, committed inside the row recording it.

### 6ac. ROUND 16 — the parity mechanism was satisfied by its own table (2026-08-03/04)

Round 15 answered the twin-guard class with a PARITY table. Round 16 measured it:
**seven of its eight witnesses could not fail**, because it searched the whole file
— which contains the table — so every witness was present whether or not the guard
it names existed. Its row-check was `any` across both halves where its own comment
promised each. It detected no new asymmetry, and the only thing it detected
reliably was a reworded comment. The mechanism written to end "one half hardened
and not its twin" was itself one half of a mechanism.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-452** `[E]` | **🔴 `B1`. The unpaired-opener fix had a DOCUMENT-wide predicate and a ONE-FENCE subject.** *"Is there an unpaired opener anywhere"* → mask EVERY fence marker in the text, in all three consumers at once. So one unterminated fence turned every correctly-paired code block in the same document into live prose: three measured refusals on the shipped documents, one of them the *"its end sentinel has probably moved UP"* misdiagnosis this masking was written to eliminate — reproduced by its own fix, from the hook that fires on `SESSION_HANDOFF` for all 47 services. ⇒ the mask reaches from the opener DOWN; lines above it are paired by construction, which is what makes the opener unpaired. Measured after: the three shapes give 0, 0, 0. A residual remains and is stated rather than hidden — an unterminated fence ABOVE a fenced example of the sentinel still refuses, because the example's own opener closes the unterminated one and no note is derivable from a balanced block. A `_unpaired_note` on the missing-figures path was written, measured unable to fire on the shape it was written for, and DELETED rather than shipped as a claim. |
| **D-453** `[E]` | **`M2`/`M7`. The parity table read the ORIGINAL file while the child under test is the mutated COPY beside it.** `_read(SCRIPTS / "gate-bite-harness.py")`, not `__file__` — so every witness was searched in unmutated bytes, and `GATE_BITE_MUTATING`, whose whole job is to let a self-mutating child skip the anchor its own mutation removed, was inert: identical verdicts with and without it. ⇒ `_read(Path(__file__))`. **And only its CODE**: `_outside_tables` already answers "is this occurrence data or rule" for anchors, and the check that needed it most did not use it. Measured before: 7 of 8 witnesses silent on the guard they witness; the one exception was caught only because its witness spans a newline the table's escaped copy does not. |
| **D-454** `[E]` | **`M3`. `any(tok in rows for tok in (gate_tok, rust_tok))` where the comment says *"aimed at each half"*.** Three of four properties were rowed on one half only, and the tokens mis-identified in both directions — the Rust baseline HAS two rows and the derived token matched neither, so the check reported a pass for a reason unrelated to whether the row exists. ⇒ the row token is written per half, separately from the witness, and both must be present. It bit immediately: **six real gaps** on the first run. |
| **D-455** `[E]` | **`M4`. `run_rust` was missing three guards its twin has, and the driver a fourth.** No null-mutation check (a harness that silently stopped mutating would have reported every Rust row red and looked like success), a raw `src.count` where the twin uses the table-aware `_find_one`, and — worst — **`gate-self-tests.py::run_all`, the driver the pre-commit hook actually runs over all 16 gates, had no timeout handler at all**: one hanging gate aborted the hook with a raw traceback and the other fifteen were reported by nothing. The mutation harness carries that guard and its comment describes this exact failure, about itself. ⇒ all four written, each with a case and a row. **One listed asymmetry is deliberately NOT closed, and is stated rather than quietly dropped:** `run_rust` has no `no_cargo` parameter, because `--no-cargo` exists to strip cargo from a PYTHON child's environment and this half runs cargo itself — giving it the flag would mean *“run the Rust mutations without Rust”*. The degrade path it does need is the `shutil.which` skip it already has, exercised under a stripped PATH. |
| **D-456** `[E]` | **`M4`, the derived half. A four-row hand-written table detects no NEW one-sided guard — so one of its four properties is now asked of the TREE.** `unbounded_children` walks every function in every script this harness has a table for, plus the driver, and reports a `subprocess.run` with no `timeout=` or a spawning function with no `TimeoutExpired` handler. **Measured when written: FOUR such functions**, including `_rust_dirty` in the harness itself, `_cargo_passed` in the figures gate (an unbounded `cargo test` from a repo-wide hook) and two `git` children in the citation gate. All four bounded. The remaining three parity properties stay an enumeration, stated plainly rather than implied to be more. |
| **D-457** `[E]` | **`M5`. `D-448`'s *"one check closes both"* was FALSE, and the row said otherwise.** The surplus is keyed by measurement KEY: a SECOND occurrence of a key the set already names is not surplus, and the missing-arm is already satisfied by the first — so moving the sentinel above a repeated figure was still zero findings, for every key in every block, because every key is already in its set. ⇒ the closure is POSITIONAL: the sentinel closes a stretch of prose, so a governed figure between it and the next heading is an orphan. **First attempt cry-wolfed on the correct document** — ending the tail at the heading alone reported the slice board's `**14 passed**` and `**7 passed**`, the counts each slice measured AT ITS CLOSE. This project writes current state in prose and history in a table, so the tail ends at the next heading **or the next table row**. Reproduction after: 1 where it was 0; quoted and fenced history silent. |
| **D-458** `[E]` | **`M6`. A proven Rust survivor: `order_key`'s derivation index.** `usize::MAX / 2 + i` → `- i` reverses two derivations sharing a layer and a plugin, and all 294 tests stayed green. `Explanation.contributions` order is public output. **The `RUST_MUTATIONS` row asserted the index half was cased** — *"Layer and index each had a case; the plugin did not"* — so the row claiming the coverage was itself the miss. ⇒ a probe that passes clean and reds on the mutant, and the row corrected to say what is true. |
| **D-459** `[E]` | **`minor 5`. `check_derivation`'s layer-before-divisor precedence had no case** while its sibling source-before-target does. A row violating both reported `ZeroDivisor` where the contract reports `UndeclaredFoldLayer`. A refusal REASON is the whole product of substrate §7, so this is a wrong answer, not a cosmetic one. |
| **D-460** `[E]` | **`minor 1`. The detector's SHAPE was the shape of its own case.** `\*\*\d[\d\s]*\*\*` matched `**7**` and nothing else — not `**7 widgets**`, `**7.5**`, `**7,000**` — so narrowing it back to `\d+` survived. *A checker's scope is a claim; so is the SHAPE it recognises inside that scope* — `M1`'s own lesson, one level down, inside the fix for it. ⇒ widened, and it immediately found **two ungoverned figures in the shipped blocks**: `cargo doc` **0 warnings**, a claim a round-12 verifier measured FALSE and which was corrected in the handoff and left standing here, and `` `dp-kernel --lib` **315 passed** ``, whose phrasing the rule misses by one word. Both fixed; the rule now reads both phrasings. |
| **D-461** `[E]` | **`minor 2`, decided rather than patched.** A bolded YEAR is reported. Exempting one would be an untested carve-out in the rule whose entire finding was an untested carve-out, and the block's own headline says every bolded figure in it is emitted by a checker — so the remedy is to unbold it. A DATE does not match: the hyphen ends the run. Both directions cased, eight shapes. |
| **D-462** `[E]` | **`minor 4`. The governed block's headline was FALSE and no detector could reach it.** *"Every figure in this block is emitted by…"* — and `Eight new seams` is a current-state count spelled as a WORD, which no detector reaches without crying wolf on ordinary prose. ⇒ **the claim is narrowed to match the detector exactly**: every BOLDED figure. The word-count is deleted; the governed `S-1`..`S-18` already carries the fact. A claim wider than its mechanism is the defect, and narrowing the claim is a fix as legitimate as widening the mechanism — this one is honest, the other would have been a guess. |
| **D-463** `[E]` | **`M8`. `INDENT_CODE` and `QUOTE_PREFIX` each accept two literal forms and only the space form had a subject.** Dropping `\t` from either left the suite green — a tab-indented mention of the sentinel refused the commit, which is the cry-wolf `D-451` exists to close, in the OTHER of the two indent forms, in the row whose headline is *"markdown has two literal forms and this file handled one."* |
| **D-464** `[E]` | **`M9`. *"Each scope's end is a NAMED sentinel"* had no mechanism** — reverting `_index.md`'s scope end to a heading left the entire suite green. The property was made true by editing data. ⇒ asserted on the shape of every scope's end. |
| **D-466** `[E]` | **The widened detector still missed the case that motivated it, and the WRAP tolerance is why.** These documents wrap; `contract_substrate_lines` in this same file has carried `\s*
?>?\s*` from the start and its comment says why. `**0
> warnings**` in the slice board — the block's own copy of the claim a round-12 verifier measured FALSE, corrected in the header block and left standing here — was invisible to the shape widened one hour earlier to catch exactly this. **Two decisions, each correct, the later one defeating the earlier**: this file's own name for the class, committed by the fix for the class. ⇒ the tolerance is carried, cased in both wrap forms, and rowed; the slice board's claim is corrected to state no figure, as the header's was. |
| **D-467** `[E]` | **A Rust mutant that does not COMPILE reddens its test and proves nothing — and that question was being asked from OUTSIDE, once, by hand.** Round 16's verifier instrumented the sweep to check that none of the eleven reds was a build failure. It is the right question, and asking it by hand is exactly what the red-baseline guard next to it exists to stop: *“RED” becomes a statement about the type checker rather than about the rule*. ⇒ a non-compiling mutant is reported `BROKE` and counted as a SURVIVOR, in the same direction as a red baseline, a drifted anchor and a timeout. |
| **D-468** `[E]` | **`file-ceiling-gate` fired at 445 lines against a 400 ceiling, which is the moment it exists to create.** The survivor register gains one test per proven survivor, so it only ever gets longer and no size would otherwise oblige anyone to split it. ⇒ split by SUBJECT: the five checks that refuse a ROW before the fold sees it moved to `tests/fold_survivors/registry.rs`. **The first attempt shipped a silent double-compile:** a bare `mod registry;` in a file that IS the crate root resolves to the SIBLING `tests/registry.rs` — an auto-discovered target of its own — so the binary ran **22** tests instead of 13 and the five moved ones never ran at all. Caught by counting, not by reading. `#[path]` names the file the split created. |
| **D-465** `[E]` | **The sweep's own survivors, found by running it rather than by trusting it.** Round 16's first sweep: 1 survivor of 101 — the gate half's anchor search could revert to a raw `text.find`, mutating the TABLE ROW that names a rule instead of the rule, with the child then running unmutated code and staying green. Uncasable because `_mutate_and_run` read `SCRIPTS / f"{gate}.py"` unconditionally; `root=` makes it drivable, as `rows=`/`root=` did for the Rust half two rules earlier. Second sweep: 3 survivors of 104, **all three in the rule written to close `M4`** — one planted probe missing BOTH a timeout and a handler is answered by either arm, so deleting either left it reported by the other, and the subject set had no floor so emptying it printed *"none of the 0 gates"*. ⇒ two probes, one per arm, each carrying the guard the other lacks, plus a floor and a silent-when-clean case. |

**The drift this round records.** `DRB-9` — `_unpaired_note` was written, wired into three
messages, and only then measured against the shape it was written for, where it cannot fire.
It survived two of the three sites for one edit cycle on the strength of reading. `DRB-10` —
the derived unbounded-child rule shipped its own three survivors: written, cased, believed,
and only the sweep disagreed. Both are the same near-miss: **a case that passes is not a case
that can fail**, and the only thing that tells them apart is running the mutation.

`D-443`..`D-446` closed the twin-guard class. These are the other six findings,
recorded as OPEN in the previous commit and discharged in this one.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|

An unterminated fence inside a governed block therefore resolved its markers, blanked the block anyway, tripped `must_claim`, and refused the commit saying *"its end sentinel has probably moved UP"* about a sentinel nobody had touched. ⇒ an unpaired opener is literal text in both places. **The reasoning is a comparison, not a preference:** blanking refuses with a diagnosis that points at the wrong file; not blanking reports *"claims 281, measured 294"* about a figure that IS in the document and DOES disagree — actionable, and true. The round-12 case asserting the opposite is flipped, with the comparison written down, because it was decided before the refusal path existed to compare against. |
| **D-448** `[E]` | **`O-R15-3`/`O-R15-2`. `must_claim` was a LOWER BOUND while its sibling enumeration in the same file is re-derived from the tree.** Emptying any scope's set left the suite green, and a governed figure ADDED to a block was never named — which is exactly what leaves a block's tail unprotected when the sentinel later moves above it (79 % of the slice board, 60 % of the header). ⇒ the set must be EXACT: a surplus is reported the day a figure appears, so the enumeration cannot silently lag the document. One check closes both. |
| **D-449** `[E]` | **`O-R15-4`. Three rounds found a bolded figure inside a governed block that no rule reads; the last was fixed by DELETING the figures, which is an instance fix with no detector.** ⇒ any `**N**` inside a governed block (after blanking, so a quoted or fenced number is not a claim) that falls in no `CLAIMS` span is a finding. Reports zero today. **The row first said *"two at the previous commit"* and that was wrong about WHICH commit** — a round-16 sweep measured 0 at `61fbcdc4e`, 0 at `4c0588bdd` and 2 (`**4**`, `**1**`) at `3f0304038`/`74b91807b`: a coverage figure asserted from memory in the row about figures asserted from memory. |
| **D-450** `[E]` | **`O-R15-5`. `_child_env`'s remaining assertion was vacuous on the machine CI runs it on — three lines below the one fixed for exactly that reason.** ⇒ asserted against a SYNTHETIC PATH carrying two fake cargos and one clean directory, so it holds regardless of what the host has. |
| **D-451** `[E]` | **`O-R15-6`. Markdown has TWO literal forms and this file handled one.** A sentinel shown in a 4-space INDENTED block was reported as a duplicate and refused the commit — the fenced twin having been fixed a round earlier. ⇒ indented lines are blanked in the marker scan too. Also: `_index.md`'s block now ends on a NAMED sentinel, so *"each scope's end is a named sentinel"* is true as written rather than true of three quarters; the `>` tolerance added last round had **no subject** (every shipped marker is at column 0, so removing it changed nothing) and now has both directions asserted; and `_line_bounds` — defined, never called — is gone. |

### 6z. ROUND 14 — the verifier named the shape: every fix was the last one with a token swapped (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-432** `[META]` | **🔴 THE FINDING THAT MATTERS IS THE SHAPE, and a verifier stated it outright: *every BLOCKING and most MAJOR findings are the previous fix with one token substituted*.** The END marker was hardened and the START marker was not · the DUPLICATE route to premature termination was closed and the MOVE route left open · the INSTANCE of a red baseline was fixed and the CLASS left with no detector · the leftover exclusion was added and left unguarded · the writes were counted at ONE call site and not across the run. **Five rounds of fixing the instance in front of me.** This round is written against the classes, and the entries below say which class each closes. |
| **D-433** `[META]` | **🔴 BLOCKING. The mutation harness could not tell *"every rule bites"* from *"the suite is already broken"* — and CI is its only automatic runner.** It never ran an UNMUTATED baseline: `_mutate_and_run` checks that the copy DIFFERS, never that the original is GREEN. Reproduced by re-introducing `D-427`'s defect: **47 of 47 red, harness rc 0**, with three semantically NULL mutations also red. `D-427` was found by a human running null mutations by hand, and **nothing replaced the human**. ⇒ `baseline_is_green` runs the unmutated self-test first, costs one child per gate, and returns a NEGATIVE count so the summary stops saying *"those rules have no case"* about a broken suite. |
| **D-434** `[E]` | **🔴 BLOCKING. `_scope_span`'s START marker had NEITHER of the two rules the END marker got.** A raw `find`, so documenting `### 6-BUILD` in an inline code span moved the block 260 lines up — and the message then blamed the END marker for being duplicated, pointing the reader at the wrong file with a remedy (delete a table header) that would have been wrong. ⇒ **one function, `_marker_hits`, for both markers**: fence-blanked text so a fenced EXAMPLE is a mention, line-start so prose and inline spans are mentions, exactly-once so a stray copy is loud — and a raw fallback so an unterminated fence, which blanks everything after it, cannot refuse a whole document. |
| **D-435** `[E]` | **The exactly-once rule and the line-start rule each introduced a FRESH cry-wolf**, in a file whose docstring counts four prior cry-wolves as its worst recurring defect. The window was the rest of the FILE, so a fenced example 900 lines below the block it supposedly ends made **the decision record unable to show its own sentinel**; `> ` is not whitespace, so the sentinel written inside the RUN-STATE's blockquote — where it belongs — was not recognised; and `| # | Slice |` is a REPEATING structural shape, so adding a second slice table refused the commit. ⇒ fence-blanked search, a blockquote-tolerant prefix, and **that block ends on a sentinel too**. Every sentinel is now NAMED per block, because one shared marker makes *"exactly once"* meaningless the moment a second block wants one. |
| **D-436** `[E]` | **And the LAST route to a silently shortened scope: MOVING the single sentinel up.** The empty-block rule catches only TOTAL collapse; one paragraph higher leaves a claim behind, so it stays quiet while everything below goes ungoverned — the defect the sentinel replaced, reached by a third road after the incidental-`---` road and the duplicate road were closed. ⇒ each block DECLARES the figures it must state (`must_claim`), so the question stops being *"did anything match"* and becomes *"did THESE match"*. It supersedes the empty-block rule, which is deleted rather than left as a rule that can no longer fail. |
| **D-437** `[META]` | **🔴 BLOCKING. `--self-test` still wrote the shipped Rust sources — and the case certifying otherwise was structurally blind.** It counted only its OWN `run_rust` call and passed `write=nowrite`, so `(write or _write)` short-circuits and the monkeypatched writer is unreachable **by construction**: a per-call assertion certifying a run the pre-commit hook never performs. ⇒ the round trip is proved on a TEMP file — which also gives `_write`'s CRLF re-application its first case — and a second case spies `_write` AND `Path.write_bytes` to require zero writes under `crates/` by any route. |
| **D-438** `[META]` | **🔴 BLOCKING. All five rows of the self-mutation table were vacuous, and the fix for that was itself unguarded.** `D-428` excluded this file's own `.bite-` copy from the leftover check; removing that exclusion was a SURVIVOR, and with it removed a semantically-null mutation goes red again — so the remedy for *"19 of 19 harness mutations surviving"* could return to being incapable of failing and nothing would notice. ⇒ a mutation row for the exclusion itself. |
| **D-439** `[E]` | **`--no-cargo`'s control was machine-dependent and therefore vacuous in CI.** The case asserted cargo's ABSENCE from the child's PATH — already true on the machine CI runs this on, so in the only automatic runner it could not fail. ⇒ a sentinel environment proves the value TRAVELLED, and a separate case proves the helper strips what it claims. Also fixed: the Rust baseline probe I added with `D-433` called cargo unconditionally and raised `FileNotFoundError` on every machine without Rust — a degrade-safety defect **inside the degrade-safety round's own new guard**. |
| **D-440** `[E]` | **`CHILD_TIMEOUT_S` was configured and never caught**, so the documented local invocation aborted at mutation 11 of 42 with a raw traceback and **47 mutations never ran, reported by nothing**. It had no case and no mutation row; both now exist, and a timed-out child is a finding about that mutation rather than the end of the run. |
| **D-441** `[E]` | **A tenth Rust survivor: `order_key`'s middle component is the SUBMITTING PLUGIN, and nothing tested it.** `M-13` states the order as (fold layer, plugin, submission index); layer and index had cases, the plugin did not, so a constant survived the whole suite. **One plugin cannot show a key that sorts by plugin** — the fixture has to submit from two with submission order inverted against plugin order. Pinned and bitten. |

Two bolded figures inside the governed handoff block were ungoverned five lines above the sentence claiming every figure there is emitted by a checker — the **third** recurrence of that class in that block, in text that itself replaced a sentence a verifier had already falsified. ⇒ the counts are gone rather than corrected: nothing measures them, and a figure with no measurement rule goes stale by construction. |

| # | Decision |
|---|---|

DELETION was never the defect — PREMATURE TERMINATION was, and the sentinel inherited it exactly.** `_scope_span` takes the FIRST end marker, so a SECOND sentinel truncates the block precisely as the first incidental `---` did: measured on the shipped handoff, one extra marker mid-block takes the scope from **five governed figures to one**, and four seeded-stale figures below it produce **zero** findings where the control produces four. `D-415` said *"a sentinel is deletable too, but deleting it fails the both-markers check LOUDLY, which is the whole difference"* — and the difference it named was not the one that mattered. ⇒ the marker must occur **exactly once** after the start, and is a terminator only at the **start of a line**: `_scope_span` reads raw text, so writing it inside a fenced example or an inline code span — **which is what documenting a marker looks like, and what this record does** — silently truncated the scope. A fenced one still counts and is reported as a duplicate: loud, which is the safe way to be wrong. |

There was still no case for a second marker mid-block. **A fix and its case written in the same sitting share the same blind spot; the round that names the blind spot is not exempt from it.** |
