That is `D-411`'s own headline (`red = True` in `run_rust`) one function up, in the commit that records it. ⇒ the same guard, and cases for both. |
| **D-418** `[E]` | **`_fence_state` had no comment tracking, so round 11's comment-ordering fix protected one scanner and left the other defeated by the identical input.** ONE ```` ``` ```` line inside an HTML comment anywhere above a block flipped the prefix scan into a fence that never closed. Both directions measured on the real documents: a silently BLINDED slice board, and — worse — a **refusal on `SESSION_HANDOFF` whose markers are perfectly fine**, from the pre-commit hook that fires on it for all 47 services. ⇒ one `_scan_line`, used by both. **A rule written twice is a rule with half a test** — which was `D-413`'s finding, in this same pair of functions, one round earlier. |
| **D-419** `[META]` | **`--self-test` rewrote the shipped Rust sources 30 times, on the pre-commit path, on every commit.** The hook runs `gate-self-tests`, which runs this harness's `--self-test`, which drives `run_rust` three times — and `run_rust` writes in place. **The incident the Python half mutates a COPY to avoid was on the automatic path for every commit across 47 services.** ⇒ the writer is injectable and the self-test injects a no-op. Then the *first* version of that fix keyed the restore on `write is None`, so a mutation of the injection itself wrote the file and skipped putting it back — leaving `fold.rs` modified after a fully-green sweep, the same class again. ⇒ the restore is keyed on **content**: restore when the bytes differ, always. |
| **D-420** `[E]` | **`--no-cargo` was never checked to reach the child, in a case whose own comment said it was.** The case called `_child_env(True)` inside its own fake runner and asserted on that — testing the helper, not the forwarding — so all three plumbing mutations survived. Measured non-equivalence: **0.37 s with the flag, 38 s without**, a 100× effect nothing detected. ⇒ the child invocation is spied at `subprocess.run`, and the cargo argv is asserted whole, which also closes the survivors that dropped `-p actor-hub`, `--exact` and the test name. |
| **D-421** `[E]` | **The `ESCAPE_DOCS` self-derivation could not see 2 of its own 10 entries.** The ternary made the specs branch **directory-only**, so the two flat `docs/specs/2026-08-02-item-*.md` files were unreachable by the assertion written to police the list — and a probe one directory over, or dated a day later, was default-uncovered. **A pair of date-prefixed globs is an enumeration wearing a wildcard.** ⇒ the derivation walks `docs/**/*.md`; a seeded probe in `docs/03_planning/` is now caught. |
| **D-422** `[META]` | **19 of 19 mutations of the harness's own new code survived, because `--self-test` drives every helper with an INJECTED runner** and therefore cannot reach any rule about the real invocation. Survivors included `_write` ignoring `like` — **the BLOCKING CRLF-clobber of the previous round, restorable with the suite green**. ⇒ the harness now has a mutation table **for itself**, which required teaching it that a `*MUTATIONS` literal is DATA: a file that mutates itself finds every anchor twice, once in the rule and once in the row naming it, and reported drift on rules that had not moved. |
| **D-423** `[META]` | **Smaller, each measured.** `_claimable`'s offset preservation is load-bearing for the span exclusion and had **no case** — blanking to `""` silently exempts every escape after the first blanked character. A TILDE fence may carry tildes in its info string (CommonMark forbids backticks only in a BACKTICK fence's), so `~~~ ~diagram~` was not a fence and its contents were live claims: cry-wolf on a shape the spec allows. The handoff's *"clippy clean · `cargo doc` **0 warnings**"* is **FALSE** for the crate set named in the same sentence — measured **4** doc warnings and **1** clippy warning, none of them this round's, in the sentence that claims every figure in the block is emitted by a checker. And the hook's trigger named three documents while the rule now reads thirteen. |

### 6w. ROUND 11 — the mutation harness dirtied the tree on SUCCESS (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-405** `[E]` | **🔴 BLOCKING. `--rust` rewrote three source files end to end on a clean, fully-green run, and then misreported its own refusal as a mutation result.** `Path.write_text` opens in text mode, so restoring `\n` content wrote `\r\n`: every `--rust` run modified `fold.rs`, `registry.rs` and `rows.rs` in their entirety. **The harness committed the exact incident its module docstring says it was shaped by** — *"leaving two `if False:` mutations in the working tree"* — on the SUCCESS path rather than the interrupted one, and the dirty-file guard added in the same commit then refused the NEXT run because of it. ⇒ match on newline-normalised text, restore from the original **bytes**. The first attempt made both sides byte-exact and four `\n`-written anchors immediately stopped matching the CRLF crate files — reported as drift, the harness telling the truth about a defect the fix had just introduced. **And the refusal sentinel WAS the survivor count**: `run_rust` returned `2`, `main` printed *"2 Rust mutation(s) SURVIVED"* for a run in which nothing executed. ⇒ `(survivors, refusal)`, two answers because they are two questions. |
| **D-406** `[META]` | **The CI wiring `D-399` describes was never done.** `gates.yml` is untouched by the commit that records it: the step still carries *"this runner has no Rust toolchain"* — the sentence `D-399` calls the defect — still says *"all 29 mutations"* against a table of 49, runs the harness **without `--no-cargo`**, and has **no `--rust` step at all**. Repo-wide, `--no-cargo` appeared in exactly one place outside the harness's own argparse: a prose sentence in `D-399`. **That is `_fence_state`'s defect one level up — defined, given a parameter, and never wired — inside the commit whose message names it.** ⇒ `--no-cargo` on the gate step, and a separate `rust-mutations` job with a toolchain, because the Rust half needs the opposite environment from the gate half. |
| **D-407** `[E]` | **The escape rule read three files and the range lives in eleven.** Round 10's span fix was correct and its REACH was not. Replaying the blanket replace file by file across `docs/` measured **eight more documents** that carry the range and stayed GREEN — among them `2026-08-02-actor-hub.md` and `2026-08-02-seams-and-triggers.md`, **files this same gate already opens** to measure two other figures. *"Blind in two of three files"* was the round-10 headline; three was never the universe, it was an enumerated list, which is NV-3. ⇒ `ESCAPE_DOCS`, and — because a list is default-uncovered — a `--self-test` that **re-derives it from the tree** and reds if any `docs/{specs,plans}/2026-08-02-*` document cites the range without being on it. |
| **D-408** `[E]` | **A moved END marker was silent in both directions.** `_scope_span` reported a missing START marker and treated a missing END as *"to end of file"*: inserting a `---` inside the handoff block silently truncates the scope (measured: a stale figure with a rule above it gives **zero** findings), and deleting the `---` entirely turns the block into the whole 1.18 MB file — mass cry-wolf for the figures, total blindness for the escape rule, whose exempt span then covers the document. **A scope with one asserted end and one guessed end is half a scope.** ⇒ both markers required, and a block that RESOLVES but contains no governed figure is itself a finding. The self-test asserted that for the real documents; production did not, so it held only at the moment someone ran the self-test. |
| **D-409** `[E]` | **Five fence shapes, one of them CRY-WOLF.** Counting every ```` ``` ```` as a toggle broke: a four-backtick fence **documenting** a three-backtick one — the standard markdown idiom — which exposed its contents as live claims; a `~~~` line inside a ``` block; a fence marker inside an HTML COMMENT, which flipped the fence AND stopped `-->` from ever clearing the comment so **both flags stuck and the rest of the block went permanently blind**; an inline ```` ```code``` ```` span at the start of a line; and a closing fence carrying text. ⇒ a marker is DELIMITED, not counted: a fence closes only on the same character, at least as long, **with no info string** (CommonMark); an info string containing the fence character means the line is an inline span; and the comment state is tested FIRST. The comment case is the adjacent-decision shape at its purest — two states, each correct alone, the ORDER between them defeating both. |
| **D-410** `[E]` | **The seam range was governed as a LIVE figure and documented as a FIXED segment, so the gate was scheduled to cry wolf on a dated trigger.** `CLAIMS` required `S-11`..`S-18` to equal the register's head while `D-398` certifies that same string as a permanent historical fact. Measured by seeding `S-19`: **two findings, on two sentences this record calls correct.** ⇒ the pattern follows the criterion instead of contradicting it — `S-1`..`S-N`, first-id-anchored, which is what *"names the whole record and tracks its head"* means — and the two current-state blocks now state the register's range rather than the round's segment. **A criterion that the code contradicts is not a criterion; it is a sentence.** |
| **D-411** `[META]` | **13 of 15 mutations of round 10's own new harness code survived.** Of the five things its commit lists — `--no-cargo`, `--rust`, the dirty refusal, the timeout, the applied-mutation check — exactly ONE had a case. The sharpest survivor: `red = True` in `run_rust`, which means **the harness could have reported every Rust mutation RED unconditionally with its own self-test green** — making *"all 9 red"* unfalsifiable by the mechanism that produced it. ⇒ cases for the reddened/surviving/refused paths, the byte-for-byte restore, and both directions of `--no-cargo`. **A harness that verifies other code and not itself is the regress this file said it stops at, one step in the wrong direction.** |
| **D-412** `[E]` | **A tenth Rust survivor, at the boundary the file next to it exists to guard.** `min > max` weakened to `>=` was green: an EXACT bound — `{min: 7, max: 7}` — is legal by `ContributionBound`'s own doc (*"inclusive on both ends"*) and folds correctly, but the only case used `{10, 5}`, strictly greater, the interesting half. **`fold_survivors.rs` reproduced the exact shape its own module doc says it exists to eliminate**, three tests from `an_ordinal_exactly_at_the_table_length_is_refused`, which is the same defect caught one file over. Round 10's 113 mutations did not find it. ⇒ pinned and bitten. |
| **D-413** `[META]` | **Four more found in round 11's OWN fixes, and one of them was a DUPLICATED RULE.** The fence open/close logic was written out twice — in `_fence_state` and in `_claimable` — so mutating either left the other satisfying every case; **a duplicated rule is a rule with half a test**. ⇒ one `_advance`. The other three were new rules shipped without cases (the end marker, the empty block, the escape reach), which is the same one-round lag every round in this run has had. **One row was deleted as an EQUIVALENT mutant** rather than given a contrived case: `is_mark or …` differs from `…` only on the closing line, and a valid closer is a bare run of backticks with nothing on it to be live. |
| **D-414** `[META]` | **Smaller, each measured: the handoff's coverage claim was FALSE.** *"Every figure in this block is emitted by this script"* — and `dp-kernel --lib` **315** was ungoverned there, because the pattern keys on the RUN-STATE's phrasing and that block wrote it differently: `D-396`'s defect in the sibling document, unswept. The same paragraph forbidding aggregates asserted **three** of them, deleted rather than corrected. `_child_env` stripped only the FIRST PATH entry carrying cargo, so on a runner with two the control did not control. `measure()` caught `OSError` while a `UnicodeDecodeError` is a `ValueError` — a raw traceback from a hook that fires repo-wide, the **third** time this file has shipped that class. A fifth stale figure in `_index.md`'s table (*"ten measured seams"* against 18), spelled as a WORD so no pattern could reach it. And the VERIFY line of the previous commit said `citation-gate --all 4847` where it is **4845** — a stale figure in the verify block of the commit about stale figures. |

### 6v. ROUND 10 — the mechanism was blind in two of the three files it guards (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-394** `[E]` | **🔴 BLOCKING. `_escaped_live_range` — round 9's headline mechanism — tested SUBSTRING MEMBERSHIP where it needed a POSITION SPAN, and was therefore blind in two of the three documents it scans.** `if any(mo.group(0) in b for b in blocks)`: two of the three governed documents legitimately carry the live range **inside** their current-state block, which is the design — so the identical string anywhere else in those same files was exempted. Measured by replaying the original blanket replace file by file: the RUN-STATE reds with six findings, `_index.md` and the handoff stay **GREEN** — **including `docs/specs/2026-08-02-actor-hub/_index.md:31`, one of the six lines the mechanism had just been written to repair.** The RUN-STATE was guarded only because it happens to contain no live range at all, and the docstring's own sentence *"two places may hold the live value"* invites the state that kills it there too. ⇒ spans, from `_scope_span`, compared against `mo.start()`. **A guard defeated by the thing it is guarding is the adjacent-decision NV shape at its purest.** |
| **D-395** `[E]` | **And the fence handling was GUESSING, twice, because the window cannot know which side of a fence it starts on.** `_scope_text` cuts at a marker, so a fence can straddle the boundary. Toggling from `False` blanked every remaining line after an unmatched opener; **pairing markers 0-1, 2-3 was measurably WORSE** — in the closer/opener/closer shape it blanks real prose *and* exposes a genuine code block, where toggling did neither. There was nothing to guess: ⇒ `_fence_state` reads the DOCUMENT PREFIX, and inside the window a plain toggle is then exactly right, including for a fence that genuinely continues past the end. Also fixed with it: a `>`-prefixed BLOCKQUOTE fence was not recognised as a fence at all — in a file whose header the gate's own docstring says *is written entirely as a blockquote* — and a comment OPENING mid-line was not blanked, one line after the multi-line fix went in. |
| **D-396** `[E]` | **`_index.md`'s own scope block carried FOUR stale figures, and the gate said every governed figure agreed.** The block states the three contracts' lengths in a TABLE (`172` / `142` / `50`, *"364 lines total"*) while the files are **202 / 157 / 66, total 425** — stale since round 3, twenty lines from a prose sentence stating two of the same numbers correctly, in the file this gate's own docstring calls *"the file with the worst record for this exact defect"*. Every `CLAIMS` pattern keyed on PROSE. ⇒ four rows for the table form, a `contract_seams_lines` measurement and a derived `contract_total_lines`. **A scope is a claim — `D-371` — and so is the SHAPE a checker recognises inside it.** That is the same lesson one level in, and it took a cold-start round to see it because from inside, a shape nobody wrote a pattern for looks exactly like a shape with nothing to find. |
| **D-397** `[META]` | **Three aggregates DELETED from the slice board rather than advanced, on the eighth recurrence** — *"33 bite-tests"*, *"eight cold-start verifiers over seven rounds, 123 findings"*, *"three gates that did not exist this morning"*. The first two had already been corrected once, in the commit **titled** *"the slice board's own summary was stale"*, four lines above the sentence **"A figure outside a checker's scope is a figure nobody is reading."** They were INSIDE the scope. ⇒ the count of rounds is not derivable from any artifact, so it gets no pattern and no sentence; §6e..§6ac carry their own tallies. **`D-375`'s remedy, applied properly this time: stop claiming it.** |
| **D-398** `[E]` | **The seam range is NOT a moving pointer, and the reason the first version gave was wrong.** A review correctly said `D-385`'s *"not generalised on purpose"* argued only about `**283 passed**` and never mentioned `S-11`..`S-N`. Generalising was then MEASURED: exactly one finding across the three documents, and it is `B10`'s slice row recording the seams that slice produced — **cry-wolf on a correct statement.** The distinguishing property is the FIRST id: `D-1`..`D-N` runs from the record's first entry, so it names the WHOLE record and tracks its head by construction; `S-11`..`S-18` runs from an interior id and names a SEGMENT, a fixed fact that stays true when `S-19` lands. ⇒ the criterion is stated, the generalisation is not made. **A right instinct with a wrong reason is still a defect: the reason is what the next round reads.** |
| **D-399** `[META]` | **The CI wiring rested on an assertion about somebody else's machine.** *"This runner has no Rust toolchain"* was verified by nothing, `ubuntu-latest` ships rustup, and every other job in this repo that needs Rust installs it explicitly. **Both branches are defects**: with cargo present the *"8.3 s, 80× faster"* justification is void and the step re-runs `cargo test` ~60 times inside a 25-minute job; with cargo absent the gate's own NOTE to developers — *"CI checks it"* — is FALSE, which is verbatim the defect `D-374` fixed. ⇒ **`--no-cargo` strips it from the CHILD's environment**, so the harness controls the condition instead of asserting one. A claim about an environment you do not own is not a measurement. |
| **D-400** `[E]` | **Three rules were RED with cargo and GREEN without — so CI, the only automatic runner of the harness, covered them nowhere.** The FAILED-line parse, the crate list, and `dp-kernel`'s `--lib`. ⇒ `measure(cargo=…)` is injectable, and three cases assert **which arguments the measurements ask for** on a machine with no toolchain. **A rule whose coverage depends on the developer's machine is not covered** — and this one was invisible precisely because the developer's machine had cargo. |
| **D-401** `[META]` | **`gate-self-tests` was counting *"exited 0 when handed the flag"*, not *"a self-test ran"* — and a real gate in this tree was being counted green with no self-test at all.** `gate-teeth-gate.py` names `--self-test` in two comments, one of them saying a gate should have *"a `def self_test` and a `--self-test` CLI flag"*, has neither, ignored the flag and exited 0. The obvious tightening — require an `add_argument` registration — was measured and is **also wrong**: it drops `gatelib.py`, which has a real self-test dispatched through `sys.argv`. **A predicate keyed on one dispatch idiom is an enumerated list wearing a regex.** ⇒ the flag must appear with comments and docstrings **stripped**, which is the rule this repo already learned when a `TODO` comment and a module docstring certified three prose-only deferrals as covered. Also: discovery is now **recursive**, matching the hook's own `^scripts/.*\.py$` trigger — `scripts/perf/slo_assert.py` has a real self-test and staging it fired a driver that then did not look at it. |
| **D-402** `[E]` | **Nine actionable mutation survivors in `crates/actor-hub` — the crate nine rounds had called untouched.** 113 mutations, and the shape repeats in all nine: **the tests asserted the interesting half of a rule and left the boring half to be inferred.** A refused DERIVATION was recorded by nobody because every refusal assertion used `RowRef::Modifier` · `check_derivation`'s fold-layer check was dead weight while the identical modifier check reddened · `>= table.len()` weakened to `>` survived because the only case used ordinal 9 against a 4-entry table, five past a boundary that lives at 4 · `Accumulator.wanted` was asserted by SIGN only, and it is the field whose module doc records a review finding it understating the truth ~6 000× · `pre_emit` was equal to `value` in every existing case · a bound whose FLOOR bites reported nothing · negative division's rounding was unpinned because every divisor case was positive, where truncate and floor agree · and the documented refusal ORDER had no test. ⇒ `tests/fold_survivors.rs`, nine pins, **all nine bitten**. **"Never once in the fold" did not survive a wider mutation set, and the nine rounds that said so were measuring a narrower one.** |
| **D-403** `[META]` | **Six of my own expectations in those nine pins were GUESSES, and the crate corrected all six.** Ownership is deliberately **not** enforced on writes — any attached plugin may target any PRESENT quantity, so *"plugin 0 writes plugin 1's `qi`"* refuses nothing and my refusal fixtures refused nothing · `Percent(v)` adds `v` to a **per-mille** factor, so `Percent(100)` is +10 %, not +100 % · a derivation's `wanted` is the raw amount **before** the bound and **after** the factor. Every one was written from what the code plausibly did rather than from what it does. ⇒ recorded because it is the same reflex as the find-and-replace: **a plausible sentence about a system, typed rather than measured.** The pins are worth having; the first draft of them was not evidence. |
| **D-404** `[META]` | **The Rust mutation harness is COMMITTED, not scratchpad** — the gap `D-391` was written to close, re-opened by me the moment the next round needed one. `gate-bite-harness.py --rust` carries the nine. Unlike the gate half it mutates **in place** (cargo compiles the crate, so a copy beside the original is not the code under test), restores in a `finally`, and **refuses to start if any file it would touch is already dirty** — so an interrupted run is detectable rather than silently mixed into a diff. Its anchors are checked by the harness's own `--self-test`, which caught six drifted ones the moment round 10's rewrites landed. **And the harness then found six survivors among round 10's OWN fixes**, the sharpest being `_fence_state`: defined, given a parameter, and **never wired** — with a case that passed anyway, because the correct code and the unwired version report the SAME NUMBER of findings about DIFFERENT lines. ⇒ that case now asserts the finding's IDENTITY. One row was DELETED as an **equivalent mutant** rather than given a contrived case: widening the test-result regex cannot be observed, because the red-build guard three lines above it rejects every input that would distinguish the two patterns. Two more were the subject-cannot-vary shape — an expected crate list *derived from* the constant it was checking. |

### 6u. ROUND 9 — a find-and-replace fabricated a quotation, three rounds running (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-384** `[META]` | **🔴 BLOCKING. A BLANKET FIND-AND-REPLACE OF `` `D-1`..`D-N` `` REWROTE SIX HISTORICAL STATEMENTS TO THE LIVE HEAD — and the forensics in the first version of this row were wrong in both directions.** Re-measured per SITE across every commit that touched each file: `docs/specs/2026-08-02-actor-hub/_index.md:31` was damaged at **`2792717cd`** — one commit BEFORE round 7 — and carried through five more; the RUN-STATE's four sites at **`4f28c35e1`**, one commit AFTER it. **Six commits carried damage, from two different start points, and neither is round 7.** Writing *"it began at round 7"* was the same reflex as the replace itself: a plausible sentence about history, typed rather than measured, inside the row recording a defect that consisted of typing history rather than measuring it. The worst is `D-195`, which cites another document: the item RUN-STATE has said `D-1`..`D-109` since it was written (one commit, `d3bb441da` — round 11 noted that *"every commit it has ever had"* borrows force from a number it does not state), and the replace made this document attribute to it a sentence it does not contain. **Restoring the number then exposed a second layer** — the clause was set in quotation marks and was never verbatim; the source spreads it across two sentences. ⇒ marked as the paraphrase it is. **A paraphrase in quotation marks is a fabricated quotation that happens to be true**, and it is the thing that made the find-and-replace invisible: nobody checks a number inside a quotation, because a quotation is supposed to be frozen. `D-196`'s next clause is *"The 85 it has not seen"* — and 194 − 109 = 85; **the replace moved the number and left the arithmetic behind.** `D-358`'s worked example was rewritten too — the row whose entire subject is this gate crying wolf on correct historical statements. All six restored from git (`9ea333123`, `d3bb441da`, the item RUN-STATE itself), not retyped. **The header block asserts "every number re-derived at the moment of writing"; these were substituted, and the assertion did not notice because it is prose.** |
| **D-385** `[E]` | **And the MECHANISM is the inverse of `D-358`, which is why four rounds could not see it.** `D-358` bounded the figure check to the two current-state blocks so a decision row could keep the number that was true when written — correct, and it left the whole rest of the document unguarded, which is exactly where the defect lives. ⇒ **`_escaped_live_range`**: the live `` `D-1`..`D-<head>` `` may appear ONLY inside a current-state block; anywhere else, write the range that was TRUE. The range is a moving pointer at this document's own head, so a row stating it is stale the moment the next decision lands. **Deliberately not generalised to the other claim shapes**: `**283 passed**` in a historical row is a coincidence of value, not a pointer. |
| **D-386** `[E]` | **`_claimable` toggled fences instead of PAIRING them, so an unterminated fence blanked every remaining line of the block.** `_scope_text` cuts its window at a marker, so a fence straddling the boundary flips the toggle ON and never off — a check whose scope stops reaching its subject halfway down. Measured MISSED: a stale count below an unterminated fence, and a block beginning mid-fence. **This repo recorded the identical defect in `citation-gate` one round earlier** (*"fence parity blinded whole file tails on 8 live docs"*) and it was reintroduced in the sibling gate by the same author the same day. ⇒ markers are paired; an unpaired one is ordinary text. Also: an HTML comment spans lines and `startswith("<!--")` blanked only the opening one, and an inline `<!-- … -->` was not blanked at all. Five cases, all bite. |
| **D-387** `[E]` | **A stale document turned `--self-test` into an UNDIAGNOSABLE RED, and the hook change made it reachable.** The suite asserted `main()` returns 0 against the real documents with both streams redirected into a bin, so the failure printed *"returned non-zero on the real, agreeing documents"* — a message that asserts the thing that just turned out to be false, names *"rules"* rather than the document, and throws away the list saying which figure is stale. ⇒ the disagreement is PRINTED. **It earned itself on its first run**: adding `gate-self-tests.py` moved the hook's gate count 38 → 39, and the new message named both documents and the figure instead of reddening blankly. |
| **D-388** `[META]` | **`D-380`'s remedy covered 3 of 15 self-tests, by an ENUMERATED LIST, twelve lines above a comment forbidding enumerated lists** (*"Scope is DIRECTORIES … an enumerated list is default-uncovered, NV-3"*). Twelve gates stayed in exactly the state `D-380` calls intolerable and the next one written would have been default-uncovered. CI did not close it either: `gate-wiring-gate --run-all` invokes each gate **with no arguments**, so it never runs a self-test. ⇒ **`scripts/gate-self-tests.py`** — scope is a DIRECTORY and a PREDICATE (*every `scripts/*.py` whose source registers `--self-test`*), discovered on every run, with a floor so that a predicate which silently stops matching reds instead of reporting *"0 gates green"*. Measured: **16 gates, all green, 62 s** — 38 s of that the figures gate running `cargo test` twice inside its own self-test. |
| **D-389** `[E]` | **`_ambiguous_ok` was reverted by making it `return False` while keeping its three parameters and its original docstring** — leaving a function that read nothing, decided nothing, was deletable whole with the suite green, and whose docstring still argued the reverted case. **A dead branch is not a record of a decision.** The module docstring had the same residual: it still asserted *"an AMBIGUOUS citation is exempted no longer"* against code that exempts it. ⇒ function deleted, reasoning inlined at the decision, docstring corrected. **The numbers were re-measured rather than carried**: flipping the real rule and diffing the real scanner over all of `docs/` gives **909 findings, every one `C1-ambiguous`** — `app/main.py` (78), `app/db/migrate.py` (64), `mcp/server.py` (64). The comment said 873 / 63 / 61; the first re-measurement said 974, because **the measuring harness reimplemented the citation loop** and skipped the filters `scan_doc` applies first. Round 8's defect, in the tool sent to check it. |
| **D-390** `[E]` | **Three smaller ones, each with a case.** `measure()` unpacked a positional pair — `hub, substrate = lines` — **outside** the `try`, so a third contract would raise a raw `ValueError` inside a hook that fires on the repo-wide `SESSION_HANDOFF`: `D-364`'s cargo crash one line along ⇒ contracts are keyed, each measured under its own name, and the dict nothing compared is gone · the `\*\*(\d+)\*\* gate scripts` row was a strict SUBSTRING of its sibling, matching the same span of the same sentence — **the coverage arm cannot see an alias**, because the pattern is not dead, it is shadowed ⇒ deleted, and the remedy is not to write two rows over one subject · the coverage arm's measurability guard could only fire when a claim was BOTH absent everywhere AND unmeasurable here, where the gap is real and worth reporting ⇒ removed. |
| **D-391** `[META]` | **`D-376` certified twelve mutations as *"verified by a script that runs them"* and the script existed only in a session scratchpad** — `D-380`'s own thesis applied to the artefact certifying `D-376`. ⇒ **`scripts/gate-bite-harness.py`**, committed: a mutation per PRODUCTION RULE across three gates. It mutates a **copy placed beside the original** and deletes it in a `finally`, because an earlier hand-run of this idea was killed mid-run and left two `if False:` mutations in the working tree — caught by luck, not by mechanism. **It found two survivors on its first run**: the escape rule's WIRING into `_check` was untested (the cases called the rule directly — round 8's defect one call deep), and the discovery floor's only assertion lived inside `self_test`, where mutating a case cannot red anything. Both closed. |
| **D-392** `[META]` | **One figure was DELETED rather than corrected: the hook's *"~60 non-vacuity cases across the three game-tier gates"*.** Re-measured it is ~150, and the *"~60"* was copied from `L-13`'s *"~60 mutations aimed at the fold"* — **a different quantity entirely**. A hand-typed count with no measurement rule goes stale by construction, and this file now has a script that counts gates on every run. ⇒ the sentence states the mechanism and lets the driver print the number. **The cheapest fix for a stale figure is often to stop claiming it.** |
| **D-393** `[E]` | **And the harness found a THIRD defect in itself, which is the one worth keeping.** It mutates a copy written **beside** the original — `scripts/.bite-<name>.py`, same directory so `REPO` still resolves — and `gate-self-tests` **scans that directory**. Discovery picked the mutated copy up, ran it, and it discovered the copy in turn: a mutation of the driver reported **GREEN for a reason that had nothing to do with the rule under test**. ⇒ a dot-prefixed file is not a gate (editor swap files and scratch copies are not gates anywhere else either), with a case that plants exactly that shape. **The second half is the sharper lesson:** the case had asserted only that a starved run exits non-zero — and it was PASSING while the mutation was live, because the run had gone non-zero for an unrelated reason (a red gate elsewhere in the list). ⇒ it now asserts the REASON, from stderr. **A case satisfied by any failure proves nothing about its own subject** — the same shape as a `const` assertion whose subject cannot vary, one level out. |

### 6t. THE QUOTE RULE WAS A CONTENT HEURISTIC — replaced with a syntax rule (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-382** `[E]` | **🔴 `_claimable`'s first version matched the literal strings `said **` and `read it (*` — the two phrasings that happened to be in the corpus that afternoon.** That is a **content heuristic wearing a rule's clothes**: it blanks a REAL claim the day someone writes *"the spec said **283 passed**"*, and misses a quotation the day they write *"reported"*. ⇒ replaced with a **syntax** rule: an italicised quotation `*"…"*` is a historical record and is blanked; everything else is a live claim. Fences and HTML comments stay blanked as before, and offsets are preserved so line numbers remain valid. |
| **D-383** `[E]` | **And the corollary is stated rather than fudged: an UNQUOTED historical figure is treated as a LIVE CLAIM, deliberately.** Nothing distinguishes *"the handoff said 281"* from *"the count is 281"* except the quotation marks, so the gate **requires** them instead of guessing — which is exactly what the heuristics it replaced were doing. Writing a past figure as `*"…"*` is what this project already does everywhere. **Both directions have a case, and both bite:** deleting the blanking reds the quoted case, widening it to blank whole lines reds the stale-count case. |

### 6s. ROUND 8 — the self-test tested a COPY of the code (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-375** `[META]` | **🔴 BLOCKING, AND THE SEVENTH RECURRENCE: the aggregate finding count was stale in TWO GOVERNED SCOPES, and `D-371`'s own text says *"the header block was correct; the handoff was correct"* — false in the commit containing it.** ⇒ the root fix is not another number: **the aggregate is not mechanically derivable, so it is no longer asserted anywhere.** The per-round tallies in §6e..§6ac are the source; the blocks now point at them. **A number you cannot derive is a number you should not assert** — seven recurrences is the measurement that a discipline was never going to fix this, and removing the claim is the only thing that could. |
| **D-376** `[META]` | **🔴 BLOCKING, AND THE DEEPEST VACUITY OF THE ENTIRE RUN: `actor-hub-figures-gate --self-test` REIMPLEMENTED THE MATCHING LOOP INLINE, so it tested a COPY of the code.** A review deleted **twelve production rules one at a time — including `main()`'s `if problems:`, i.e. the gate ceasing to fail at all — and the self-test stayed GREEN for all twelve.** It never called `_check`, `measure`, `_cargo_passed`, `_max_id`, `_hook_gate_scripts` or `main`. **A self-test that does not call the thing it tests is the purest form of the defect this file exists to prevent**, and it shipped inside the file written to end that defect. ⇒ rewritten to drive `_check` and `main` with injected scopes, readers and measurements; **all twelve mutations now red**, verified by a script that runs them. |
| **D-377** `[E]` | **And one of its cases asserted the OPPOSITE of production behaviour.** *"an unmeasurable figure is a finding, not a pass"* — in `_check` an unmeasurable figure is a non-blocking **NOTE**. The inline copy counted it as a finding, so the case passed while describing behaviour the code does not have. **Two implementations of one rule will disagree, and the test's copy is the one nobody re-reads.** |
| **D-378** `[E]` | **🔴 CRY WOLF: a quoted historical figure, a fenced example and an HTML COMMENT each refused a commit** inside a governed block — and these blocks are *precisely* where this project writes *"the handoff said X at round seven"*, which the board block does today. ⇒ `_claimable()` blanks fences, HTML comments and quoted-figure prose before matching. Blockquote lines are deliberately NOT blanked: the RUN-STATE header is written entirely as one, and blanking it would empty the scope. |
| **D-379** `[E]` | **`_ambiguous_ok` is REVERTED, and the measurement is why.** It was added because *"a plan proposes to CREATE it"* is false by construction for a path matching several existing files — **sound reasoning, wrong class.** Measured: 873 findings whose top twenty cited paths are `app/main.py` (63), `mcp/server.py` (63), `app/db/migrate.py` (61) — **per-service shorthand in a 47-service monorepo**, elided because the enclosing paragraph already named the service. The finding's own text *"a reader cannot resolve it either"* is false for every one, and it **blocked a real commit on one line of ordinary prose**. ⇒ **a rule whose justification is sound and whose measured class contradicts it is still wrong; the measurement wins.** |
| **D-380** `[META]` | **A SELF-TEST NOBODY RUNS IS NOT A SELF-TEST — and none of the three ran automatically.** The ~60 non-vacuity cases across the game-tier gates are the artefact this whole round cites as proof that *"every rule bites"*: the hook ran each gate's normal mode, and `gate-wiring-gate --run-all` invokes gates **with no arguments**. **Deleting a case, or one silently starting to fail, reddened nothing.** ⇒ all three `--self-test`s are wired pre-commit, conditional on the gate itself being staged — which is exactly when a case can rot. |
| **D-381** `[E]` | **Three more, each measured:** the coverage arm was keyed on the measurement KEY, so a dead **pattern** with a live sibling of the same key was invisible — the `NV-3` shape the arm was written to close ⇒ keyed on the pattern · `contract_lines` was still measured, printed and **never compared**, so the shipped state was *"six measured, five compared"* in the commit whose message states the defect as *"six measured, two compared"* ⇒ both line counts are governed, and the coverage arm caught the blockquote-wrapped form immediately · and the round-7 scope row added for the board governs only `**N passed**`, **not** the findings/verifiers/rounds figures that were actually stale — which `D-375` resolves by deleting the claim rather than by adding a seventh pattern. |

### 6r. THE DEGRADATION MESSAGE PROMISED "CI CHECKS IT" — AND CI DID NOT (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-373** `[E]` | **🔴 The fix for `D-363` told the developer *"NOT CHECKED here (cargo is not on PATH); CI checks it"* — and CI did not.** `gate-wiring-gate`'s discovery predicate keys on a **filename shape** (`*-gate.py` / `*_gate.py` / `*-lint.py`), and the script was named `actor-hub-figures.py`, so `--run-all` never executed it. **The whole point of degrading gracefully was that a second layer would catch what the first skipped, and that second layer did not exist.** ⇒ renamed `actor-hub-figures-gate.py`; `gate-wiring-gate` now discovers it (79 → 80 gates) and CI runs it. **A promise about another mechanism is a claim like any other, and this one was made in the same commit that fixed a mechanism claimed rather than verified.** |
| **D-374** `[META]` | **Found by asking a question the verifier had not yet been given: *"is the thing I promised actually true?"*** The round-7 report flagged the CI invocation as a no-op (`F4`) and I fixed the *arguments*; nothing had asked whether the script was **discovered at all**. ⇒ the general form, and it is cheap: **after writing any sentence of the form *"X will catch it"*, run X and watch it catch it.** Three of this round's worst findings — `D-353`, `D-364`, `D-373` — are one sentence of that shape, unverified, each time.

### 6q. THE STOP-AUDIT FOUND THE BOARD'S OWN SUMMARY STALE — outside every checker's scope (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-371** `[META]` | **🔴 A STOP-AUDIT ASKED TO SEE THE SLICE BOARD ITSELF, AND THE BOARD'S SUMMARY BLOCK SAID *"81 findings over five rounds"* AT ROUND SEVEN — where the count is 123 over seven.** The header block two screens above it was correct; the handoff was correct; `_index.md` was correct. **The board was wrong because it was the one current-state block no checker covered** — `actor-hub-figures` governed the header, the handoff and `_index.md`, and stopped there. **This is `F7`'s shape one file along** (`_index.md`, uncovered, the file stale twice) and the sixth recurrence of the stale-figure defect in one round. ⇒ the board block is now a fourth `SCOPES` entry, and the bite reds: staling it to *281 passed* is refused. **A figure outside a checker's scope is a figure nobody is reading — including the author who wrote the checker.** |
| **D-372** `[META]` | **And the lesson generalises past this file: A CHECKER'S SCOPE IS ITSELF A CLAIM, AND IT NEEDS THE SAME TREATMENT AS THE CLAIMS IT CHECKS.** Four times this round a check was correct and its **reach** was not — the float gate's `src/`-only tree, the citation gate's enumerated `SOURCE_EXT`, the figures gate's two documents, and now its three blocks. Each was written as *"the scope is X"* and never asked *"what does X leave out, and who reads that?"* ⇒ `--self-test` now asserts **each governed document's block contains at least one governed claim**, so a scope that reaches nothing reds instead of passing. It is the only mechanical answer available: coverage cannot be inferred from a green run. |

### 6p. ROUND 7 — the remedy claimed coverage it did not have, and could not run without cargo (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-363** `[E]` | **🔴 BLOCKING, AND THE BLAST RADIUS WAS THE WHOLE REPO: the figures hook crashed with a raw `FileNotFoundError` when `cargo` was absent** — in a hook triggered by the **repo-wide** `SESSION_HANDOFF.md`, which this project's SESSION phase mandates updating on essentially every commit, on every branch, across 47 services. **A frontend or Python contributor could not commit a handoff update.** ⇒ an unmeasurable figure is now a **printed SKIP, not a block** (*"NOT CHECKED here (cargo is not on PATH); CI checks it"*), which is how every sibling gate in this repo already degrades. **The gate I wrote to stop a documentation defect could have stopped 46 other services from committing.** |
| **D-364** `[META]` | **🔴 BLOCKING: the flagship remedy CLAIMED coverage it did not have — six figures measured, TWO compared, while both documents said *"every figure in this block is emitted by this script, which `--check`s them"*.** `dp-kernel --lib`, the gate-script count, the contract line counts and the seam range were computed, printed and **thrown away**; three headline figures were never measured at all. **That is `D-353`'s defect one level up — a mechanism CLAIMED rather than VERIFIED — inside the mechanism written to end it.** ⇒ every claim shape is now in one `CLAIMS` table, and **a shape with no subject anywhere is itself a finding**, so a vacuous arm reds instead of passing. |
| **D-365** `[E]` | **🔴 And the check passed SILENTLY when its anchor moved.** `text.find(marker)` returning `-1` set the block to `""` and reported *"the docs agree with the artifacts"* against **zero subjects** — and the RUN-STATE arm's fixed 4 000-character window contained **no matching claim at all**, so half the check had never had a subject. ⇒ the scope is **marker-bounded**, a missing marker is a finding, and the `--self-test` now asserts **each document's block contains at least one governed claim**. **A vacuous arm and a passing arm look identical from outside; only a coverage assertion tells them apart.** |
| **D-366** `[E]` | **`_index.md` was uncovered — the one file with the worst record for this exact defect.** It is stale twice in this round's own log (`D-347`, `D-350`), it was hand-advanced by the commit adding the gate, and neither the script's document list nor the hook's trigger named it. **Bite: setting it to `D-1..D-300` left the check green.** ⇒ covered in both, and the bite now reds. |
| **D-367** `[E]` | **A failing `cargo test` summed to zero passes, after which the script told the developer *"do not advance the number"* — i.e. to rewrite the doc to match a broken build.** ⇒ a red or absent test run raises `Unmeasurable`; the count is never inferred from a build that did not pass. |
| **D-368** `[META]` | **🔴 AND THE FIX FOR ROUND 6's OVER-CUT IMMEDIATELY OVER-CORRECTED — caught by measuring BEFORE committing, not after.** Restoring ambiguous citations without a line number took the corpus from **4 848 to 11 377**, because a BARE name in prose (`lib.rs`, `mod.rs`) matches dozens of files by construction: **6 532 new prose-mention wolves.** ⇒ the restoration is **ANCHORED-only**, which is where the verifier's measured 873 live, and the create-a-file story is false by construction only there. **Final: 5 751**, with the 873 recovered. |
| **D-369** `[E]` | **The prose-mention rule's residual is now NAMED instead of discovered.** Of 3 576 suppressed findings the review measured **308 with explicit modify/delete/existing language** that are genuinely dead — including a contract described as *"frozen"* that does not exist. **That is a MISS, and it is the deliberate half of the trade**: the cry-wolf direction blocks work, the miss direction does not. Written into the docstring's *"what still walks past it"* section rather than left for round 8. |
| **D-370** `[E]` | **Four more, each with a bite:** the CI invocation `[sys.executable, rel]` passes **no arguments**, so `--run-all` ran the script without `--check` — a 40-second unconditional green that proved nothing ⇒ **checking is now the default** and `--print` is the opt-out · `LINK_SPAN_RE` and `LINK_RE` still disagreed on a `>` in the href, so the same citation got opposite verdicts depending on a character in the *link* it sat in ⇒ one target class · an **empty link label** made `edge_types[](source/target)` a dead link ⇒ a label must be non-empty · and **three self-test cases could not fail** — the titled-link form had none, the ambiguous arm's `is_link_text` guard had none, and the case written to replace a vacuous case matched **neither** regex with or without the rule it named. |

### 6o. SELF-CAUGHT BETWEEN ROUNDS — a no-op loop and a third dead guard (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-360** `[E]` | **🔴 THE CODE-SPAN EXCLUSION I ADDED IN ROUND 6 WAS A NO-OP LOOP, FOUND BY READING MY OWN DIFF WHILE THE NEXT VERIFIER RAN.** It iterated `LINK_RE`, skipped matches inside a backtick span, **and then did nothing at all** — the real link check is a *later* loop over the same regex. So a markdown link quoted in prose still reddened, and the exclusion existed only as text. ⇒ moved to the arm that actually checks, with two cases: a quoted link is silent, the same link unquoted still reds. **A rule written near the check is not a rule applied at the check.** |
| **D-361** `[E]` | **And copying it to the SECOND link arm made it a third dead guard.** `LINK_DEF_RE` is anchored `^\s*\[`, so its match can only begin at column 0 or after whitespace — **never inside a backtick span**. The bite-test caught it immediately: deleting the guard left the whole self-test green, exactly like `(?!\s*\|)` (`D-352`) and the table-row case (`D-357`). ⇒ removed, with the reason written where the guard was. **Three occurrences of one shape in one round is not carelessness; it is what happens when a guard is added because it sounds right instead of because a bite proved it necessary.** |
| **D-362** `[E]` | **The exclusion is deliberately NOT applied to CITATIONS, and that asymmetry is load-bearing.** This repo writes almost every citation in backticks — ``​`resolve.rs:84`​`` — so excluding code spans from the citation scan would blind the gate to nearly its whole subject. A link inside backticks is a *quotation of markdown*; a citation inside backticks is *how a citation is written here*. **The same delimiter means two different things, and a rule that treats them alike is wrong for one of them.** |

### 6n. ROUND 6 — the cut was aimed at the wrong class, and the numbers had no mechanism (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-354** `[E]` | **🔴 THE ROUND-5 FENCE CUT WAS AIMED AT THE WRONG CLASS, AND IT REMOVED REAL FINDINGS.** It was justified by *"539 findings, every one of them a false positive"*. **Re-measured: 538, of which 155 carry a `:N` line number** — genuine `file:line` citations inside blocks titled *"Current architecture (evidence)"*, one of them a `C2-past-eof` on a 59-line file **inside this gate's own blocking corpus**: the exact defect class the gate exists for. It also blinded **97 057 of 522 781 doc lines (18.6 %)** and said so nowhere. ⇒ **the fence exclusion is REVERTED.** A scope cut justified by a number nobody re-measured is the same defect as a claim nobody re-measured. |
| **D-355** `[E]` | **🔴 AND THE CLASS THAT ACTUALLY DOMINATES WAS NEVER THE ONE BEING PATCHED: 3 382 of 8 095 findings had NO LINE NUMBER.** The dominant shape is this repo's planning idiom — a table row naming a file the plan proposes to **create** — and it blocked a real commit. **The docstring already carved out BARE filenames with no line number as prose mentions; nothing justified the asymmetry with anchored ones, and nothing stated it.** ⇒ **a citation with no line number is a prose mention, anchored or not.** With a line number the meaning changes: you are pointing at content that must already exist, which is what the gate is for. **Baseline 8 095 → 4 848, and the 155 real in-fence citations came back** (`C2-past-eof` 59 → 63). |
| **D-356** `[E]` | **🔴 The link-text skip silently disabled the LINE-EXISTS rule.** Its justification — *"the href beside it IS the resolution"* — answers **which file** and says nothing about **which line**, but the `continue` fired before the `C2` branch, hiding three live broken ranges. ⇒ the skip is now a flag applied to the resolution arm only; `C2` still runs. **A justification that is true of one rule does not license skipping the next one.** |
| **D-357** `[META]` | **🔴 `D-353` DECLARED THE STALE-NUMBER REMEDY *"every number is now derived by a script that reads the artifacts"* — AND NO SUCH SCRIPT EXISTED.** Four of the five figures that row presents as measured were wrong (`9 039` for 9 040 · `539` for 538 · `398` for 402 · `38` gate scripts for 37). **That is the fourth recurrence, in the row claiming it was mechanised** — the repo's own *"intent is not a mechanism"*, written inside a sentence claiming a mechanism. ⇒ **`scripts/actor-hub-figures-gate.py` now exists**: it runs `cargo test`, reads the max `D-` id, counts the contract lines and the hook's gate scripts, and `--check` **reds when a current-state block disagrees**. Wired pre-commit, conditional on either document being staged. |
| **D-359** `[META]` | **🔴 AND ON ITS FIRST COMMIT THE FIGURES GATE CAUGHT THE FIFTH RECURRENCE — MINE, IN THE COMMIT ADDING THE GATE.** The handoff said `D-1..D-353`; the run-state this commit was writing had reached `D-358`. **I typed a number that had been true one commit earlier**, which is the identical shape as `D-350`, `D-351` and `D-353`, committed while writing the row about it. **The gate reddened, the commit was refused, and the number was read instead of typed.** That is the difference between a rule and a mechanism, demonstrated on the mechanism's first run. |
| **D-358** `[META]` | **And the figures checker reproduced the cry-wolf failure on its first run — eight findings, every one a correct HISTORICAL statement** (slice `S4`'s *"202 passed"* from the design round; *"`D-1`..`D-109`"* from the item round's snapshot). **A decision record is supposed to contain the numbers that were true when it was written.** ⇒ scoped to the two CURRENT-STATE blocks. **Four rounds of learning this lesson, and the tool written to fix a different defect committed it immediately** — which is the argument for the cold-start round, not against the tool. |

**Five more MAJOR and four MINOR, each fixed:** `Tree._dirs` shipped **entirely untested** while changing 258 verdicts (deleting it left the self-test green) — two cases added · the case written to fix a vacuous case was **itself vacuous**, double-guarded by the path-shape filter — replaced with one only the `^` anchor can satisfy · `_is_placeholder` reached **one link arm of two**, re-introducing the two-arms-disagree defect `_check_link` exists to kill · fence parity blinded whole file tails on 8 live docs — moot with the exclusion reverted · `LINK_SPAN_RE` did not match the titled link form its own sibling handles · an **inline code span** is prose about markdown and is now excluded, the same class one delimiter along · the float gate's `scanning = False` on fence close had no case · and the `C4` checkout-dependence is **narrowed, not closed**, now stated as such where the claim lives rather than recorded as done.

### 6m. ROUND 5 — the wolf was RELABELLED, not removed, and the fix was to cut the rule's reach (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-351** `[E]` | **🔴 BLOCKING: round 4 did not remove ten of the twenty-six `+`-idiom false positives — it MOVED THEM from `C2-bad-range` to `C3-unanchored`.** Allowing `+` as non-junk let the citation fall through to the *next* rule, which reported it unresolvable. **Every one of the ten is the link TEXT of a markdown link whose href is the fully anchored path on the same line**, so `C3`'s own justification — *"a reader cannot resolve it either"* — is factually false for all ten. The commit's VERIFY said *"the delta is the false positives removed"*; for these it was a relabel, and the gate still blocked. **Measured as a real commit blocker on a line copied verbatim out of existing repo prose.** |
| **D-352** `[E]` | **🔴 AND THE FIX WAS NOT ANOTHER NARROWING — IT WAS CUTTING TWO WHOLE CLASSES OUT OF THE RULE'S REACH.** A citation **inside a fenced code block** is content, not a citation: a TypeScript snippet, a shell transcript, a JSON sample. A citation that **is the text of a markdown link** is resolvable by definition, because the href beside it *is* the resolution. **Measured: 539 + 398 findings, and the `--all` baseline fell 9 039 → 8 095 with the strict corpus still green and all ten blockers gone.** ⇒ **when a rule's justification is false for a whole class of its findings, narrow the SCOPE, do not add another exception** — four rounds of exceptions had left the wolf intact. |
| **D-353** `[META]` | **🔴 AND THE STALE NUMBER RECURRED A THIRD TIME, IN THE COMMIT RECORDING ITS SECOND RECURRENCE.** `D-343` named it; `D-350` recorded it happening again; this commit updated the handoff **279 → 281** while its own VERIFY line said **283** — advancing to the *previous* commit's value. The same document also carried **three different bite counts** (31, 22, 33) one commit after one titled *"count the bites exactly"*, and a *"load-bearing"* link count that said three where there are four. ⇒ **every number in the handoff and the header is now derived by a script that reads the artifacts** (`cargo test`, the bite scripts, the max `D-` id, `wc -l` on the contracts, the hook's gate list). **Three recurrences is not carelessness; it is the absence of a mechanism, and the mechanism is: never type a number you did not just measure.** |

**Nine more MAJOR, each fixed:**

| finding | what it was |
|---|---|
| the two link arms disagreed **depending on whether `npm install` had run** | `C1`/`C3` resolve against `git ls-files`; `C4` called `Path.exists()`, which sees gitignored build output. One link, opposite verdicts, and an `--all` baseline that was not reproducible between machines — CI on a fresh clone would red what a developer sees green. ⇒ both arms now resolve against the same tree, with directories derived from path prefixes |
| a false-positive guard that guarded nothing | `LINK_DEF_RE`'s `(?!\s*\|)` table lookahead can never change a verdict, because `^\s*\[` already refuses a line starting with `\|`. **Its self-test case went green with the lookahead deleted** — a new vacuity, shipped in the round about vacuity |
| the repo-escape rule could be deleted with the suite green | every case counted findings, and the fallback *does-not-resolve* arm produces the same count. ⇒ four cases now assert the **verdict string**, not the count |
| the float gate reddened on comments and strings **inside a doc example** | fenced lines were scanned RAW, so `// was 1.5 before` and `assert_eq!(s, "1.5")` were findings — in a gate whose docstring says the stripper exists because *"a gate that reds on its own documentation gets switched off"* |
| `rust,ignore` was treated as compiled | only the FIRST info token was consulted. ⇒ **every** token must be compile-compatible |
| the unterminated-fence residual was a **MISS**, documented as cry-wolf-only | a dangling fence made the NEXT block's opening fence read as a closing one, so a genuine `f64` doc-test went invisible. ⇒ fence state resets at every doc-block boundary |
| `file://`, `ftp://` and `tel:` were dead repo files | four live `file:///` links in `docs/`. ⇒ an explicit scheme set, used by the link rules and the URL blanker alike |
| two template placeholder links | widening the inline target class made a link whose target is the literal shape `L<N>_*.md` red in two template files that are *about* a naming shape |
| two more self-test cases were double-guarded | each stayed green when the rule it named was deleted, because a second narrowing also caught it. ⇒ each now has a case only its own rule can satisfy |

### 6l. ROUND 4 — REFUTED, and the fix pass had made two gates CRY WOLF (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-348** `[META]` | **🔴 11 MAJOR, 4 MINOR — and the dominant failure was NEW: my round-3 repairs turned both gates into commit blockers on CORRECT content.** Measured across `docs/`: **9 016 → 9 102 findings, ~73 of the 96 new ones false positives.** `SOURCE_EXT` gained a bare `c` and `h`, which truncated `.cypher` → `.c`, `.conf` → `.c`, `.cpp` → `.c` (**36** findings on real files) · the trailing-junk rule refused `+`, which is this repo's idiom for *"line 125 and following"* (**26**) · `LINK_DEF_RE` accepted any `[label]: token`, so a budget table row, a TypeScript index signature **inside a code fence**, a numeric range and every footnote became dead links (**11**). **A gate that cries wolf gets switched off within a day — a sentence that appears in `citation-gate.py` three times, written by the author who then shipped this.** Fixed by narrowing each rule *and* by giving each false positive its own self-test case, so the cry-wolf direction is now guarded as hard as the miss direction. |
| **D-349** `[E]` | **🔴 The doc-fence scanner added to close a hole had a hole and a wolf, and one MASKED the other.** `body = stripped.lstrip('/')` left the `!` of a `//!`, so **every module-level doc-test was invisible** — in a crate where `lib.rs`, `fold.rs`, `rows.rs`, `report.rs` and `ordinal.rs` all lead with `//!`, and where the function's own condition names `//!`. **Intent present, mechanism absent.** And any ```` ``` ```` toggled scanning, so a ```` ```text ```` block — *how this repo writes its contract diagrams* — had its prose read as code. **Fixing either alone exposed the other**, which is `NV-4` in both directions at once. The fence handler now needs **two** states (`in_fence` and `scanning`): the first single-flag fix made a non-compiled block's CLOSING fence look like a compiled block's OPENING one, and reported *"§3.4"*, *"1."*, *"2."* and *"−60 %"* as floats across five files. |
| **D-350** `[E]` | **🔴 And the round-3 fix pass reproduced `D-343` — the stale-number defect — inside the commit that RECORDS `D-343`.** `SESSION_HANDOFF` was updated 270 → **279** while the same commit's own VERIFY line said **281**; `_index.md` and the handoff were both advanced to `D-1..D-339` while **§6k of that same commit adds `D-340`..`D-347`**. The `D-347` row even lists *"a stale `D-1..D-295` in `_index.md`"* among the things it fixed. ⇒ **advancing a number is not re-deriving it**, and the only reliable version of this fix is to read the value out of the artifact at the moment of writing. |

### 6k. ROUND 3 — REFUTED AGAIN, and the defect was INSIDE round 2's own fix (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-340** `[META]` | **🔴 THE STOPPING RULE EARNED ITS KEEP ON THE VERY ROUND IT WAS INVOKED FOR.** Round 3 was the *confirm* round — the second of the two consecutive clean rounds `D-212` requires. It came back **REFUTED: 5 MAJOR, 12 MINOR**, and its sharpest finding is a **live behavioural defect in the fix `D-326` had just shipped.** One clean round would have sealed a broken `CAPPED` record. **A round that finds nothing is a stopping rule, not a proof — and a round that finds something is the reason the rule is two.** |
| **D-341** `[E]` | **🔴 `F1` — the `CAPPED` record introduced to stop three clamps biting silently REPORTED A VALUE THE FOLD NEVER EMITTED.** `emitted: i32::MAX` was hardcoded on the accumulator record. A downward overflow emits `i32::MIN`, so one fold produced **two `CAPPED` events for one quantity whose `emitted` fields contradicted each other** — and the lying one was the record substrate §7 exists to make non-silent. Measured: `stored = i32::MIN`, two `Flat(i32::MIN)`, one `Percent(i32::MAX)` ⇒ `value = i32::MIN`, accumulator record claims `i32::MAX`. **Both of its fields were unguarded**: zeroing `wanted` or hardcoding `emitted` left the suite green, because the only test asserted `.any(|c| c.site == Accumulator)` — *that a record exists*, never that it says anything true — and exercised the positive direction only. ⇒ the emitted value is computed once and both records carry it; two new tests assert every cap record for one quantity **agrees**, in both signs. |
| **D-342** `[E]` | **🔴 `F2` — THE ROUND PRODUCED ITS FOURTH PHANTOM CITATION *BY SHIPPING THE GATE BUILT FOR THE FIRST THREE*, and the gate reported green on it.** `actor-hub.md` cited `dp-kernel/src/entity_status.rs:28` for `pub enum GoneState {` — true at the sealing commit. **The same commit moved the enum**, and line 28 of that file is now a doc comment: *"prose that happens to live in a source file"*, the exact class `citation-gate.py` documents it **cannot** catch. ⇒ re-pointed at `entity-existence/src/lib.rs:33`, with the move and the incident written into §3.3. **A code move invalidates every citation aimed at the lines it left behind, and no line-existence check can see it.** |
| **D-343** `[E]` | **🔴 `F3` — the SESSION_HANDOFF block written in this commit was a PRE-FIX SNAPSHOT: four numbers superseded by the same commit that contained it** (`D-310`→`D-339`, 270→279 tests, 11→22 bites, `S-11..S-15`→`S-11..S-18`), plus a gate list omitting `citation-gate` two rows below where it names it. **The RUN-STATE's own `B10` row carried the same stale seam range while marked `[x]`.** CLAUDE.md makes this the first file the next session reads. ⇒ corrected. **The lesson is mechanical: a summary written mid-run is stale by the end of the run, and the last act of a run is to re-derive it, not to trust it.** |
| **D-344** `[E]` | **🔴 `F4` + `F5` — the §4 amendment and the ceiling correction each reached ONE of their two homes.** `rows.rs`, whose header calls itself *"the whole plugin contract"*, still carried the **pre-amendment** row shapes — a `value` field that does not exist and `source` typed as a `QuantityOrdinal` — i.e. it did precisely what the amendment's stated reason said it existed to prevent. And `engine-substrate.md` still read *"`floor(q)` · `ceiling(q)`: **UNWRITTEN**"* while `fold.rs` three files away carried the correction saying that claim is false. ⇒ both swept. **`D-276` measured this exact shape a round ago — *"a finding names a LOCATION; a correction must sweep a CONCEPT"* — and it recurred inside the commit that recorded it.** |
| **D-345** `[E]` | **Both new gates had another hole each, and both were the SAME hole one level along.** The float gate blanks comments before scanning — so **a float inside a rustdoc example was invisible**, although a doc-test *compiles and runs under `cargo test`* and is the nearest example the next author copies, in a gate whose docstring says test code is deliberately in scope. ⇒ fenced blocks in doc comments are extracted and scanned; prose in a doc comment is not. The citation gate's `SOURCE_EXT` had **`jsx` dead behind `js`** — the identical ordered-alternation defect `D-333` fixed for `ts|tsx` and `js|json` and did not sweep — plus trailing junk truncating a line number (`:99+1` read as 99) and **reference-style links wholly uncovered**. ⇒ all four fixed, each with a self-test case. |
| **D-346** `[META]` | **And the residuals are now WRITTEN INTO the gates rather than discovered by the next reviewer.** The float gate names three things a token scan cannot reach — `include!`, `#[path]`, a float behind a type alias in another crate — and that `GUARDED_TREES` is itself an enumerated list, so a *new* crate carrying hashed bytes is uncovered until someone adds it: **the same NV-3 shape the gate enforces for files, and it cannot enforce for itself.** The citation gate names its own enumerated `SOURCE_EXT`, HTML anchors and nested link brackets. **A green run is not an audit, and a gate that says so is worth more than one that does not.** |
| **D-347** `[E]` | **Nine MINOR findings, all fixed, three of them the same failure in three places:** a `saturating_mul` in `rows.rs` that is **structurally impossible** to fire (both operands `i32` in an `i64`) while its two *weaker* siblings got a whole paragraph disclosing exactly that · a dead `sum != 0` conjunct inside the expression written to fix a vacuity finding · the float gate's docstring stating **two different scopes**, the stale one *below* the correction. Plus: `actor.rs` used the named-const-plus-`const _ = NAME` shape that `ordinal.rs` documents as a dead-code suppressor wearing a mechanism's clothes · `fold` and `report` were **private modules**, so the fold's whole contract — formula, three-pass limit, totality, the `S-18` correction — never reached `cargo doc`, in a crate whose SDK argument is *"the public surface IS the plugin API"* · a duplicated inert `[dev-dependencies]` · `S-18` filed before `S-17` · a stale `D-1..D-295` in `_index.md`. |

### 6i. THE B2–B9 VERIFIER — 20 findings, VERDICT REFUTED, and the shape repeats (2026-08-02)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-318** `[META]` | **🔴 REFUTED — and the arithmetic survived everything. Every one of the twenty findings is in a CHECK or a CLAIM, not in the fold.** The agent mutated the `max(0,…)` floor, both channels of the accumulator, the `CONTRIBUTE`-never-`SET` rule, five refusal paths, three registry build refusals, the `Capped` record and the op discriminator — **all red**. It then found **three assertions that cannot fail**, a documented semantic with no test that could see it, two holes in a gate written this same run, and a doc claim contradicted by the hub's own direct dependency. **The pattern is now measured four times in this round: what fails is never the thing that was measured, it is the sentence written around it.** |
| **D-319** `[E]` | **🔴 A `const` assertion that named neither the array it guarded nor the type it described — and narrowing the array left it GREEN, all tests passing, and a documented-total fold panicking out of bounds.** `LAYER_INDEX_COVERS_THE_ORDINAL` compared `u8::MAX + 1 == 256`, true on every target forever. Cutting `declared_layers` to `[bool; 128]` compiled, kept the assertion green, passed 59 tests — and made `FoldLayer(200)` an index-out-of-bounds panic. ⇒ **the assertion is DELETED and the width DERIVED** (`FoldLayer::ORDINAL_SPACE`), so there is nothing left to keep in step; plus `every_fold_layer_ordinal_is_declarable_and_checkable`, which exercises `0/127/128/255` and reds on the narrowing. **Deriving beats asserting: it removes the second fact rather than watching it.** |
| **D-320** `[E]` | **A second tautology, and its cause is an ADJACENT CORRECT DECISION — `NV-4` in the flesh.** `assert!(MAX_PLUGINS <= MAX_DECLARED_QUANTITIES)` is `X <= X`, because `ordinal.rs` defines `MAX_PLUGINS = MAX_DECLARED_QUANTITIES` — the *right* decision (`D-255`: the two ceilings move together) is exactly what makes the checker permanently conforming. **Deleted.** Three files away sits `MAX_PLUGINS == u32::BITS`, which genuinely fails and was measured failing at 16 and 64. **Two assertions, one file apart, and only one of them is a check.** |
| **D-321** `[E]` | **🔴 The fold's CENTRAL documented claim had no test that could distinguish it.** The module doc says pass 2 reads its source quantity's **pass-1** value; replacing `pass1[…]` with `stored[…]` left all 59 tests green, because **every derivation fixture gave the source quantity no modifier rows** — the input that separates the two readings was structurally absent. Fixed with `a_derivation_reads_the_pass_one_value_not_the_stored_one`: speed 10 000 `+2 000` flat, derived `×1/3` ⇒ **4 001 correct, 3 334 under the mutation.** |
| **D-322** `[E]` | **🔴 The float gate written THIS RUN was emptied of its stated scope BY THIS RUN — `NV-4` again, and both halves were mine.** Its docstring says *"TEST CODE IS IN SCOPE, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE"*; it guarded `<crate>/src`; and then the file-ceiling split moved **~800 lines of tests out of `src/` into `tests/`**. A probe file with `let x: f64 = 1.5;` in `crates/actor-hub/tests/` passed with **exit 0**. Both decisions individually correct, together a hole. ⇒ scope is now the **whole crate**. |
| **D-323** `[E]` | **🔴 And every scientific-notation float walked through it.** `1e5` has **no decimal point at all**, and the lookahead on the dotted arm rejected `4.0e-9` outright; `1_000.` slipped past because `\b` does not match between `_` and `.`. Measured passing with exit 0: `1e5`, `2e-3`, `1E10`, `4.0e-9`, `5e-1`, `1_0.5`, `1_000.` — all valid `f64` literals. Also `search` reported **one** finding per line, so two floats on a line read as one. ⇒ three explicit alternatives, `finditer`, and **eleven new self-test cases**. **The gate's own self-test had already caught `2f64` before shipping; the round-2 review caught seven more it never thought to ask about — which is the argument for the cold-start agent, not against the self-test.** |
| **D-324** `[E]` | **🔴 And a pragma written inside a STRING LITERAL silenced it.** `let msg = "hashed-substrate-float-gate: ok - not really";` above an `f64` field ⇒ zero findings. **The tell: the same file stripped strings for the SCAN and not for the PRAGMA** — sibling arms of one file disagreeing about what counts as code. ⇒ the pragma is now looked up in a strings-kept view and refused when it survives there, i.e. it counts only inside a comment. |
| **D-325** `[E]` | **🔴 `fold.rs` claimed the ceiling model is UNWRITTEN. IT IS NOT, and the counter-evidence is in the hub's own direct dependency.** `ruleset-core` ships `ResourceDecl { quantity, min, base, ceiling: CeilingBinding, … }`, held by `ResourceTable` over **the same ordinal space**, carried by every `Ruleset`, and already validated by `ResourceError::BadBounds`. What is true is narrower: **the hub does not CONSULT it.** ⇒ the doc now says that, and the consequence is registered rather than hidden as **`S-18`** — a reality can declare `ceiling: Fixed(1000)` and this fold resolves 10 500 with no `Capped` record, because reconciling `QuantityDecl.initial` against `ResourceDecl.base` decides **what a pool is**, which is the resource feature's question. **`D-282`'s lesson, at the code tier: a claim about shipped code is a measurement, and "UNWRITTEN" is the easiest kind to assert without making one.** |
| **D-326** `[E]` | **Three clamps bit silently against §7, and one `Capped` record understated its own subject by ~6 000×.** The emit's `wanted` was read *after* the accumulator had already saturated, so the number it reported was the saturation bound, not what the author asked for. ⇒ `Capped` gains a **`site`** (`Accumulator` · `Emit` · `DerivedAmount` · `DerivedBound`), the accumulator saturation is its own event, and a derivation's two clamps each report. **`DerivedBound` is the author's declaration WORKING and is reported anyway** — *"a bound ceiling is a fact in the log rather than a number nobody can explain."* |
| **D-327** `[E]` | **`detach` was the one silent verb on a type whose sibling's doc says *"nothing silent"*.** `attach` refuses `NotDeclared` and `AlreadyAttached` through a `Result`; `detach` accepted anything, including a plugin never attached and an ordinal this reality never declared. ⇒ `Result<(), DetachError>`. |
| **D-328** `[E]` | **Two tests deleted or corrected rather than defended.** `the_public_surface_names_no_feature_vocabulary` bound five known-good items and claimed to prove the hub names no feature vocabulary — **adding `pub struct Hp;` left it green**, because its scope was a hand-written list (`NV-3`, default-uncovered). **Deleted, with the reason in its place**, since the honest version is a reading and not a predicate. `all_covers_every_channel`'s first line asserted `ALL.len() == COUNT` where `ALL: [_; COUNT]` — the length *is* the constant; the arm is gone and the dedup that actually bites is now annotated as the one doing the work. |
| **D-329** `[E]` | **Six more, each with the mutation that had been green:** the pass-1 intermediate clamp (a wrapping truncation handed a derivation a negative source) · `saturating_mul` (needed a product landing in `[2^63, 2^64)` to be distinguishable — merely exceeding `i64::MAX` can wrap back into the positive half and *look* like saturation) · both components of the intra-layer sort key (one plugin cannot show a key that sorts by plugin; a shared index space plus a stable sort hid the derivation offset) · the explanations' ordering · the emit division's **truncation toward zero**, unspecified in both contracts and now pinned, because a future `div_euclid` "fix" would move every negative quantity by one with nothing red. |
| **D-330** `[E]` | **And two `saturating_add`s were NOT given a test, deliberately.** Both addends are `i32` widened into an `i64`, so overflowing one needs **~2^32 rows for a single quantity** — unreachable, and the review confirmed it: `wrapping_add` leaves the suite green and **no input can redden it**. ⇒ they are documented as **defensive uniformity, not guards**, with the reachable sibling (`saturating_mul`, which overflows at three rows each way) named beside them. **Writing a test that cannot fail in order to close a review finding would have been the finding.** |
| **D-331** `[E]` | **The contract was AMENDED, not bent around.** The sealed §4 read `ModifierRow { …, op, value, … }` and `DerivationRow { target, source, op, factor_milli, bound }` — while the shipped rows collapse `op`+`value` into the value-carrying `ModifierOp` (`D-253`), add `divisor` (`U-6`) and `fold_layer`, and use `source` for the **submitting plugin** on both rows with the derivation's quantity renamed `source_quantity`. The sealed text used one name for two concepts one line apart. **A reader following *"the only specification"* would have read `source` as a quantity and been handed a plugin**, so the contract moved and carries the reason inline. |

And `docs/../crates/x.rs` matched nothing in `C1` while `C4` normalised the identical syntax through `Path.resolve()`. ⇒ one `normalise()` — separators and `..` — used by both. |

It also missed the CommonMark title form (`[x](dead.md 'title')` — invisible) and reddened the angle-bracket form on files that exist. |
| **D-336** `[E]` | **The escape hatch needed no reason and could be fired by prose.** `_pragma_covers` tested only `PRAGMA in line`, so `<!-- citation-gate: ok -->` with no reason silenced three lines while the failure message promised `— <reason>` — and **any document quoting the pragma silenced its own next three lines**, which the first document explaining this gate would have done. ⇒ the pragma must be an HTML comment **and** carry a reason. |

The count and exit code were right. **A non-vacuity self-test emitting a green line for a red rule is the exact costume the standard is named after.** |
